direction or flow direction. For a general Anosov vector field, the
maps
m\rightarrow E_{u}\left(m\right)
,
m\rightarrow E_{s}\left(m\right)

and
m\rightarrow E_{u}\left(m\right)\oplus E_{s}\left(m\right)

are only Hölder continuous with (a priori different) Hölder exponents
respectively
[
31
]
:

\beta_{u},\beta_{s},\beta_{0}\in]0,1],\quad\beta:=\mathrm{min}\left(\beta_{u},\beta_{s}\right).

(4.3)

Figure
4.1:
Anosov flow
\phi^{t}
(in solid line) generated
by a vector field
X
on a compact manifold
M
.

Let
\mathcal{A}\in C^{0}\left(M;T^{*}M\right)
be the continuous
one form on
M
called
Anosov one form
defined for every

m\in M
by the conditions

\mathcal{A}\left(m\right)\left(X\left(m\right)\right)=1\quad\text{and}\quad\mathrm{Ker}\left(\mathcal{A}\left(m\right)\right)=E_{u}\left(m\right)\oplus E_{s}\left(m\right).

(4.4)

From this definition,
\mathcal{A}
is preserved
13
13

13

Notice from (
4.2
), that
\mathcal{A}
is the
unique continuous one form preserved by the flow with the normalization
condition
\mathcal{A}\left(X\right)=1
.
by the flow
\phi^{t}
and the map
m\rightarrow\mathcal{A}\left(m\right)

is Hölder continuous with exponent
\beta_{0}
.

4.1.2
Contact Anosov vector field

In addition to section
4.1.1
we assume
in this paper that the Anosov vector field
X
is
contact
,
i.e. that the Anosov one form
\mathcal{A}
defined in (
4.4
),
is a
smooth contact one form
on
M
. This means that the
distribution
E_{u}\left(m\right)\oplus E_{s}\left(m\right)
is smooth
(i.e.
C^{\infty}
) w.r.t.
m\in M
(hence with Hölder exponent

\beta_{0}=1
) and that the linear space
\mathrm{Ker}\left(\mathcal{A}\left(m\right)\right)=E_{u}\left(m\right)\oplus E_{s}\left(m\right)

endowed with the two form
d\mathcal{A}\left(m\right)
is a linear
symplectic space for every
m\in M
.

Since
\mathcal{A}
is invariant by the flow, for any
u_{1},u_{2}\in E_{s}\left(m\right)

we have
\forall t\in\mathbb{R}
,
\left(d\mathcal{A}\right)\left(u_{1},u_{2}\right)=\left(d\left(\left(\phi^{t}\right)^{*}\mathcal{A}\right)\right)\left(u_{1},u_{2}\right)=\left(d\mathcal{A}\right)\left(d\phi^{t}\left(u_{1}\right),d\phi^{t}\left(u_{2}\right)\right)\underset{t\rightarrow+\infty}{\rightarrow}0

hence
d\mathcal{A}_{/E_{s}}=0
meaning that
E_{s}\left(m\right)

is
d\mathcal{A}-
isotropic. Similarly
E_{u}\left(m\right)
is

d\mathcal{A}-
isotropic hence
E_{u}\left(m\right),E_{s}\left(m\right)

are
d\mathcal{A}-
Lagrangian and

d:=\mathrm{dim}E_{u}\left(m\right)=\mathrm{dim}E_{s}\left(m\right),

(4.5)

hence
\mathrm{dim}M=2d+1
is odd.

Remark 4.1
.

The vector field
X
is the
Reeb vector field

of
\mathcal{A}
, i.e.
X
is determined from
\mathcal{A}
by

\mathcal{A}\left(X\right)=1
and
14
14

14

From Cartan formula and invariance of
\mathcal{A}
:
0=\mathcal{L}_{X}\mathcal{A}=\iota_{X}d\mathcal{A}+d\iota_{X}\mathcal{A}

and
\iota_{X}\mathcal{A}=1
give
\left(d\mathcal{A}\right)\left(X,.\right)=0
.

\left(d\mathcal{A}\right)\left(X,.\right)=0,

(4.6)

as the setting of section
3
.

4.1.3
Dual decomposition

The dual bundle decomposition of (
4.1
) is

T^{*}M=E_{u}^{*}\oplus E_{s}^{*}\oplus E_{0}^{*}

(4.7)

with

\displaystyle E_{u}^{*}

\displaystyle:=\left(E_{s}\oplus E_{0}\right)^{\perp}=\left\{\rho\in T^{*}M,\quad\mathrm{Ker}\rho\supset E_{s}\oplus E_{0}\right\},

(4.8)

\displaystyle E_{s}^{*}

\displaystyle:=\left(E_{u}\oplus E_{0}\right)^{\perp}=\left\{\rho\in T^{*}M,\quad\mathrm{Ker}\rho\supset E_{u}\oplus E_{0}\right\},

(4.9)

\displaystyle E_{0}^{*}

\displaystyle:=\left(E_{u}\oplus E_{s}\right)^{\perp}=\left\{\rho\in T^{*}M,\quad\mathrm{Ker}\rho\supset E_{u}\oplus E_{s}\right\}

(4.10)

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:one_form})}{=}\mathbb{R}\mathcal{A}=\left\{\omega\mathcal{A}\left(m\right),\quad m\in M,\omega\in\mathbb{R}\right\}.

(4.11)

Hence

\mathrm{dim}E_{u}^{*}\left(m\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:dim_d})}{=}\mathrm{dim}E_{s}^{*}\left(m\right)=d,\quad\mathrm{dim}E_{0}^{*}\left(m\right)=1.

From (
4.3
), the map
m\in M\rightarrow E_{u}^{*}\left(m\right)

is
\beta_{s}
-Hölder continuous and
m\in M\rightarrow E_{s}^{*}\left(m\right)

is
\beta_{u}
-Hölder continuous. However the maps
m\in M\rightarrow E_{0}^{*}\left(m\right)=\mathbb{R}\mathcal{A}\left(m\right)

and
m\in M\rightarrow\left(E_{u}^{*}\oplus E_{s}^{*}\right)\left(m\right)=E_{0}^{\perp}\left(m\right)=\mathrm{Ker}\left(X\right)\left(m\right)

are smooth and have already been defined in (
3.3
) and
(
3.6
).

A cotangent vector
\rho\in T^{*}M
is decomposed accordingly to
the dual decomposition (
4.7
) as

\rho=\rho_{u}+\rho_{s}+\rho_{0}

(4.12)

with components

\rho_{u}\in E_{u}^{*},\rho_{s}\in E_{s}^{*},\quad\rho_{0}=\omega\mathcal{A}\left(m\right)\in E_{0}^{*}

with
m=\pi\left(\rho\right)
and the frequency
\omega\underset{(\ref{eq:omega_function})}{=}\boldsymbol{\omega}\left(\rho\right)\in\mathbb{R}
.

By duality, the hyperbolicity assumption (
4.2
)
gives that the components (
4.12
) of
\rho\left(t\right)=\tilde{\phi}^{t}\left(\rho\right)

satisfy

\displaystyle\exists C

\displaystyle>0,\forall\rho\left(0\right)\in T^{*}M,\forall t\geq 0,

\displaystyle\left\|\rho_{u}\left(t\right)\right\|_{g_{M}}\geq\frac{1}{C}e^{\lambda_{\mathrm{min}}t}\left\|\rho_{u}\left(0\right)\right\|_{g_{M}},\qquad\left\|\rho_{s}\left(t\right)\right\|_{g_{M}}\leq Ce^{-\lambda_{\mathrm{min}}t}\left\|\rho_{s}\left(0\right)\right\|_{g_{M}},\quad\omega\left(t\right)=\omega\left(0\right).

(4.13)

See figure
4.2
.

Figure
4.2:
The Anosov flow
\phi^{t}=\exp\left(tX\right)

on
M
induces a Hamiltonian flow
\tilde{\phi}^{t}=\exp\left(t\tilde{X}\right)

in cotangent space
T^{*}M
. The magenta lines on
M
represent
“internal oscillations” of a function

u
, that is a wave packet and is supposed here to have a small support
near
\phi^{t}\left(m\right)
. These oscillations correspond to a
cotangent vector
\rho\in T_{\phi^{t}\left(m\right)}^{*}M
. Transported
by the flow the oscillations of
e^{tX}u=u\circ\phi^{t}
increase
and the wave front of these oscillations become parallel to
E_{s}\oplus E_{0}

equivalently
\rho\left(t\right)=\tilde{\phi^{t}}\left(\rho\right)

converges to the direction of
E_{u}^{*}\subset T^{*}M
and remains
in the frequency level
\Sigma_{\omega}:=\boldsymbol{\omega}^{-1}\left(\omega\right)
.
The
trapped set
of the lifted flow
\tilde{\phi^{t}}
is
the rank one vector bundle
E_{0}^{*}=\mathbb{R}\mathcal{A}
(green
line) where
\mathcal{A}
is the Anosov one form. From Lemma
3.2
,

\Sigma:=E_{0}^{*}\backslash\left\{0\right\}
is a symplectic sub-manifold
of
T^{*}M
, with
\mathrm{dim}\Sigma=2\left(d+1\right)
.

4.1.4
Trapped set

From (
4.13
) we deduce the following Lemma.

Lemma 4.2
.

The set
E_{0}^{*}\underset{(\ref{eq:def_E_0*-1})}{=}\mathbb{R}\mathcal{A}

is the
trapped set
(or
non wandering set
)
of the flow
\tilde{\phi}^{t}
in the sense that

\displaystyle E_{0}^{*}\underset{(\ref{eq:def_E_0*-1})}{=}\mathbb{R}\mathcal{A}\underset{(\ref{eq:def_Sigma})}{=}\Sigma\cup\left\{0\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{\rho\in T^{*}M\,\,\mid\quad\exists\mathcal{K}\subset T^{*}M\mbox{ compact, s.t. }\tilde{\phi}^{t}\left(\rho\right)\in\mathcal{K},\quad\forall t\in\mathbb{R}\right\},

(4.14)

and
E_{0}^{*}
is
transversely hyperbolic
.

Démonstration
.

From (
4.12
), the trapped set
E_{0}^{*}
is characterized
by
\rho_{u}=0,\rho_{s}=0
and from (
4.13
),
E_{0}^{*}

is transversely hyperbolic.
∎

4.2
Anisotropic Sobolev space,
decay of norm outside the trapped set and discrete Ruelle spectrum

In Lemma
4.2
we have observed that the trapped
set
E_{0}^{*}
is transversely hyperbolic. This will permit us to
define a weight function or escape function
W:T^{*}M\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}

that decays outside the trapped set (i.e. that is a
Lyapounov function

for the flow
\tilde{\phi}^{t}
), following the constructions in

[
16
]
or
[
20
, Def 5.6]
.
Then we define the anisotropic Sobolev space
\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)

and obtain that the norm of the pull back operator
e^{tX}
restricted
to the outside of
E_{0}^{*}
decays exponentially fast with an arbitrarily
large rate. This is the important property that will enable us to
restrict the analysis of the operator
e^{tX}
for
t>0
, to a neighborhood
of the trapped set
E_{0}^{*}
in the forthcoming sections. In this
section we recall all these results.

4.2.1
Definition and properties of the
escape function
W

Following
[
20
, Def 5.6]
,
we consider the positive valued continuous function
W\in C\left(T^{*}M;\mathbb{R}^{+}\backslash\left\{0\right\}\right)

called
weight function
defined as follows. Let
R\geq 0
,

\gamma\in[1-\beta,1[
with
\beta
given in (
4.3
).
For
\rho\in T^{*}M
, with stable/unstable components
\rho_{s}\in E_{s}^{*},\rho_{u}\in E_{u}^{*}

given in (
4.12
), we define

W\left(\rho\right):=\frac{\left\langle h_{\gamma}\left(\rho\right)\left\|\rho_{s}\right\|_{g_{\rho}}\right\rangle^{R}}{\left\langle h_{\gamma}\left(\rho\right)\left\|\rho_{u}\right\|_{g_{\rho}}\right\rangle^{R}}

(4.15)

with

h_{\gamma}\left(\rho\right):=\left\langle\left(\left\|\rho_{u}\right\|_{g_{\rho}}^{2}+\left\|\rho_{s}\right\|_{g_{\rho}}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\right\rangle^{-\gamma}

(4.16)

Remark 4.3
.

In the previous expressions, the vertical vectors
\rho_{u},\rho_{s}\in T_{m}^{*}M

are naturally identified with vectors in the tangent space
T_{\rho}\left(T^{*}M\right)

of
T^{*}M
at point
\rho\in T^{*}M
, so we can get their norm,

\left\|\rho_{u}\right\|_{g_{\rho}},\left\|\rho_{s}\right\|_{g_{\rho}}
.

The function
W:\rho\in T^{*}M\rightarrow W\left(\rho\right)\in\mathbb{R}

in (
4.15
) is positive and Hölder continuous, since the
decomposition (
4.12
) is Hölder continuous.
W
has
a few important properties given in
[
20
, Thm 5.9]

as
decay property
and
slow variation properties
.

4.2.2
Anisotropic spaces
\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M;F\right)

We will define the anisotropic Sobolev spaces
\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M;F\right)

used in this paper, in order to get Theorem
4.8
below,
saying that the operator
X_{F}
has
intrinsic discrete spectrum

in
\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M;F\right)
.

Remark 4.4
.

Let us remark why to get discrete
spectrum, we cannot consider the Hilbert space
L^{2}\left(M;F\right)
.
If we choose an arbitrary smooth Hermitian metric on the vector bundle

F\rightarrow M
and using the measure
dm
on
M
, (
3.2
),
we can define the Hilbert space
L^{2}\left(M;F\right)
of sections
of
F
. One can show that the operator
X_{F}
, Eq.(
2.3
),
acting in
L^{2}\left(M;F\right)
has only
essential spectrum

contained in some vertical band (the detailed argument will be explained
in a forthcoming paper):

\mathrm{spec}\left(X_{F}:L^{2}\left(M;F\right)\rightarrow L^{2}\left(M;F\right)\right)=\left\{z\in\mathbb{C},\mathrm{Re}\left(z\right)\in\left[\gamma_{L^{2}}^{-},\gamma_{L^{2}}^{+}\right]\right\},

with

\gamma_{L^{2}}^{\pm}=\lim_{t\rightarrow\pm\infty}\log\left\|e^{tX_{F}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{1/t}.

For example, considering the vector field
X
itself and the trivial
bundle
F=M\times\mathbb{C}
, since
\mathrm{div}_{dm}X=0
, we have
that
X=-X^{\dagger}
is skew symmetric in
L^{2}\left(M\right)

and
X
has continuous spectrum on the imaginary axis
i\mathbb{R}
.

For simplicity of notations, we will forget the vector bundle
F
,
that plays no role for this construction, i.e. we first consider only
a trivial bundle
F=M\times\mathbb{C}
. For any
u\in C^{\infty}\left(M\right)

we define its
\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)
-norm

\left\|u\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}:=\left\|W\mathcal{T}u\right\|_{L^{2}\left(T^{*}M\right)}

(4.17)

where
W
is used as a multiplication operator on
\mathcal{S}\left(T^{*}M\right)
.
Let
\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)
be the Hilbert space obtained
by completion of
C^{\infty}\left(M\right)
with respect to this
norm and called
anisotropic Sobolev space
:

\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right):=\overline{\left\{u\in C^{\infty}\left(M\right),\quad\left\|u\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}<\infty\right\}}.

(4.18)

In other words
W\mathcal{T}:\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)\rightarrow L^{2}\left(T^{*}M\right)

is an isometry. To define
\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M;F\right)
we proceed
similarly by completion of the space of smooth sections
C^{\infty}\left(M;F\right)
.
For more details or information, we refer to
[
20
, def 4.37]
.

Remark 4.5
.

The function
W
in (
4.15
) satisfies

\exists C>0,\forall\rho\in T^{*}M,\,C^{-1}\left\langle\left|\rho\right|_{g_{M}}\right\rangle^{-\left|r\right|}\leq W\left(\rho\right)\leq C\left\langle\left|\rho\right|_{g_{M}}\right\rangle^{\left|r\right|}.

(4.19)

with

r=\frac{R}{2}\left(1-\gamma\right)>0.

(4.20)

Consequently

H^{r}\left(M\right)\subset\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)\subset H^{-r}\left(M\right).

(4.21)

where
H^{r}\left(M\right):=\mathrm{Op}\left(\left\langle\left|\xi\right|_{g_{M}}\right\rangle^{-r}\right)\left(L^{2}\left(M\right)\right)

is the standard
Sobolev space

of order
r
.

4.2.3
Decay of norm outside the trapped
set

Recall the truncation operator
\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\right)

at distance
\sigma
from the set
\Sigma
defined in (
3.31
).

Theorem 4.6

(Decay outside the trapped set)
.

[
20
, Thm 5.13]
For
any
\Lambda>0
, we can choose
R\gg 1
large enough as a parameter
of the function
W
in (
4.15
), such that
\exists C>0,\forall t\geq 0,

\exists\sigma_{t},\forall\sigma>\sigma_{t}
we have

\left\|e^{tX}\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\right)\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M;F\right)}\leq Ce^{-\Lambda t},

(4.22)

\left\|\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\right)\right)e^{tX}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M;F\right)}\leq Ce^{-\Lambda t}.

(4.23)

Remark 4.7
.

Consider the decomposition

e^{tX}=e^{tX}\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\right)\right)+e^{tX}\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\right)
.
Theorem
4.6
means that the first component
e^{tX}\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\right)\right)

decays exponentially fast as
O\left(e^{-\Lambda t}\right)
with
an arbitrarily large rate
\Lambda\gg 1
. We deduce that in order
to describe the dominant effect of the operator
e^{tX}
and Ruelle
eigenvalues on the spectral domain
\mathrm{Re}\left(z\right)>-\Lambda
,
one has to study the second component
e^{tX}\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\right)

namely the dynamics
e^{tX}
restricted to a neighborhood of the
trapped set
\Sigma=E_{0}^{*}\backslash\left\{0\right\}
. Then,
from Theorem
3.15
, we only need to study
the operator
\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(d\tilde{\phi}_{N}^{t}\right)

acting on the normal bundle over the trapped set
\Sigma
. This is
the subject of the section
4.3
below.

4.2.4
Discrete Ruelle spectrum in anisotropic Sobolev spaces
\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M;F\right)

In paper
[
20
, thm 5.14]
,
Theorem
4.6
is used to prove the following theorem. Recall
that the function
W
in (
4.15
) depends on the exponent

R
and that the order
r=\frac{R}{2}\left(1-\gamma\right)
is defined
from
R
in (
4.20
).

Theorem 4.8
.

“Group property and discrete spectrum”
[
20
, thm 2.11]
.

For a general Anosov vector field
X
on
M
, the family of operators

e^{tX_{F}}:\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M;F\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M;F\right),\qquad t\in\mathbb{R},

form a
strongly continuous group
and the generator
X_{F}

has
discrete spectrum
denoted
\mathrm{spect}\left(X_{F}\right)
,
on
C-r\lambda_{\mathrm{min}}\leq\mathrm{Re}\left(z\right)\leq C^{\prime}

with
C,C^{\prime}
independent of the parameter
R
. This spectrum is
intrinsic

(i.e. does not depend on
W
) and called
future Ruelle spectrum

in
[
20
, Thm 2.11]
.

See Figure
1.2
.

4.3
Description of the operator
e^{tX}

near the set
\Sigma
with the bundle
N_{s}

4.3.1
Decomposition
N=N_{u}\oplus N_{s}

From the Anosov property, we have the decomposition
\mathrm{Ker}\mathcal{A}\underset{(\ref{eq:one_form})}{=}E_{u}\oplus E_{s}

into Hölder continuous sub-bundles, that gives a refined decomposition
of (
3.11
) into isotropic subspaces:

Lemma 4.9
.

Let

N_{u}:=N\cap d\pi^{-1}\left(E_{u}\right),\quad N_{s}:=N\cap d\pi^{-1}\left(E_{s}\right),

N=N_{u}\oplus N_{s},

(4.24)

K_{u}:=K\cap d\pi^{-1}\left(E_{u}\right),\quad K_{s}:=K\cap d\pi^{-1}\left(E_{s}\right),\quad K=K_{u}\oplus K_{s},

(4.25)

\mathrm{dim}K_{u}=\mathrm{dim}K_{s}=\mathrm{dim}N_{u}=\mathrm{dim}N_{s}=d,\quad\mathrm{dim}K_{0}=2,

Each component
K_{u},K_{s},\tilde{E}_{0},\tilde{E}_{0}^{*},N_{u},N_{s}

is a
\Omega-
isotropic vector space, invariant under the dynamics

d\tilde{\phi}^{t}
. The indices
u,s,0
denotes respectively instability,
stability or neutrality. See Figure
1.1
.

Remark 4.10
.

•

Notice that due to (
2.10
) that reverses the direction
of dynamics,
N_{u}
is stable for
\left(d\tilde{\phi}^{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}

and
N_{s}
is unstable.

•

The symplectic normal bundle
N\rightarrow\Sigma
is smooth, but
the isotropic sub-bundle
N_{s}\rightarrow\Sigma
is only
\beta_{s}
-Hölder
continuous.

Démonstration
.

From the fact explained in section
4.1.2

that
E_{s},E_{u}
are
d\mathcal{A}-
isotropic and that
d\pi

is a symplectomorphism in (
3.22
) and (
3.23
),
we deduce that
N_{u},N_{s},K_{u},K_{s}
are
\Omega-
isotropic.
∎

4.3.2
Transfer operators on
N_{s}

We start from Theorem
3.15
. We have the
splitting
N=N_{s}\oplus N_{u}
in (
4.24
) of the symplectic
bundle
N
over
\Sigma
into Lagrangian sub-bundles. We consider
the operator
\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(d\tilde{\phi}_{N}^{t}\right)

on the right hand side of (
3.40
) and our purpose is
to express it as an operator acting only on the Lagrangian sub-bundle

N_{s}
. The bundles
N_{u},N_{s}
are invariant under the dynamics

d\tilde{\phi}^{t}
, hence the flow map splits accordingly (recall
Remark
4.10
)

d\tilde{\phi}_{N}^{t}=d\tilde{\phi}_{N_{u}}^{t}+d\tilde{\phi}_{N_{s}}^{t}\quad:N\rightarrow N.

Notation 4.11
.

The bundle
N_{s}\rightarrow\Sigma
defined
in (
4.25
) is Hölder continuous with exponent
\beta
.
For simplicity we denote

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\beta}\left(N_{s}\right)

\displaystyle:=C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{S}\left(N_{s}\left(.\right)\right)\right)

(4.26)

as the set of functions
f\in C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{S}\left(N_{s}\left(.\right)\right)\right)
,

\beta
-Hölder continuous on
\Sigma
with fast decay in frequency

\omega
, valued in the
Schwartz space

of functions
\mathcal{S}\left(N_{s}\left(\rho\right)\right)
for
each
\rho\in\Sigma
. This notation as already been introduced in
(
1.8
) and will also concerns later other space of
sections over
\Sigma
. Notice that
\mathcal{S}\left(N_{s}\left(.\right)\right)\rightarrow\Sigma

is an infinite rank bundle vector over
\Sigma
.

4.3.3
The bundle
\mathcal{F}\left(N_{s}\right)\rightarrow\Sigma

Let us denote
\left|\mathrm{det}N_{s}\right|^{-1/2}
the dual of
the half density bundle in
N_{s}

[
28
, p.509]
.

Definition 4.12
.

Let

\mathcal{F}\left(N_{s}\right):=\left|\mathrm{det}N_{s}\right|^{-1/2}\otimes\mathcal{S}_{\beta}\left(N_{s}\right)

(4.27)

and

X_{\mathcal{F}}:C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}\left(N_{s}\right)\right)\rightarrow C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}\left(N_{s}\right)\right)

(4.28)

be the derivation of sections of
\mathcal{F}
over the vector field

\tilde{X}
, that is generator of the group of operators,
\forall t\in\mathbb{R}
,

e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}:=\left|\mathrm{det}\left(d\tilde{\phi}_{N_{s}}^{t}\right)\right|^{-1/2}\left(d\tilde{\phi}_{N_{s}}^{t}\right)^{-\circ}:C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}\left(N_{s}\right)\right)\rightarrow C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}\left(N_{s}\right)\right).

(4.29)

Remark 4.13
.

The derivation
X_{\mathcal{F}}
over
\tilde{X}
in (
4.28
)
is well defined since the bundle
\mathcal{F}\left(N_{s}\right)

is constructed from the bundle
N_{s}
and the given vector field

X
on
M
induces a natural derivation on
N_{s}
hence
X_{\mathcal{F}}

on
\mathcal{F}\left(N_{s}\right)
. Recall from Remark
4.10

that the linear map
d\tilde{\phi}_{N_{s}}^{t}
is expanding on
N_{s}
,
hence
\left|\mathrm{det}\left(d\tilde{\phi}_{N_{s}}^{t}\right)\right|>1
.
The operator
X_{\mathcal{F}}
has a domain that includes sections
smooth along the flow direction. Recall that for simplicity of notation
we have ignored the bundle
F
in (
4.27
).

Now we will relate the operator
\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(d\tilde{\phi}_{N}^{t}\right)

on the right hand side of (
3.40
) to the operator
e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}

defined in (
4.29
). In (
C.8
) we define
the Bargman transform

\mathcal{B}_{N_{s}}:\mathcal{S}_{\beta}\left(N_{s}\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(N_{s}\oplus N_{s}^{*}\right).

As explained in Lemma
C.16
, we have an
\left(\Omega,g\right)-
isomorphism

\Psi:N=N_{s}\oplus N_{s}^{\perp_{g}}\rightarrow N_{s}\oplus N_{s}^{*}.

But
N_{u}\neq N_{s}^{\perp_{g}}
in general, so we introduce
S_{N_{s},N_{u}}:N\rightarrow N

the linear (shear) symplectic map defined as identity on
N_{s}

and any vector of
N_{s}^{\perp_{g}}
is mapped to its projection
on
N_{u}
parallel to
N_{s}
:

S_{N_{s},N_{u}}\left(N_{s}\right)=N_{s},\quad S_{N_{s},N_{u}}\left(N_{s}^{\perp_{g}}\right)=N_{u}.

Notice that the bundle of maps
S_{N_{s},N_{u}},\Psi
is uniformly
bounded over
\Sigma
. The operator
\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(S_{N_{s},N_{u}}\Psi^{-1}\right):\mathcal{S}\left(N_{s}\oplus N_{s}^{*}\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(N\right)

is defined as in (
C.36
) and we set

\mathcal{B}_{N_{s},N_{u}}:=\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(S_{N_{s},N_{u}}\Psi^{-1}\right)\mathcal{B}_{N_{s}}\quad:\mathcal{S}_{\beta}\left(N_{s}\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(N\right).

(4.30)

Lemma 4.14
.

We have

\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(d\tilde{\phi}_{N}^{t}\right)=\mathcal{B}_{N_{s},N_{u}}e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\mathcal{B}_{N_{s},N_{u}}^{\dagger}.

(4.31)

Démonstration
.

Let
\Phi_{N_{s}}:=\left(d\tilde{\phi}_{N_{s}}^{t}\right)\oplus\left(d\tilde{\phi}_{N_{s}}^{t}\right)^{*-1}:N_{s}\oplus N_{s}^{*}\rightarrow N_{s}\oplus N_{s}^{*}
.
From Lemma
C.31
with the setting
\phi=\left(d\tilde{\phi}_{N_{s}}^{t}\right)^{-1}
,
we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi_{N_{s}}\right)

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:Op_tilde_PHI})}{=}\left|\mathrm{det}\left(d\tilde{\phi}_{N_{s}}^{t}\right)\right|^{-1/2}\mathcal{B}_{N_{s}}\left(d\tilde{\phi}_{N_{s}}^{t}\right)^{-\circ}\mathcal{B}_{N_{s}}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:def_XF-1})}{=}\mathcal{B}_{N_{s}}e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\mathcal{B}_{N_{s}}^{\dagger}\quad:\mathcal{S}\left(N_{s}\oplus N_{s}^{*}\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(N_{s}\oplus N_{s}^{*}\right)

(4.32)

By construction, we have the conjugation

d\tilde{\phi}_{N}^{t}=\left(S_{N_{s},N_{u}}\Psi^{-1}\right)\circ\Phi_{N_{s}}\circ\left(S_{N_{s},N_{u}}\Psi^{-1}\right)^{-1}\quad:N\rightarrow N

that gives

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(d\tilde{\phi}_{N}^{t}\right)

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:homom},\ref{eq:op_tilde_Phi_Ns})}{=}\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(S_{N_{s},N_{u}}\Psi^{-1}\right)\mathcal{B}_{N_{s}}e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\mathcal{B}_{N_{s}}^{\dagger}\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(S_{N_{s},N_{u}}\Psi^{-1}\right)^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:def_B_Ns_Nu})}{=}\mathcal{B}_{N_{s},N_{u}}e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\mathcal{B}_{N_{s},N_{u}}^{\dagger}.

(4.33)

∎

We finish this section with the description of the operator
e^{tX}

near the set
\Sigma
using the bundle
N_{s}
.

Definition 4.15
.

We define the operators

\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}:=\mathcal{B}_{N_{s},N_{u}}^{\dagger}\mathcal{T}_{N}\underset{(\ref{eq:def_T_N})}{=}\mathcal{B}_{N_{s},N_{u}}^{\dagger}\chi_{\Sigma}^{\mu}\widetilde{\left(\exp_{N}\right)^{\circ}}\mathcal{T}\quad:C^{\infty}\left(M\right)\rightarrow C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}\left(N_{s}\right)\right)

(4.34)

\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}^{\Delta}:=\mathcal{T}_{N}^{\Delta}\mathcal{B}_{N_{s},N_{u}}\underset{(\ref{eq:def_T_N_Delta})}{=}\mathcal{T}^{\dagger}\widetilde{\left(\exp_{N}^{-1}\right)^{\circ}}\chi_{\Sigma}^{\mu}\mathcal{B}_{N_{s},N_{u}}\quad:C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}\left(N_{s}\right)\right)\rightarrow C^{\infty}\left(M\right).

(4.35)

Theorem 4.16
.

For any fixed
t\in\mathbb{R}
,
we have

e^{tX}\approx\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}^{\Delta}\,\Upsilon_{t}^{1/2}e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}

(4.36)

Recall that the metaplectic correction
\Upsilon_{t}^{1/2}>0
defined
in (
3.39
) is a function on
\Sigma
considered
as a multiplication operator.

Démonstration
.

We write

\displaystyle e^{tX}

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:exp_tX_N})}{\approx}\mathcal{T}_{N}^{\Delta}\,\Upsilon_{t}^{1/2}\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(d\tilde{\phi}_{N}^{t}\right)\mathcal{T}_{N}

(4.37)

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:B_exp_B},\ref{eq:def_T_Ns},\ref{eq:def_T_Ns_Delta})}{=}\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}^{\Delta}\,\Upsilon_{t}^{1/2}e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}.

∎

4.4
The bundle map
e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}
and sub-bundle
\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(N_{s}\right)

The expression (
4.36
) will be convenient to get some
properties of the dynamical operator
e^{tX}
from the study of properties
of the operator
e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}
. For example, one important
property that we will first exploit is that
d\tilde{\phi}_{N_{s}}^{t}:N_{s}\rightarrow N_{s}

is a linear map hence the operators
\left(d\tilde{\phi}_{N_{s}}^{t}\right)^{-\circ}

and
e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}
in (
4.29
) preserve the finite
rank sub-bundle of homogeneous polynomials of degree
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
This property is responsible for the band structure of the spectrum.

4.4.1
Taylor projectors
T_{k}

Since
d\tilde{\phi}_{N_{s}}^{t}:N_{s}\rightarrow N_{s}
is a vector
bundle homomorphism, the push-forward operator
\left(d\tilde{\phi}_{N_{s}}^{t}\right)^{-\circ}

in (
4.29
) keeps invariant the space of homogeneous polynomial
functions of order
k\in\mathbb{N}
denoted
\mathrm{Pol}_{k}\left(N_{s}\right)\subset\mathcal{S}^{\prime}_{\beta}\left(N_{s}\right)
.
To express this property, we first consider the vector bundle
E_{s}\rightarrow M

and the bundle map of finite rank “Taylor projectors” over
\mathrm{Id}
,
from Definition
C.38
, denoted

T_{k}:\mathcal{S}_{\beta}\left(E_{s}\right)\rightarrow\mathrm{Pol}_{k}\left(E_{s}\right)\subset\mathcal{S}^{\prime}_{\beta}\left(E_{s}\right)

(4.38)

and

T_{\left[0,K\right]}:=\sum_{k=0}^{K}T_{k},\qquad T_{\geq\left(K+1\right)}=\mathrm{Id}-T_{\left[0,K\right]},

(4.39)

satisfying

T_{k}T_{k^{\prime}}=\delta_{k=k^{\prime}}T_{k}.

(4.40)

Using the projection map
d\pi:N_{s}\rightarrow E_{s}
in (
3.23
),
we lift these operators to the vector bundle
\mathcal{S}_{\beta}\left(N_{s}\right)\rightarrow\Sigma
:

\tilde{T}_{k}:\mathcal{S}_{\beta}\left(N_{s}\right)\rightarrow\mathrm{Pol}_{k}\left(N_{s}\right)\subset\mathcal{S}^{\prime}_{\beta}\left(N_{s}\right).

(4.41)

We define
\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}
and
\tilde{T}_{\geq\left(K+1\right)}

similarly to (
4.39
). We have (in the next equation and
after, a strict notation should be
\mathrm{Id}_{\left|\mathrm{det}N_{s}\right|^{-1/2}}\otimes\tilde{T}_{k}

instead of
\tilde{T}_{k}
)

\left[e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}},\tilde{T}_{k}\right]=0.

(4.42)

Definition 4.17
.

We denote

\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(N_{s}\right):=\left|\mathrm{det}N_{s}\right|^{-1/2}\otimes\mathcal{\mathrm{Pol}}_{k}\left(N_{s}\right)

(4.43)

that is a finite rank bundle over
\Sigma
. The space of sections

C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(N_{s}\right)\right)

is the image of the bundle map
\tilde{T}_{k}:C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}\left(N_{s}\right)\right)\rightarrow C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(N_{s}\right)\right)
.

We denote
X_{\mathcal{F}_{k}}
the generator
X_{\mathcal{F}}

in (
4.28
) restricted to the invariant space of sections

C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(N_{s}\right)\right)
.

More generally, for
0\leq k_{1}\leq k_{2}
, we denote the direct
sum bundle

\mathcal{F}_{\left[k_{1},k_{2}\right]}\left(N_{s}\right):=\bigoplus_{k=k_{1}}^{k_{2}}\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(N_{s}\right),

(4.44)

and denote
X_{\mathcal{F}_{\left[k_{1},k_{2}\right]}}
the generator
in
C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}_{\left[k_{1},k_{2}\right]}\left(N_{s}\right)\right)
.

4.4.2
Spectrum of the derivation
X_{\mathcal{F}_{k}}
in
L^{2}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(N_{s}\right)\right)

The operators
\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}_{k}}}\right)_{t\in\mathbb{R}}

in (
4.29
) form a group of point-wise linear bundle maps
over
\tilde{\phi}^{t}:\Sigma\rightarrow\Sigma
that preserves the
volume. Consequently all
L^{p}
norms
\left\|e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}_{k}}}\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}_{k}\right)}

for
p\in\left[1,\infty\right]
are equal, as it is for general multiplication
operators.

Definition 4.18
.

For any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, let

\gamma_{k}^{\pm}:=\lim_{t\rightarrow\pm\infty}\log\left\|e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}_{k}}}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(N_{s}\right)\right)}^{1/t}

(4.45)

Remark 4.19
.

The values
\gamma_{k}^{\pm}
defined in (
4.45
) with

L^{2}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(N_{s}\right)\right)
coincide
with the Definition (
1.6
) using
L^{\infty}\left(M;\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(E_{s}\right)\right)
.

Lemma 4.20
.

We have the estimates
\forall\epsilon>0,\exists C_{\epsilon}>0,\forall t\geq 0,

\left\|e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}_{k}}}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(N_{s}\right)\right)}\leq C_{\epsilon}e^{t\left(\gamma_{k}^{+}+\epsilon\right)},\qquad\left\|e^{-tX_{\mathcal{F}_{k}}}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(N_{s}\right)\right)}\leq C_{\epsilon}e^{-t\left(\gamma_{k}^{-}-\epsilon\right)}.

(4.46)

Equivalently
\forall\epsilon>0,\exists C_{\epsilon}>0
,
\forall z\in\mathbb{C}
,

\mathrm{Re}\left(z\right)>\gamma_{k}^{+}+\epsilon
or
\mathrm{Re}\left(z\right)<\gamma_{k}^{-}-\epsilon
,

\left\|\left(z-X_{\mathcal{F}_{k}}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(N_{s}\right)\right)}\leq C_{\epsilon}.

(4.47)

Consequently the spectrum of the operator
X_{\mathcal{F}_{k}}
in

L^{2}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(N_{s}\right)\right)
is contained
in the vertical band

B_{k}:=\left\{\mathrm{Re}\left(z\right)\in\left[\gamma_{k}^{-},\gamma_{k}^{+}\right]\right\}.

(4.48)

Remark 4.21
.

We will explain in a forthcoming paper that the spectrum of
X_{\mathcal{F}_{k}}

in
L^{2}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(N_{s}\right)\right)
consists
only of
essential spectrum

that fills the whole band
B_{k}
.

Démonstration
.

Eq.(
4.45
) implies (
4.46
) and also (
4.47
)
and claim for (
4.48
) by writing the convergent expression
for
\mathrm{Re}\left(z\right)>\gamma_{k}^{+}+\epsilon
:

\left(z-X_{\mathcal{F}_{k}}\right)^{-1}=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{t\left(X_{\mathcal{F}_{k}}-z\right)}dt

that gives, if
\epsilon^{\prime}<\epsilon
,

\displaystyle\left\|\left(z-X_{\mathcal{F}_{k}}\right)^{-1}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\mathrm{Re}\left(z\right)t}\left\|e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}_{k}}}\right\|dt\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\mathrm{Re}\left(z\right)t}C_{\epsilon^{\prime}}e^{t\left(\gamma_{k}^{+}+\epsilon^{\prime}\right)}dt\leq C_{\epsilon}.

Similarly for
\mathrm{Re}\left(z\right)<\gamma_{k}^{-}-\epsilon
,
we have
\left(z-X_{\mathcal{F}_{k}}\right)^{-1}=-\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{t\left(X_{\mathcal{F}_{k}}-z\right)}dt
,
and get also (
4.47
).
∎

4.4.3
Sobolev space
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(N_{s}\right)

and remainder term

The operator
e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}
acts in the infinite rank bundle

\mathcal{F}\left(N_{s}\right)
in (
4.27
). We explain
here how to “extract” the finite codimension part
e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{\geq\left(K+1\right)}
.
We define a weight function
\mathcal{W}:N\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}

similar to
W:T^{*}M\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
in (
4.15
),
with the same parameters
0<\gamma<1
,
R>0
, as follows. For
\rho\in\Sigma
,

v=v_{u}+v_{s}\in N\left(\rho\right)=N_{u}\left(\rho\right)\oplus N_{s}\left(\rho\right)

with
v_{u}\in N_{u}\left(\rho\right)
,
v_{s}\in N_{s}\left(\rho\right)
,
let

\mathcal{W}\left(v\right):=\frac{\left\langle h_{\gamma}\left(v\right)\left\|v_{s}\right\|_{g_{\rho}}\right\rangle^{R}}{\left\langle h_{\gamma}\left(v\right)\left\|v_{u}\right\|_{g_{\rho}}\right\rangle^{R}}

(4.49)

with

h_{\gamma}\left(v\right)=\left\langle\left\|v\right\|_{g_{\rho}}\right\rangle^{-\gamma}.

The metric
g
on
T^{*}M
is moderate
[
20
, Lemma 4.12]
.
This implies that under the exponential map
\exp_{N}:N\rightarrow T^{*}M

defined in (
3.33
), the functions
\mathcal{W}
and
W

are equivalent in the neighborhood of the trapped set defined in (
3.34
),
i.e.
\exists C\geq 1,\exists\omega_{0}>0,\forall v\in N
with
\left\|v\right\|_{g}\leq\left\langle\omega\right\rangle^{\mu/2}
,

\omega=\boldsymbol{\omega}\left(\pi\left(v\right)\right)
,
\left|\omega\right|\geq\omega_{0}
,
we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{C}\mathcal{W}\left(v\right)\leq W\left(\exp_{N}\left(v\right)\right)\leq C\mathcal{W}\left(v\right).

(4.50)

We define the Sobolev norm of
u\in\mathcal{S}_{\beta}\left(N_{s}\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:notation})}{=}C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{S}\left(N_{s}\right)\right)

by

\left\|u\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(N_{s}\right)}:=\left\|\mathcal{W}\mathcal{B}_{N_{s},N_{u}}u\right\|_{L^{2}\left(N\right)},

and the anisotropic Sobolev space bundle by completion

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(N_{s}\right)

\displaystyle:=\overline{\left\{u\in\mathcal{S}_{\beta}\left(N_{s}\right),\quad\left\|u\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(N_{s}\right)}<\infty\right\}}.

(4.51)

Lemma 4.22
.

For any
\epsilon>0
,
K\in\mathbb{N}
, we
can choose
R
in (
4.49
) large enough and
\omega_{0}>0

so that
\exists C_{K,\epsilon}>0,\forall t\geq 0
,

\left\|e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{\geq\left(K+1\right)}\chi_{\left|\omega\right|\geq\omega_{0}}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(N_{s}\right)}\leq C_{K,\epsilon}e^{\left(\gamma_{K+1}^{+}+\epsilon\right)t}.

(4.52)

Démonstration
.

Let
K\in\mathbb{N}
,
\epsilon>0
and
t\geq 0
. Recall that
e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}

is a bundle map over
\tilde{\phi}^{t}:\Sigma\rightarrow\Sigma
.
Let
\rho\in\Sigma
with
\boldsymbol{\omega}\left(\rho\right)\geq\omega_{0}

and
m=\pi\left(\rho\right)\in M
. We first observe that the operator

e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{\geq\left(K+1\right)}:\mathcal{S}\left(N_{s}\left(\rho\right)\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(N_{s}\left(\tilde{\phi}^{t}\left(\rho\right)\right)\right)

is isomorphic to

e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{\geq\left(K+1\right)}:\mathcal{S}\left(E_{s}\left(m\right)\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(E_{s}\left(\phi^{t}\left(m\right)\right)\right).

with
m\in M
on a compact space providing uniformity over
\Sigma
.
We apply Proposition
D.3
in the appendix giving that

\forall\epsilon>0,
\exists C_{K,\epsilon}>0,\forall t\geq 0
,
\forall u\in\mathcal{S}_{\beta}\left(N_{s}\right)
,

\displaystyle\left\|e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{\geq\left(K+1\right)}\chi_{\left|\omega\right|\geq\omega_{0}}u\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(N_{s}\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma}\left\|\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{\geq\left(K+1\right)}\chi_{\left|\omega\right|\geq\omega_{0}}u\right)\left(\rho\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(N_{s}\right)}^{2}d\rho

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:norm_Lw_out-1})}{\leq}\left(C_{K,\epsilon}e^{\left(\gamma_{K+1}^{+}+\epsilon\right)t}\right)^{2}\int_{\Sigma}\left\|\left(\chi_{\left|\omega\right|\geq\omega_{0}}u\right)\left(\rho\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(N_{s}\right)}^{2}d\rho

\displaystyle\leq\left(C_{K,\epsilon}e^{\left(\gamma_{K+1}^{+}+\epsilon\right)t}\right)^{2}\left\|u\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(N_{s}\right)}^{2},

giving (
4.52
).
∎

Proposition 4.23
.

For any
K\in\mathbb{N}
, we can choose
R
in (
4.49
)
large enough so that for every
k\in\left[0,K\right]
, there exists

C_{k}
such that

\left\|\tilde{T}_{k}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(N_{s}\right)}\leq C_{k}.

(4.53)

Démonstration
.

As in the proof of Lemma
4.22
, we use the fact that
we have a bundle map and at each point of the base, we use Lemma
D.6

that is uniform over
\Sigma
.
∎

4.5
Symbols and F.I.O. on
\Sigma

Recall
\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(E_{s}\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:def_Fk})}{=}\left|\mathrm{det}E_{s}\right|^{-1/2}\otimes\mathrm{Pol}_{k}\left(E_{s}\right)

that is a
C^{\beta}
continuous bundle over
M
and more generally

\mathcal{F}_{[k_{1},k_{2}]}\left(E_{s}\right)
in (
4.44
).

Definition 4.24
.

A
symbol
is a function on
M
operator
valued in
L\left(\mathcal{F}_{\left[0,K\right]}\left(E_{s}\right)\right)

(or
L\left(\mathcal{F}\left(E_{s}\right)\right)
):

a\in C^{\beta}\left(M;L\left(\mathcal{F}_{\left[0,K\right]}\left(E_{s}\right)\right)\right).

Using the projection map
\sqrt{\omega}d\pi:N_{s}\rightarrow E_{s}

in (
3.23
) that is invertible, we obtain a lifted function
denoted

\tilde{a}\in C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;L\left(\mathcal{F}_{\left[0,K\right]}\left(N_{s}\right)\right)\right),

considered below as a multiplicative operator.

Remark 4.25
.

Here is precisely how we get
\tilde{a}
from
a
: for
\rho\in\Sigma
,

m=\pi\left(\rho\right)\in M
, one has
\left(\sqrt{\omega}d\pi_{\rho}\right)^{\circ}:\mathcal{S}\left(E_{s}\left(m\right)\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(N_{s}\left(\rho\right)\right)

and set

\tilde{a}:\rho\in\Sigma\rightarrow\tilde{a}\left(\rho\right):=\left(\sqrt{\omega}d\pi_{\rho}\right)^{\circ}a\left(\sqrt{\omega}d\pi_{\rho}\right)^{-\circ}.

Example 4.26
.

Later we will use Definition (
4.55
) only for symbols
of the form

a=T_{k}\text{ (giving $\tilde{a}=\tilde{T}_{k}$),}\text{ or }a=T_{\left[0,K\right]}\text{ or }a\in\mathrm{End}\left(\mathcal{F}_{\left[0,K\right]}\right)\text{ or }a=T_{\geq\left(K+1\right)}\text{ or }a=\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{F}}.

(4.54)

Recall the operators
\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}:C^{\infty}\left(M\right)\rightarrow C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}\left(N_{s}\right)\right)

and
\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}^{\Delta}:C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}\left(N_{s}\right)\right)\rightarrow C^{\infty}\left(M\right)

defined in (
4.34
) and (
4.35
).

Definition 4.27

(F.I.O., quantum operator)
.

For a symbol
a\in C^{\beta}\left(M;L\left(\mathcal{F}\left(E_{s}\right)\right)\right)

and
t\in\mathbb{R}
, the
quantization
of the symplectic
bundle map
e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{a}:C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}\left(N_{s}\right)\right)\rightarrow C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}\left(N_{s}\right)\right)

is the F.I.O. operator

\displaystyle\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{a}\right)

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}^{\Delta}\Upsilon_{t}^{1/2}e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{a}\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}\quad:C^{\infty}\left(M\right)\rightarrow C^{\infty}\left(M\right)

(4.55)

Remark 4.28
.

The term F.I.O. stands for
Fourier integral operator
,
that is the usual name for this kind of operator.

Using the new notation (
4.55
), previous Theorem
4.16

can be rephrased as follows.

Theorem 4.29

(
Approximation of the dynamics by a quantum operator
)
.

For any
t\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle e^{tX}

\displaystyle\approx\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\right).

(4.56)

4.5.1
Continuity theorem, boundness estimates with respect to time

Remark 4.30
.

In the rest of this section we take any
K\in\mathbb{N}
and fix
it. Then we choose a weight function
W
with exponent
R
such
that (
4.52
) holds true and consider symbols
a\in C^{\beta}\left(M;L\left(\mathcal{F}_{\left[0,K\right]}\left(E_{s}\right)\right)\right)
.

Theorem 4.31

(continuity theorem for F.I.O.)
.

There exists
C>0
, such that for any
symbol
a\in C^{\beta}\left(M;L\left(\mathcal{F}_{\left[0,K\right]}\left(E_{s}\right)\right)\right)

and any
t\in\mathbb{R}
,

\left\|\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{a}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\leq C\left\|e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{a}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(\mathcal{F}\right)}.

(4.57)

Démonstration
.

We first give an equivalent expression for the operator as we already
saw in Theorem
3.19
. This expression is more complicated
in appearance but more useful for the proof to work uniformly w.r.t.
time
t
.

Lemma 4.32

(Equivalent expression)
.

We have

\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{a}\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:def_Op_a})}{=}\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}^{\Delta}\Upsilon_{t}^{1/2}e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{a}\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}\approx\mathcal{T}_{\Sigma}^{\Delta}\left(\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(d\tilde{\phi}_{T\Sigma}^{t}\right)\otimes\left(\mathcal{B}_{N_{s},N_{u}}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{a}\right)\mathcal{B}_{N_{s},N_{u}}^{\dagger}\right)\right)\mathcal{T}_{\Sigma}

(4.58)

Démonstration
.

We have already obtained

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}^{\Delta}\,\Upsilon_{t}^{1/2}e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:exp_tX_N-1},\ref{eq:exp_tX_Lamda})}{\approx}\mathcal{T}_{\Sigma}^{\Delta}\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\left(d\tilde{\phi}^{t}\right)_{/\Sigma}\right)\mathcal{T}_{\Sigma}

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:fact})}{=}\mathcal{T}_{\Sigma}^{\Delta}\left(\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(d\tilde{\phi}_{T\Sigma}^{t}\right)\otimes_{\Sigma}\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(d\tilde{\phi}_{N}^{t}\right)\right)\mathcal{T}_{\Sigma}

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:Op_tilde_dPhi_N})}{=}\mathcal{T}_{\Sigma}^{\Delta}\left(\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(d\tilde{\phi}_{T\Sigma}^{t}\right)\otimes\left(\mathcal{B}_{N_{s},N_{u}}e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\mathcal{B}_{N_{s},N_{u}}^{\dagger}\right)\right)\mathcal{T}_{\Sigma}.

By inserting the bundle map
\tilde{a}
we obtain (
4.58
).
∎

We have defined
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(N_{s}\right)
in
(
4.51
). Similarly, we can define
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(N\right):=L^{2}\left(N;\mathcal{W}^{2}\right)

with the weight
\mathcal{W}
on
N
in (
4.49
) and
define
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(T_{\Sigma}T^{*}M\right)
by
the weight
\mathcal{W}
extended trivially to
T_{\Sigma}T^{*}M\underset{(\ref{eq:decomp_K_K0_N})}{=}T\Sigma\oplus N

by
\mathcal{W}\left(u,v\right)=\mathcal{W}\left(v\right)
for any

u\in T_{\rho}\Sigma
,
v\in N_{\rho}
.

Lemma 4.33
.

\exists C>0
,

\left\|\mathcal{T}_{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(T_{\Sigma}T^{*}M\right)}\leq C,\qquad\left\|\mathcal{T}_{\Sigma}^{\Delta}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(T_{\Sigma}T^{*}M\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\leq C.

(4.59)

Démonstration
.

Recall the expression
\mathcal{T}_{\Sigma}\underset{(\ref{eq:def_T_Sigma})}{=}\chi_{\Sigma}^{\mu}r_{/\Sigma}\left(\widetilde{\exp^{\circ}}\right)\mathcal{T}

where, from (
4.50
), the operator
\chi_{\Sigma}^{\mu}r_{/\Sigma}\left(\widetilde{\exp^{\circ}}\right)

satisfies
\exists C>0
,

\left\|\chi_{\Sigma}^{\mu}r_{/\Sigma}\left(\widetilde{\exp^{\circ}}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}\left(T^{*}M;W\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(T_{\Sigma}T^{*}M\right)}\leq C.

Since
\mathcal{T}:\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)\rightarrow L^{2}\left(T^{*}M;W\right)

is an isometry, we deduce the first bound in (
4.59
).
Similarly from the expression
\mathcal{T}_{\Sigma}^{\Delta}\underset{(\ref{eq:def_T_sigma_Delta})}{=}\mathcal{T}^{\dagger}\widetilde{\left(\exp_{N}^{-1}\right)^{\circ}}r_{N}\chi_{\Sigma}^{\mu}
,
we deduce the second claim.
∎

Lemma 4.34
.

We have

\left\|\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(d\tilde{\phi}_{T\Sigma}^{t}\right)\otimes\left(\mathcal{B}_{N_{s},N_{u}}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{a}\right)\mathcal{B}_{N_{s},N_{u}}^{\dagger}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(T_{\Sigma}T^{*}M\right)}=\left\|e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{a}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(\mathcal{F}\right)}.

(4.60)

Démonstration
.

From the definition of the norms above, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(d\tilde{\phi}_{T\Sigma}^{t}\right)\otimes\left(\mathcal{B}_{N_{s},N_{u}}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{a}\right)\mathcal{B}_{N_{s},N_{u}}^{\dagger}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(T_{\Sigma}T^{*}M\right)}

\displaystyle=\left\|\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(d\tilde{\phi}_{T\Sigma}^{t}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\left(\mathcal{B}_{N_{s},N_{u}}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{a}\right)\mathcal{B}_{N_{s},N_{u}}^{\dagger}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(N\right)}

\displaystyle=\left\|e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{a}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(\mathcal{F}\right)}

∎

We come back to the proof of Theorem
4.31
. Then

\left\|\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{a}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\underset{(\ref{eq:equiv},\ref{eq:bound_T_check-1},\ref{eq:equiv2})}{\leq}C\left\|e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{a}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(\mathcal{F}\right)}

giving (
4.57
).
∎

Here are some example of Theorem
4.31
that we
will use later.

Corollary 4.35
.

We have
\forall\epsilon>0,\exists C_{K,\epsilon}>0
,
\forall t\geq 0
,
(positive time)

\left\|\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{\geq\left(K+1\right)}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\leq C_{K,\epsilon}e^{t\left(\gamma_{K+1}^{+}+\epsilon\right)}

(4.61)

and for any
0\leq k_{1}\leq k_{2}\leq K
,
\forall\epsilon>0,\exists C_{K,\epsilon}>0
,
\forall t\geq 0
,

\left\|\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{\left[k_{1},k_{2}\right]}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\leq C_{K,\epsilon}e^{t\left(\gamma_{k_{1}}^{+}+\epsilon\right)}.

(4.62)

Also
\forall t\leq 0
, (negative time)

\left\|\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{\left[k_{1},k_{2}\right]}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\leq C_{K,\epsilon}e^{t\left(\gamma_{k_{2}}^{-}-\epsilon\right)}.

(4.63)

Démonstration
.

To get (
4.61
) we have that
\exists C,\forall\epsilon>0,\exists C_{K,\epsilon}>0
,
\forall t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{\geq\left(K+1\right)}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:bound_OP_Sigma})}{\leq}C\left\|e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{\geq\left(K+1\right)}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(\mathcal{F}\right)}

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:norm_Lw_out})}{\leq}C_{K,\epsilon}e^{\left(\gamma_{K+1}^{+}+\epsilon\right)t}.

To get (
4.62
), in the equations below we will use (*)
that
\gamma_{k+1}^{+}\leq\gamma_{k}^{+},\forall k\in\mathbb{N}
.
We have that
\exists C,\forall\epsilon>0,\exists C_{K},C_{K,\epsilon}>0
,
\forall t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{\left[k_{1},k_{2}\right]}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:bound_OP_Sigma})}{\leq}C\left\|e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{\left[k_{1},k_{2}\right]}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(\mathcal{F}\right)}\leq C\left\|e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}_{\left[k_{1},k_{2}\right]}}}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}_{\left[k_{1},k_{2}\right]}\right)}

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:bound-2})}{\leq}C_{K,\epsilon}\max_{k\in\left[k_{1},k_{2}\right]}e^{t\left(\gamma_{k}^{+}+\epsilon\right)}\underset{(*)}{=}C_{K,\epsilon}e^{t\left(\gamma_{k_{1}}^{+}+\epsilon\right)}

Finally to get (
4.63
), we repeat the same first lines
as above and write
\forall\epsilon>0,\exists C_{K,\epsilon}>0
,
\forall t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{-tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{\left[k_{1},k_{2}\right]}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\leq

\displaystyle C\left\|e^{-tX_{\mathcal{F}_{\left[k_{1},k_{2}\right]}}}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}_{\left[k_{1},k_{2}\right]}\right)}

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:bound-2})}{\leq}C_{K,\epsilon}\max_{k\in\left[k_{1},k_{2}\right]}e^{-t\left(\gamma_{k}^{-}-\epsilon\right)}\underset{(*)}{=}

\displaystyle C_{K,\epsilon}e^{-t\left(\gamma_{k_{2}}^{-}-\epsilon\right)}.

∎

Remark 4.36
.

In particular, taking
t=0
we get for any
k\in\left[0,K\right]
,

\left\|\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{T}_{k}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\underset{(\ref{eq:bound-4-1})}{\leq}C_{K}.

(4.64)

4.5.2
Composition formula

Recall
\mathcal{F}_{\left[0,K\right]}
defined in (
4.44
).
Recall that the operators
\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}},\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}^{\Delta}

in Definition
3.14
depends on the exponent
0<\mu<1

introduced in the cut-off (
3.34
). Consequently the
quantization
\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(.\right)
defined in (
4.55
)
also depends on
\mu
. Recall that
\beta
is the Hölder exponent
of
E_{u},E_{s}
in (
4.3
).

Theorem 4.37

(
Composition formula
)
.

Take
\mu<\beta
. For
any symbols
a,b\in C^{\beta}\left(M;L\left(\mathcal{F}_{\left[0,K\right]}\left(E_{s}\right)\right)\right)
,
any
t,t^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{a}\right)\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{t^{\prime}X_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{b}\right)\approx\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{a}e^{t^{\prime}X_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{b}\right).

(4.65)

(where the right hand side term has the factor
\Upsilon_{t+t^{\prime}}^{1/2}

in its Definition (
4.55
)).

Démonstration
.

For convenience, we first naturally extend the Definition
3.12

of the equivalence
\approx
in (
3.32
) to operators
on
\mathcal{S}\left(T^{*}M\right)
and also
C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}\right)

(instead of
C^{\infty}\left(M\right)
only). We define

\displaystyle\check{\mathcal{T}}_{N_{s}}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{B}_{N_{s},N_{u}}^{\dagger}\chi_{\Sigma}^{\mu}\widetilde{\left(\exp_{N}\right)^{\circ}}\quad:\mathcal{S}\left(T^{*}M\right)\rightarrow C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}\right),

(4.66)

\displaystyle\check{\mathcal{T}}_{N_{s}}^{\Delta}

\displaystyle:=\widetilde{\left(\exp_{N}^{-1}\right)^{\circ}}\chi_{\Sigma}^{\mu}\mathcal{B}_{N_{s},N_{u}}\quad:C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}\right)\rightarrow C\left(T^{*}M\right).

These operators enter in the following expressions

\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}\underset{(\ref{eq:def_T_Ns})}{=}\check{\mathcal{T}}_{N_{s}}\mathcal{T},\qquad\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}^{\Delta}\underset{(\ref{eq:def_T_Ns_Delta})}{=}\mathcal{T}^{\dagger}\check{\mathcal{T}}_{N_{s}}^{\Delta}.

(4.67)

For an operators
A:C\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}\right)\rightarrow C\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}\right)
,
we denote

\tilde{\tilde{A}}:=\check{\mathcal{T}}_{N_{s}}^{\Delta}A\check{\mathcal{T}}_{N_{s}}:\mathcal{S}\left(T^{*}M\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\left(T^{*}M\right)

(4.68)

.

Definition 4.38
.

For two operators
A,B:\mathcal{S}\left(T^{*}M\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\left(T^{*}M\right)

we write

A\approx B

(4.69)

if there exists
m<0
,
t\in\mathbb{R}
, for any
N>0
, any
\sigma>0
,
there exists a constant
C_{\sigma,N,t}>0
such that for any
\rho,\rho^{\prime}\in T^{*}M
,

\left|\langle\delta_{\rho^{\prime}}|\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\left(A-B\right)\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\delta_{\rho}\rangle\right|\leq C_{\sigma,N,t}\left\langle\mathrm{dist}_{g}\left(\rho^{\prime},\tilde{\phi}^{t}\left(\rho\right)\right)\right\rangle^{-N}\left\langle\left|\rho\right|\right\rangle^{m}.

For two operators
A,B:C\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}\right)\rightarrow C\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}\right)

we write
A\approx B
if
\tilde{\tilde{A}}\approx\tilde{\tilde{B}}
.

Using operators
\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}},\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}^{\Delta}

defined in (
4.34
,
4.35
), let

\mathcal{P}_{N_{s}}:=\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}^{\Delta}\quad:C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}\right)\rightarrow C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}\right).

(4.70)

Lemma 4.39
.

We have

\mathcal{P}_{N_{s}}^{2}\approx\mathcal{P}_{N_{s}},\qquad\mathcal{P}_{N_{s}}\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}\approx\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}.

(4.71)

Démonstration
.

Taking
t=0
in (
4.36
) gives

\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}^{\Delta}\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}\approx\mathrm{Id}.

(4.72)

Then

\mathcal{P}_{N_{s}}^{2}\underset{(\ref{eq:def_P_Ns})}{=}\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}\left(\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}^{\Delta}\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}\right)\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}^{\Delta}\underset{(\ref{eq:equiv_Id})}{\approx}\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}^{\Delta}\underset{(\ref{eq:def_P_Ns})}{=}\mathcal{P}_{N_{s}}.

\mathcal{P}_{N_{s}}\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}\underset{(\ref{eq:def_P_Ns})}{=}\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}\left(\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}^{\Delta}\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:equiv_Id})}{\approx}\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}.

∎

We show the following Lemma (similar to
[
20
, Lemma 4.47]
)

Lemma 4.40

(Basic Lemma)
.

For any symbol
a\in C^{\beta}\left(M;L\left(\mathcal{F}_{\left[0,K\right]}\left(E_{s}\right)\right)\right)
,
we have for
\mu<\beta
that

\left[\tilde{a},\mathcal{P}_{N_{s}}\right]\approx 0.

(4.73)

Démonstration
.

Observe that

\check{\mathcal{T}}_{N_{s}}\check{\mathcal{T}}_{N_{s}}^{\Delta}\underset{(\ref{eq:def_T_check_Ns})}{\approx}\mathrm{Id},\qquad\check{\mathcal{T}}_{N_{s}}^{\Delta}\check{\mathcal{T}}_{N_{s}}\underset{(\ref{eq:def_T_check_Ns})}{\approx}\mathrm{\mathcal{P}}_{N}.

(4.74)

The notation
\approx
has been defined for operators. This definition
can been understood also for Schwartz kernel of operators lifted on

T^{*}M
and we use it below. We have
\forall N\geq 0,\exists C_{N}>0,\forall\rho,\rho^{\prime}\in T^{*}M
,

\displaystyle\left|\langle\delta_{\rho^{\prime}}|\tilde{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}}_{N_{s}}\delta_{\rho}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:def_tilde2})}{=}\left|\langle\delta_{\rho^{\prime}}|\check{\mathcal{T}}_{N_{s}}^{\Delta}\mathcal{P}_{N_{s}}\check{\mathcal{T}}_{N_{s}}\delta_{\rho}\rangle\right|\underset{(\ref{eq:def_P_Ns},\ref{eq:_expressions})}{=}\left|\langle\delta_{\rho^{\prime}}|\check{\mathcal{T}}_{N_{s}}^{\Delta}\check{\mathcal{T}}_{N_{s}}\mathcal{T}\mathcal{T}^{\dagger}\check{\mathcal{T}}_{N_{s}}^{\Delta}\check{\mathcal{T}}_{N_{s}}\delta_{\rho}\rangle\right|

(4.75)

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:approx_Id},\ref{eq:def_P_wave_packet_projector})}{\approx}\left|\langle\delta_{\rho^{\prime}}|\mathcal{P}\delta_{\rho}\rangle\right|\underset{(\ref{eq:estimate_Bergman_kernel})}{\leq}C_{N}\left\langle\mathrm{dist}_{g}\left(\rho^{\prime},\rho\right)\right\rangle^{-N}.

Then

\displaystyle\left|\langle\delta_{\rho^{\prime}}|\check{\mathcal{T}}_{N_{s}}^{\Delta}\left[\tilde{a},\mathcal{P}_{N_{s}}\right]\check{\mathcal{T}}_{N_{s}}\delta_{\rho}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:approx_Id},\ref{eq:def_tilde2})}{\approx}\left|\langle\delta_{\rho^{\prime}}|\left[\tilde{\tilde{a}},\tilde{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}}_{N_{s}}\right]\delta_{\rho}\rangle\right|

(4.76)

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{\rho^{\prime\prime}\in T^{*}M}\langle\delta_{\rho^{\prime}}|\tilde{\tilde{a}}\delta_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\langle\delta_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}|\tilde{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}}_{N_{s}}\delta_{\rho}\rangle-\langle\delta_{\rho^{\prime}}|\tilde{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}}_{N_{s}}\delta_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\langle\delta_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}|\tilde{\tilde{a}}\delta_{\rho}\rangle\right|.

The bundle
E_{s}
is Hölder continuous with some exponent
\beta>0

and consequently
\tilde{a}
satisfies the following property (called
slowly varying property in
[
20
, def 4.40]
),
using any local trivialization, for any
\varrho,\varrho^{\prime}\in\Sigma
,
setting
x=\pi\left(\varrho\right),x^{\prime}=\pi\left(\varrho^{\prime}\right)\in M
,
we assume
\omega=\boldsymbol{\omega}\left(\varrho\right)\asymp\boldsymbol{\omega}\left(\varrho^{\prime}\right)
,
we use
\left|x^{\prime}-x\right|\underset{(\ref{eq:metric_g_tilde_in_coordinates})}{\lesssim}\left\langle\omega\right\rangle^{-1/2}\mathrm{dist}_{g}\left(\varrho^{\prime},\varrho\right)
,
and we have

\left\|\tilde{a}\left(\varrho^{\prime}\right)-\tilde{a}\left(\varrho\right)\right\|\asymp\left\|a\left(x^{\prime}\right)-a\left(x\right)\right\|\lesssim\left|x^{\prime}-x\right|^{\beta}\asymp\left\langle\omega\right\rangle^{-\beta/2}\left(\mathrm{dist}_{g}\left(\varrho^{\prime},\varrho\right)\right)^{\beta}.

(4.77)

Hence for
\rho,\rho^{\prime}\in T^{*}M
, due to the cutoff
\chi_{\Sigma}^{\mu}

in (
3.44
) at distance
\left\langle\omega\right\rangle^{\mu/2}

from
\Sigma
, writing
\rho\equiv\left(\varrho,v\right)\in T^{*}M

with
\varrho\in\Sigma
,
v\in N_{\varrho}
,
\exp_{N}\left(v\right)=\rho,

we have for
\mathrm{dist}_{g}\left(\rho^{\prime},\rho\right)\lesssim 1
and

\mathrm{dist}_{g}\left(\rho^{\prime\prime},\rho\right)\lesssim 1
that

\left|\langle\delta_{\rho^{\prime}}|\tilde{\tilde{a}}\delta_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}\rangle-\langle\delta_{\rho^{\prime\prime}}|\tilde{\tilde{a}}\delta_{\rho}\rangle\right|\leq\left\langle\omega\right\rangle^{\mu/2}\left|\tilde{a}\left(\varrho^{\prime}\right)-\tilde{a}\left(\varrho\right)\right|\underset{(\ref{eq:ineq2})}{\leq}\left\langle\omega\right\rangle^{\mu/2}\left\langle\omega\right\rangle^{-\beta/2}.

(4.78)

We deduce

\left|\langle\delta_{\rho^{\prime}}|\check{\mathcal{T}}_{N_{s}}^{\Delta}\left[\tilde{a},\mathcal{P}_{N_{s}}\right]\check{\mathcal{T}}_{N_{s}}\delta_{\rho}\rangle\right|\underset{(\ref{eq:commut-a_P_Ns},\ref{eq:kernel_P_tild2})}{\leq}C\left\langle\omega\right\rangle^{-\left(\beta-\mu\right)/2}C_{N}\left\langle\mathrm{dist}_{g}\left(\rho^{\prime},\rho\right)\right\rangle^{-N}.

From Definition
4.38
, we get that
\left[\tilde{a},\mathcal{P}_{N_{s}}\right]\approx 0

if
\beta>\mu
.
∎

Observe that

\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{t^{\prime}X_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:expression})}{\approx}e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}e^{t^{\prime}X_{\mathcal{F}}}=e^{\left(t+t^{\prime}\right)X_{\mathcal{F}}}\underset{(\ref{eq:expression})}{\approx}\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{\left(t+t^{\prime}\right)X_{\mathcal{F}}}\right).

(4.79)

Also

\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{a}\right)\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{b}\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:def_Op_a},\ref{eq:def_P_Ns})}{=}\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}^{\Delta}\tilde{a}\mathcal{P}_{N_{s}}\tilde{b}\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}\underset{(\ref{eq:commut-a_P_Ns},\ref{eq:equiv6})}{\approx}\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}^{\Delta}\tilde{a}\tilde{b}\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}=\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{a}\tilde{b}\right).

(4.80)

and similarly

\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{a}\right)\approx\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{a}\right)

(4.81)

\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{a}\right)\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)\approx\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{a}e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\right).

(4.82)

From these relations, we deduce (
4.65
) as follows.
We set
\tilde{a}^{\prime}=e^{-t^{\prime}X_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{a}e^{t^{\prime}X_{\mathcal{F}}}
.

\displaystyle\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{a}\right)\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{t^{\prime}X_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{b}\right)

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:equiv1},\ref{eq:equiv3},\ref{eq:equiv4})}{\approx}\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{t^{\prime}X_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{a}^{\prime}\tilde{b}\right)

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:equiv5})}{\approx}\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}e^{t^{\prime}X_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{a}^{\prime}\tilde{b}\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:equiv1},\ref{eq:equiv3},\ref{eq:equiv4})}{\approx}\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{a}e^{t^{\prime}X_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{b}\right).

∎

The next corollary follows as special cases of (
4.65
).

Corollary 4.41
.

Take
\mu<\beta
. We have

•

Egorov formula:
for a symbol
a\in C^{\beta}\left(M;\mathrm{End}\left(\mathcal{F}_{\left[0,K\right]}\left(E_{s}\right)\right)\right),

t\in\mathbb{R}
,

\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{a}\right)\approx\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{t\mathrm{ad}_{X_{\mathcal{F}}}}\tilde{a}\right)\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\right).

(4.83)

with the natural action
e^{t\mathrm{ad}_{X_{\mathcal{F}}}}\tilde{a}=e^{t\left[X_{\mathcal{F}},.\right]}\tilde{a}:=e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{a}e^{-tX_{\mathcal{F}}}
.

•

Approximate projectors
: for any
k,k^{\prime}\leq K
,

\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{T}_{k}\right)\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{T}_{k^{\prime}}\right)\approx\delta_{k=k^{\prime}}\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{T}_{k}\right).

(4.84)

•

Decomposition of the dynamics in components
: for any
k,k^{\prime}\leq K
,

\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{k}\right)\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{t^{\prime}X_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{k^{\prime}}\right)\approx\delta_{k=k^{\prime}}\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{\left(t+t^{\prime}\right)X_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{k}\right).

(4.85)

Remark 4.42
.

The algebraic structure of (
4.85
) will manifest itself later
in the band structure of the Ruelle spectrum.

Démonstration
.

To get (
4.83
), we write

\displaystyle\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{a}\right)

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:compos_operators})}{\approx}\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{a}\right)=\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\left(e^{t\mathrm{ad}_{X_{\mathcal{F}}}}\tilde{a}\right)e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:compos_operators})}{\approx}\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{t\mathrm{ad}_{X_{\mathcal{F}}}}\tilde{a}\right)\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)

To get (
4.84
), we write

\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{T}_{k}\right)\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{T}_{k^{\prime}}\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:compos_operators})}{\approx}\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{T}_{k}\tilde{T}_{k^{\prime}}\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:delta_T})}{=}\delta_{k=k^{\prime}}\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{T}_{k}\right).

To get (
4.85
), we write

\displaystyle\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{k}\right)\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{t^{\prime}X_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{k^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:compos_operators})}{\approx}\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{k}e^{t^{\prime}X_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{k^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:commut_T})}{=}\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}e^{t^{\prime}X_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{k}\tilde{T}_{k^{\prime}}\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:delta_T})}{=}\delta_{k=k^{\prime}}\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{\left(t+t^{\prime}\right)X_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{k}\right).

∎

4.5.3
Trace formula

We present some trace formula for PDO (Pseudo Differential Operators),
i.e. FIO (Fourier Integral Operators) at
t=0
. We will use this
formula in section
7.1
. It is also
possible to express
\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\left[\omega,\omega^{\prime}\right]}e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{a}\right)\right)

for
t\neq 0
, but we don’t need it in this paper.

Theorem 4.43

(Trace formula)
.

Take
\mu<\beta
. There exists
C>0
,
for any
\omega\geq\delta\geq 1
, for any symbol
a\in C^{\beta}\left(M;L\left(\mathcal{F}_{\left[0,K\right]}\left(E_{s}\right)\right)\right)
,

\left\|\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}\left(\rho\right)\in\left[\omega-\delta,\omega+\delta\right]}\tilde{a}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}}\leq C\omega^{d}\delta.

(4.86)

\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}\left(\rho\right)\in\left[\omega-\delta,\omega+\delta\right]}\tilde{a}\right)\right)=\left(\frac{\omega^{d}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d+1}}\left(2\delta\right)\right)\int_{M}\mathrm{Tr}\left(a\left(m\right)\right)dm+r\left(\omega,\delta\right),

(4.87)

with remainder

\left|r\left(\omega,\delta\right)\right|\leq C\omega^{d}\delta\left(\left|\omega\right|^{-\left(\beta-\mu\right)/2}+\frac{\delta}{\omega}\right)+C\omega^{d}.

Remark 4.44
.

Later in (
7.39
) we will use (
4.87
)
as follows. For any small
c^{\prime}>0
we will take
\delta\gg 1
and
\omega\gg\delta

large enough so that the remainder
r\left(\omega,\delta\right)

is smaller than
c^{\prime}\omega^{d}\delta
, i.e. negligible w.r.t. the
first term.

Démonstration
.

Since the symbol
\boldsymbol{1}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}\left(\rho\right)\in\left[\omega-\delta,\omega+\delta\right]}\tilde{a}

is fiber-wise, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}\left(\rho\right)\in\left[\omega-\delta,\omega+\delta\right]}\tilde{a}\right)

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:def_Op_a})}{=}\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}^{\Delta}\boldsymbol{1}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}\left(\rho\right)\in\left[\omega-\delta,\omega+\delta\right]}\tilde{a}\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\rho\in\Sigma}A\left(\rho\right)\boldsymbol{1}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}\left(\rho\right)\in\left[\omega-\delta,\omega+\delta\right]}\tilde{a}\left(\rho\right)B\left(\rho\right)\frac{d\rho}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d+1}}

(4.88)

with for
\rho\in\Sigma
, the finite dimensional vector space
\mathcal{F}_{\left[0,K\right]}\left(N_{s}\left(\rho\right)\right)

and the finite rank linear operators

\displaystyle A\left(\rho\right)

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}^{\Delta}\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}\left(\rho\right)\quad:\mathcal{F}_{\left[0,K\right]}\left(N_{s}\left(\rho\right)\right)\rightarrow C^{\infty}\left(M\right)

(4.89)

\displaystyle B\left(\rho\right)

\displaystyle:=\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}\left(\rho\right)\mathcal{T}_{N_{s}}\quad:C^{\infty}\left(M\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{F}_{\left[0,K\right]}\left(N_{s}\left(\rho\right)\right)

(4.90)

Where
\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}\left(\rho\right)
is the bundle
map projector onto
\mathcal{F}_{\left[0,K\right]}\left(N_{s}\left(\rho\right)\right)
,
defined in (
4.41
). We have
\exists C>0,\forall\rho\in\Sigma,

\left\|A\left(\rho\right)\right\|\leq C,\qquad\left\|B\left(\rho\right)\right\|\leq C.

and

\left\|\boldsymbol{1}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}\left(\rho\right)\in\left[\omega-\delta,\omega+\delta\right]}\tilde{a}\left(\rho\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}}\leq C

(4.91)

For
\omega\geq\delta>0
, we have

\int_{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{1}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}\left(\rho\right)\in\left[\omega-\delta,\omega+\delta\right]}d\rho\underset{(\ref{eq:dvol_E0*})}{=}\mathrm{Vol}\left(M\right)\int_{\omega-\delta}^{\omega+\delta}\omega^{\prime d}d\omega^{\prime}=\mathrm{Vol}\left(M\right)\omega^{d}\left(2\delta\right)\left(1+O\left(\frac{\delta}{\omega}\right)\right).

(4.92)

Hence

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}\left(\rho\right)\in\left[\omega-\delta,\omega+\delta\right]}\tilde{a}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}}

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:int1})}{\leq}C\int_{\Sigma}\left\|\boldsymbol{1}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}\left(\rho\right)\in\left[\omega-\delta,\omega+\delta\right]}\tilde{a}\left(\rho\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}}\frac{d\rho}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d+1}}

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:C},\ref{eq:int_sig})}{\leq}C\omega^{d}\delta,

giving (
4.86
). Then

\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}\left(\rho\right)\in\left[\omega-\delta,\omega+\delta\right]}\tilde{a}\right)\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:int1})}{=}\int_{\Sigma}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}\left(\rho\right)\in\left[\omega-\delta,\omega+\delta\right]}\tilde{a}\left(\rho\right)B\left(\rho\right)A\left(\rho\right)\right)\frac{d\rho}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d+1}},

where

\displaystyle B\left(\rho\right)A\left(\rho\right)

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:def_A-1},\ref{eq:def_B-2},\ref{eq:def_T_Ns_Delta},\ref{eq:def_T_Ns})}{=}\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}\left(\rho\right)\mathcal{B}_{N_{s},N_{u}}^{\dagger}\chi_{\Sigma}^{\mu}\widetilde{\left(\exp_{N}\right)^{\circ}}\mathcal{T}\mathcal{T}^{\dagger}\widetilde{\left(\exp_{N}^{-1}\right)^{\circ}}\chi_{\Sigma}^{\mu}\mathcal{B}_{N_{s},N_{u}}\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}\left(\rho\right)

\displaystyle=\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}\left(\rho\right)+O\left(\left\langle\omega\right\rangle^{-\left(\beta-\mu\right)/2}\right),

where
\boldsymbol{\omega}\left(\rho\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}\left(\beta-\mu\right)}

comes from Hölder irregularity as in (
4.78
). Hence

\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}\left(\rho\right)\in\left[\omega-\delta,\omega+\delta\right]}\tilde{a}\right)\right)=\int_{\Sigma}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}\left(\rho\right)\in\left[\omega-\delta,\omega+\delta\right]}\tilde{a}\left(\rho\right)\right)\frac{d\rho}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d+1}}+R

with remainder

\displaystyle\left|R\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{1}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}\left(\rho\right)\in\left[\omega-\delta,\omega+\delta\right]}\boldsymbol{\omega}\left(\rho\right)^{-\left(\beta-\mu\right)/2}d\rho+C\omega^{d}

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:int_sig})}{\leq}C\omega^{d}\delta\omega^{-\left(\beta-\mu\right)/2}+C\omega^{d}.

C\omega^{d}
is due to sharp cutoff in frequency, and main term

\int_{\Sigma}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\boldsymbol{\omega}\left(\rho\right)\in\left[\omega-\delta,\omega+\delta\right]}\tilde{a}\left(\rho\right)\right)\frac{d\rho}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d+1}}\underset{(\ref{eq:dvol_E0*},\ref{eq:int_sig})}{=}\frac{\omega^{d}\left(2\delta\right)}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d+1}}\left(1+O\left(\frac{\delta}{\omega}\right)\right)\int_{M}\mathrm{Tr}\left(a\left(m\right)\right)dm.

∎

We have obtained (
4.87
).

5
Proof of Theorem
1.7

(band spectrum)

We will follow the strategy presented in the proof of
[
20
, Lemma 5.16]
.
Let
K\in\mathbb{N}
,
\epsilon>0
be fixed and consider
z\in\mathbb{C}

in the spectral plane such that

\gamma_{K+1}^{+}+\epsilon<\mathrm{Re}\left(z\right)<\gamma_{K}^{-}-\epsilon.

(5.1)

We put

\omega=\mathrm{Im}\left(z\right).

(5.2)

Let
\delta>0
. We consider the frequency intervals

J_{\omega}:=\left[\omega-1,\omega+1\right],\qquad J^{\prime}_{\omega,\delta}:=\left[\omega-1-\delta,\omega+1+\delta\right],

(5.3)

so that
J_{\omega}\subset J^{\prime}_{\omega,\delta}
. Let
\sigma>0
.
We consider the following partition of the cotangent bundle

T^{*}M=\Omega_{0}\cup\Omega_{1}\cup\Omega_{2}

(5.4)

with, as in (
3.30
),

\Omega_{0}:=\left\{\rho\in T^{*}M\,\mid\quad\left\|\rho_{u}+\rho_{s}\right\|_{g_{\rho}}\leq\sigma\text{ and }\boldsymbol{\omega}\left(\rho\right)\in J^{\prime}_{\omega,\delta}\right\},

(5.5)

\Omega_{1}:=\left\{\rho\in T^{*}M\,\mid\quad\left\|\rho_{u}+\rho_{s}\right\|_{g_{\rho}}>\sigma\text{ and }\boldsymbol{\omega}\left(\rho\right)\in J^{\prime}_{\omega,\delta}\right\},

(5.6)

\Omega_{2}:=\left\{\rho\in T^{*}M\,\mid\quad\boldsymbol{\omega}\left(\rho\right)\notin J^{\prime}_{\omega,\delta}\right\}.

For
j=0,1,2
, we define
\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{j}}:T^{*}M\rightarrow\left[0,1\right]

as being the characteristic function of the set
\Omega_{j}
above
and as in (
3.31
), we set

\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{j}}\right):=\mathcal{T}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{j}}\mathcal{T}\quad:C^{\infty}\left(M\right)\rightarrow C^{\infty}\left(M\right),

giving a resolution of identity

\displaystyle\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\underset{(\ref{eq:partition})}{=}

\displaystyle\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{0}}\right)+\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{1}}\right)+\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(T_{\left[0,K\right]}\right)\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{0}}\right)+\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(T_{\geq\left(K+1\right)}\right)\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{0}}\right)+\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{1}}\right)+\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{2}}\right)

(5.7)

\displaystyle+\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{F}}\right)\right)\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{0}}\right).

(5.8)

We will now construct the resolvent of
X
at
z
from the contribution
of each term in (
5.7
), except for the last term. For
this last term, we observe that

\mathrm{Id}-\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{F}}\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:expression})}{\approx}0.

Since
\sigma
is fixed in (
5.5
) we have that

\lim_{\left|\omega\right|\rightarrow\infty}\left\|\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{F}}\right)\right)\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{0}}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\underset{(\ref{eq:result_of_Shur})}{=}0.

(5.9)

5.1
Contribution of
\Omega_{0}

Recall that
\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{0}}\right)

defined from
\Omega_{0}
in (
5.5
) depends
on
\omega
defined in (
5.2
).

Lemma 5.1
.

We have (for negative time)
\forall\epsilon>0,\exists C_{\epsilon}>0
,
\forall t\leq 0
,

\exists\omega_{t}>0
,
\forall\omega>\omega_{t}
,

\left\|e^{tX}\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}\right)\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{0}}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\leq C_{\epsilon}e^{t\left(\gamma_{K}^{-}-\epsilon\right)},

(5.10)

and (for positive time)
\forall\epsilon>0,\exists C_{\epsilon}>0
,
\forall t\geq 0
,

\exists\omega_{t}>0
,
\forall\omega>\omega_{t}
,

\left\|e^{tX_{F}}\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}\right)\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{0}}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\leq C_{\epsilon}e^{t\left(\gamma_{0}^{+}+\epsilon\right)},

(5.11)

\left\|e^{tX}\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{T}_{\geq\left(K+1\right)}\right)\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{0}}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\leq C_{\epsilon}e^{t\left(\gamma_{K+1}^{+}+\epsilon\right)}.

(5.12)

Démonstration
.

Let
t\leq 0
. We have

\displaystyle e^{tX}\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:expression})}{\approx}

\displaystyle\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}\right)

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:compos_operators})}{\approx}

\displaystyle\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}\right)

(5.13)

Now we use the cut-off in frequency
\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{0}}\right)
.
We have

\left\|\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{0}}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\leq C,

(5.14)

and

\displaystyle\left\|e^{tX}\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}\right)\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{0}}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\underset{(\ref{eq:ineq},\ref{eq:result_of_Shur})}{\leq}

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}\right)\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{0}}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}+r\left(\omega\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\left\|\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{0}}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}+r\left(\omega\right)

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:bound-4},\ref{eq:Op_Omega0_bounded})}{\leq}

\displaystyle C_{K,\epsilon^{\prime}}e^{t\left(\gamma_{K}^{-}-\epsilon^{\prime}\right)}+r\left(\omega\right),

with some function
r\left(\omega\right)
that satisfies
\lim_{\left|\omega\right|\rightarrow\infty}r\left(\omega\right)=0
.
This gives (
5.10
). Similarly, using (
4.62
)
we get (
5.11
) and using (
4.61
) we get (
5.12
).
∎

Approximate resolvent
R_{\mathrm{in}}^{\left(0\right)}\left(z\right)

and
R_{\mathrm{out}}^{\left(0\right)}\left(z\right)
:

For
T\geq 0
we define

R_{\mathrm{in}}^{\left(0\right)}\left(z\right):=-\left(\int_{-T}^{0}e^{-tz}e^{tX}dt\right)\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}\right)\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{0}}\right).

(5.15)

Then

\displaystyle\left\|R_{\mathrm{in}}^{\left(0\right)}\left(z\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\underset{(\ref{eq:def_R0},\ref{eq:exp-tX})}{\leq}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}e^{t\mathrm{Re}\left(z\right)}C_{\epsilon^{\prime}}e^{-t\left(\gamma_{K}^{-}-\epsilon^{\prime}\right)}dt\underset{(\ref{eq:Re_z})}{\leq}C_{\epsilon^{\prime}}\int_{0}^{T}e^{-\left(\epsilon-\epsilon^{\prime}\right)t}dt\leq C_{\epsilon}.

(5.16)

where we have taken
\epsilon^{\prime}=\frac{1}{2}\epsilon
. We also have

\displaystyle\left(z-X\right)R_{\mathrm{in}}^{\left(0\right)}\left(z\right)

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:def_R0})}{=}\left(\mathrm{Id}-e^{Tz}e^{-TX}\right)\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}\right)\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{0}}\right)

and

\displaystyle\left\|\left(z-X\right)R_{\mathrm{in}}^{\left(0\right)}\left(z\right)-\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}\right)\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{0}}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:exp-tX})}{\leq}e^{T\mathrm{Re}\left(z\right)}C_{\epsilon^{\prime}}e^{-T\left(\gamma_{K}^{-}-\epsilon^{\prime}\right)}

(5.17)

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:Re_z})}{\leq}C_{\epsilon^{\prime}}e^{-\left(\epsilon-\epsilon^{\prime}\right)T}\leq C_{\epsilon}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\epsilon T}

(5.18)

with
\epsilon=\frac{1}{2}\epsilon^{\prime}
. Similarly we define

R_{\mathrm{out}}^{\left(0\right)}\left(z\right):=\left(\int_{0}^{T}e^{-tz}e^{tX}dt\right)\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{T}_{\geq\left(K+1\right)}\right)\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{0}}\right)

(5.19)

and get

\left\|R_{\mathrm{out}}^{\left(0\right)}\left(z\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\underset{(\ref{eq:exp-tXK+1},\ref{eq:Re_z})}{\leq}C_{\epsilon}

(5.20)

and

\left\|\left(z-X\right)R_{\mathrm{out}}^{\left(0\right)}\left(z\right)-\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{T}_{\geq\left(K+1\right)}\right)\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{0}}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\underset{(\ref{eq:exp-tXK+1},\ref{eq:Re_z})}{\leq}C_{\epsilon}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\epsilon T}.

(5.21)

5.2
Contribution of
\Omega_{1}

Recall that
\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{1}}\right)

defined from
\Omega_{1}
in (
5.6
) depends on
\sigma>0
.
Let
\sigma^{\prime}>0
.

e^{tX}\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{1}}\right)=e^{tX}\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma^{\prime}}\right)\right)\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{1}}\right)+e^{tX}\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma^{\prime}}\right)\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{1}}\right)

For the first term, from Theorem
4.6
, we have
\forall\Lambda>0,\exists C>0,\forall t\geq 0,

\exists\sigma_{t},\forall\sigma^{\prime}>\sigma_{t}
,

\left\|e^{tX}\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma^{\prime}}\right)\right)\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{1}}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\underset{(\ref{eq:decay_outside})}{\leq}\frac{1}{2}Ce^{-\Lambda t}.

For the second term, since the support of the symbols are disjoint,
we have
\forall\Lambda>0,\exists C>0,\forall t\geq 0,

\forall\sigma^{\prime},\exists\sigma>\sigma^{\prime}
,
\exists\omega_{t}>0,\forall\left|\omega\right|>\omega_{t},

\left\|\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma^{\prime}}\right)\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{1}}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\leq\frac{1}{2}Ce^{-\Lambda t}

Taking
\Lambda>0
large enough, we deduce that
\exists C>0,\forall t\geq 0,

\exists\sigma_{t},\forall\sigma>\sigma_{t},\exists\omega_{t}>0,\forall\left|\omega\right|>\omega_{t},

\left\|e^{tX}\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{1}}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\leq Ce^{t\gamma_{K+1}^{+}}.

(5.22)

Approximate resolvent:

For
T\geq 0
we define

R^{\left(1\right)}\left(z\right):=\int_{0}^{T}e^{-tz}e^{tX}\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{1}}\right)dt

(5.23)

and similarly to (
5.16
) and (
5.17
) we get

\left\|R^{\left(1\right)}\left(z\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\underset{(\ref{eq:Xi_Omega1},\ref{eq:Re_z})}{\leq}C_{\epsilon},

(5.24)

and

\left\|\left(z-X\right)R^{\left(1\right)}\left(z\right)-\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{1}}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\underset{(\ref{eq:Xi_Omega1},\ref{eq:Re_z})}{\leq}Ce^{-\epsilon T}.

(5.25)

5.3
Contribution of
\Omega_{2}

We define

R^{\left(2\right)}\left(z\right):=\mathrm{Op}\left(\frac{1}{z-i\omega\left(.\right)}\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{2}}\right)

We have the following properties. Recall that
\delta>0
enters in
the Definition (
5.3
) of
J^{\prime}_{\omega,\delta}
, hence
for
\Omega_{2}
.

Lemma 5.2
.

[
20
, Lemma 5.17]
There
exists
C>0
such that for any
\omega\in\mathbb{R},\delta>0
,

\left\|R^{\left(2\right)}\left(z\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\leq\frac{C}{\delta},

(5.26)

and
