\left\|\left(z-X\right)R^{\left(2\right)}\left(z\right)-\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Omega_{2,\sigma}}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\leq\frac{C}{\delta}.

(5.27)

5.4
Sum of contributions

The final approximate resolvent is defined by

R\left(z\right):=R_{\mathrm{in}}^{\left(0\right)}\left(z\right)+R_{\mathrm{out}}^{\left(0\right)}\left(z\right)+R^{\left(1\right)}\left(z\right)+R^{\left(2\right)}\left(z\right).

From previous estimates we have

\left\|R\left(z\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\underset{(\ref{eq:b_R0},\ref{eq:b_R0out},\ref{eq:b_R1},\ref{eq:b_R2})}{\leq}3C_{\epsilon}+\frac{C}{\delta},

and

\left\|\left(z-X\right)R\left(z\right)-\mathrm{Id}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\underset{(\ref{eq:Id_decomp},\ref{eq:r_omega},\ref{eq:R0_in},\ref{eq:R0_out},\ref{eq:R1},\ref{eq:R2})}{\leq}r\left(\omega\right)+2C_{\epsilon}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\epsilon T}+Ce^{-\epsilon T}+\frac{C}{\delta},

with
\lim_{\left|\omega\right|\rightarrow\infty}r\left(\omega\right)=0
.
Hence we can take in this order
T\gg 1
,
\sigma\gg 1
,
\delta\gg 1
,
\left|\omega\right|\gg 1
,
large enough so that

\left(z-X\right)R\left(z\right)=\mathrm{Id}-r_{2}\left(z\right)

with

\left\|r_{2}\left(z\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}<\frac{1}{2}.

Hence
\left(\mathrm{Id}-r_{2}\left(z\right)\right)^{-1}
is bounded.
We set
\tilde{R}\left(z\right):=R\left(z\right)\left(\mathrm{Id}-r_{2}\left(z\right)\right)^{-1}

and get that

\left(z-X\right)\tilde{R}\left(z\right)=\mathrm{Id}.

With a similar construction on the left
\tilde{R}_{l}\left(z\right)\left(z-X\right)=\mathrm{Id}
,
we deduce that

\left\|\left(z-X\right)^{-1}\right\|\leq C_{\epsilon}

is bounded by
C_{\epsilon}>0
that does not depend on
\omega
.
We have shown (
1.15
) and (
1.16
). This
finishes the proof of Theorem
1.7
.

6
Proof of Theorem
1.5

(emergence of quantum dynamics)

Recall that the space
\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)
in (
4.18
)
depends on the weight
W
in (
4.15
) that itself depends
on a parameter
R\in\mathbb{R}
. Let
K\in\mathbb{N}
and
\gamma_{K+1}^{+}

defined in (
4.45
). Using Theorem
4.8
,
we can take
R
large enough such that the generator
X
in
\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)

has discrete Ruelle spectrum on
\mathrm{Re}\left(z\right)>\gamma_{K+1}^{+}

.

We will use the approximate projector
\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}\right)

defined from the symbol (
4.39
) and (
4.55
),
the operator
\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\omega}\right)
in (
1.25
)
that selects the low frequencies. We have the decomposition

\displaystyle e^{tX}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\right)e^{tX}\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}\right)\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\right)\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\omega}\right)\right)

(6.1)

\displaystyle+\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\right)e^{tX}\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}\right)\right)\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\right)\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\omega}\right)\right)

(6.2)

\displaystyle+\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\right)\right)e^{tX}\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\omega}\right)\right)+\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\right)e^{tX}\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\right)\right)\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\omega}\right)\right)

(6.3)

\displaystyle+e^{tX}\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\omega}\right)

(6.4)

For the first term in the right hand side of (
6.1
) we have

\displaystyle e^{tX}\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}\right)

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:expression})}{\approx}\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}\right)

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:compos_operators})}{\approx}\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}\right)

(6.5)

hence

\displaystyle\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\right)e^{tX}\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}\right)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\right)\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\omega}\right)\right)

(6.6)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\right)\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}\right)\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\right)\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\omega}\right)\right)+r_{t,\omega}

(6.7)

with

\lim_{\left|\omega\right|\rightarrow\infty}\left\|r_{t,\omega}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\underset{(\ref{eq:result_of_Shur})}{=}0.

(6.8)

For the second term in the right hand side of (
6.1
), we proceed
exactly as for the proof of Eq.(
5.12
) in Lemma
5.1

above, with the difference that we replace
\mathrm{Op}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Omega_{0}}\right)

by
\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\right)\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\omega}\right)\right)
.
We get that
\forall\epsilon>0,\exists C_{\epsilon}>0
,
\forall t\geq 0
,

\exists\omega_{t}>0
,
\forall\omega>\omega_{t}
,

\left\|\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\right)e^{tX}\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}\right)\right)\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\right)\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\omega}\right)\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\leq C_{\epsilon}e^{t\left(\gamma_{K+1}^{+}+\epsilon\right)}

Let
\Lambda\geq-\gamma_{K+1}^{+}
. For the terms in line (
6.3
),
we have
\exists C>0,\forall t\geq 0,

\exists\sigma_{t},\forall\sigma>\sigma_{t}
,

\left\|\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\right)\right)e^{tX}\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\omega}\right)\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\underset{(\ref{eq:decay_outside-1})}{\leq}Ce^{-\Lambda t}\leq Ce^{t\gamma_{K+1}^{+}},

\left\|\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\right)e^{tX}\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\right)\right)\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\omega}\right)\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\underset{(\ref{eq:decay_outside-1})}{\leq}Ce^{-\Lambda t}\leq Ce^{t\gamma_{K+1}^{+}}.

The operator
\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\omega}\right)
is compact hence
we can decompose the last term in the right hand side of (
6.1
)
and the last term in (
6.5
) as

\left(e^{tX}-\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\right)\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}\right)\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\right)\right)\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\omega}\right)=R_{t}+r^{\prime\prime}

(6.9)

where
R_{t}
is finite rank and
\left\|r^{\prime\prime}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\leq C_{\epsilon}e^{t\left(\gamma_{K+1}^{+}+\epsilon\right)}
.
We get

e^{tX}\underset{(\ref{eq:eq},\ref{eq:eq4},\ref{eq:eq2})}{=}\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\right)\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}\right)\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\right)+R_{t}+r^{\prime\prime}+r_{t,\omega},

where
\left|\omega\right|
is taken large enough in (
6.8
)
so that
\left\|r_{t,\omega}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\leq Ce^{t\left(\gamma_{K+1}^{+}+\epsilon\right)}

also. We have obtained
\exists C>0
,
\forall t>0
,
\exists\sigma_{t}>0
,
\forall\sigma>\sigma_{t}
,

\exists R_{\sigma,t}
finite rank operator,

\left\|e^{tX}-\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\right)\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}\right)\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\right)+R_{\sigma,t}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\leq Ce^{t\left(\gamma_{K+1}^{+}+\epsilon\right)}.

giving Theorem
1.5
.

Remark 6.1
.

by the effects of weight
W
we can replace
\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\right)\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}\right)\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\Sigma,\sigma}\right)

by
\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}\right)

in (
1.13
).

7
Proof of Theorem
1.11

(Weyl law)

The aim of this section is to prove Theorem
1.11
.
We do this in three steps.

7.1
Approximate projector

For
\omega\in\mathbb{R}
,
\delta>0
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we
define an approximate projector
P
for the union of band
k^{\prime}\in\left[0,k\right]

and frequency interval
\left[\omega-\delta,\omega+\delta\right]

as follows. We will relate its trace to a symplectic volume. We denote

\boldsymbol{1}_{\left[\omega-\delta,\omega+\delta\right]}:C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}_{\left[0,k\right]}\right)\rightarrow C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}_{\left[0,k\right]}\right)

the multiplication operator by the characteristic function for
\rho\in\Sigma
,

\boldsymbol{1}_{\left[\omega-\delta,\omega+\delta\right]}\left(\rho\right):=\begin{cases}1&\text{ if }\boldsymbol{\omega}\left(\rho\right)\in\left[\omega-\delta,\omega+\delta\right]\\
0&\text{ if not.}\end{cases}.

We define the operator

P:=\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\text{$\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\left[\omega-\delta,\omega+\delta\right]}\tilde{T}_{\left[0,k\right]}\right)$}\quad:C^{\infty}\left(M\right)\rightarrow C^{\infty}\left(M\right).

(7.1)

The next lemma shows that the trace of
P
is related to the symplectic
volume of the support of function
\boldsymbol{1}_{\left[\omega-\delta,\omega+\delta\right]}

on the trapped set
\Sigma
.

Lemma 7.1
.

The operator
P
is trace class in
\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)

and
\exists C>0
,
\forall\omega\geq\delta\geq 1
,

\left\|P\right\|_{\mathrm{Tr}}\leq C\omega^{d}\delta,\qquad\left\|P\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}}\leq C,

(7.2)

\left|\mathrm{Tr}\left(P\right)-\mathrm{rank}\left(\mathcal{F}_{\left[0,k\right]}\right)\frac{\mathrm{Vol}\left(M\right)\omega^{d}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d+1}}\left(2\delta\right)\right|\leq C\omega^{d}\delta\left(\left|\omega\right|^{-\left(\beta-\mu\right)/2}+\frac{\delta}{\omega}\right)+C\omega^{d}

(7.3)

Remark 7.2
.

Later in (
7.39
) we will use (
7.3
) as follows.
For any small
c^{\prime}>0
we will take
\delta\gg 1
and
\omega\gg\delta

large enough so that the right hand side of (
7.3
) is
smaller than
c^{\prime}\omega^{d}\delta
.

Démonstration
.

We have

\left\|P\right\|_{\mathrm{Tr}}\underset{(\ref{eq:P_k-1})}{=}\left\|\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\text{$\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\left[\omega-\delta,\omega+\delta\right]}\tilde{T}_{\left[0,k\right]}\right)$}\right\|_{\mathrm{Tr}}\underset{(\ref{eq:bound_OP_Sigma-1})}{\leq}C\omega^{d}\delta,

\left\|P\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\underset{(\ref{eq:P_k-1})}{=}\left\|\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\text{$\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\left[\omega-\delta,\omega+\delta\right]}\tilde{T}_{\left[0,k\right]}\right)$}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\underset{(\ref{eq:bound_OP_Sigma})}{\leq}C.

Eq. (
7.3
) comes from (
4.87
)
because
T_{\left[0,k\right]}\left(m\right)
is a projector onto

\mathrm{Pol}_{\left[0,k\right]}\left(E_{s}\left(m\right)\right)

hence

\mathrm{Tr}\left(T_{\left[0,k\right]}\left(m\right)\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:def_Tk})}{=}\mathrm{dim}\left(\mathrm{Pol}_{\left[0,k\right]}\left(E_{s}\left(m\right)\right)\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:def_F-1})}{=}\mathrm{rank}\left(\mathcal{F}_{\left[0,k\right]}\right)

and

\int_{M}\mathrm{Tr}\left(T_{\left[0,k\right]}\left(m\right)\right)dm=\mathrm{Vol}\left(M\right)\mathrm{rank}\left(\mathcal{F}_{\left[0,k\right]}\right).

∎

7.2
Truncated resolvent

Definition 7.3
.

For
z\in\mathbb{C}
,
\Lambda>0
, let

\mathcal{R}\left(z\right):=\left(z-\left(X-\Lambda P\right)\right)^{-1}

(7.4)

D\left(z\right):=\left(z-X\right)^{-1}-\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)

(7.5)

Remark 7.4
.

A similar construction of truncated resolvent
\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)

has been used in
[
20
, Proof of Prop. 5.14]
.

Let
\delta^{\prime}>0
and define the rectangle in the spectral plane

\tilde{R}\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right):=\left[\gamma_{k}^{-}-2\epsilon,\gamma_{0}^{+}+2\epsilon\right]\times i\left[\omega-\delta+\delta^{\prime},\omega+\delta-\delta^{\prime}\right]

(7.6)

The next lemma is similar to
[
20
, Lemma 5.16]

so we omit the proof.

Lemma 7.5
.

There exists
C>0
such that, if
\delta^{\prime}>0

in (
7.6
) and
\Lambda>0
in (
7.4
) are
sufficiently large, then for any
\omega\in\mathbb{R},\delta>\delta^{\prime}>0
,

z\in\tilde{R}\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)
,

\left\|\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\leq C.

(7.7)

Remark 7.6
.

A consequence of Lemma
7.5
and expression (
7.5
)
is that the poles of
D\left(z\right)
in
\tilde{R}\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)

coincide with the poles of the resolvent
\left(z-X\right)^{-1}
,
i.e. Ruelle resonances.

Let us consider the following union of two horizontal bands (see Figure

7.1
)

\displaystyle B_{\pm}\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle:=\left[\gamma_{k}^{-}-\epsilon,\gamma_{0}^{+}+\epsilon\right]\times i\left[\omega\pm\left(\delta-2\delta^{\prime}\right),\omega\pm\left(\delta-\delta^{\prime}\right)\right],

(7.8)

\displaystyle B\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle:=B_{+}\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)\cup B_{-}\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right).

(7.9)

Figure
7.1:
Picture in the spectral plane for the rectangular
domain
\tilde{R}\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)
defined in (
7.6
),
the sub-domains
B_{\pm}\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)
defined
in (
7.8
) and
R\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)
defined
later in (
7.30
).

We will need later (in the proof of Lemma
7.11
), the
following proposition.

Proposition 7.7
.

The operator
D\left(z\right):\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)

is meromorphic w.r.t.
z\in\tilde{R}\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)

and trace class outside of its poles of first order. In particular

\mathrm{Tr}\left(D\left(z\right)\right)\in L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{1}

is locally integrable and with setting
z:=x+iy
, we have
\exists C>0,\forall\delta>1,\forall\omega>\delta,

\int_{B\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)}\left|\mathrm{Tr}\left(D\left(z\right)\right)\right|dxdy\leq C\delta^{\prime}\omega^{d}\ln\delta.

(7.10)

Démonstration
.

We first show few lemmas.

Démonstration
.

Lemma 7.8
.

D\left(z\right)
is trace class outside of its poles and

\mathrm{Tr}\left(D\left(z\right)\right)=\partial_{z}\ln F\left(z\right)

(7.11)

with

\displaystyle A\left(z\right)

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)\Lambda P,

(7.12)

F\left(z\right):=\mathrm{det}\left(A\left(z\right)\right).

(7.13)

D\left(z\right)
is meromorphic because both
\left(z-X\right)^{-1}

and
\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)
are meromorphic, see
[
20
, Lemma 5.16]
.

\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)
is even holomorphic on the rectangle

\tilde{R}\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)
, from Lemma
7.5
.
Then

\displaystyle D\left(z\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:def_D})}{=}

\displaystyle\left(z-X\right)^{-1}-\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(z-\left(X-\Lambda P\right)\right)^{-1}\left(\left(z-\left(X-\Lambda P\right)\right)-\left(z-X\right)\right)\left(z-X\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)\Lambda P\left(z-X\right)^{-1}.

(7.14)

In this last expression
P
is trace class.
\left(z-X\right)^{-1}

and
\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)
are bounded outside of their poles
hence
D\left(z\right)
is trace class outside the poles of
\left(z-X\right)^{-1}

and
\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)
. We have

\displaystyle D\left(z\right)

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:def_D})}{=}\left(z-X\right)^{-1}-\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)=\left(z-\left(X-\Lambda P\right)+\Lambda P\right)^{-1}-\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:def_RR})}{=}\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)\Lambda P\right)^{-1}-\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:def_A})}{=}\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)\left(A\left(z\right)^{-1}-\mathrm{Id}\right).

(7.15)

We compute

\partial_{z}A\left(z\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:def_A})}{=}\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)^{2}\Lambda P\underset{(\ref{eq:def_A})}{=}\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)\left(\mathrm{Id}-A\left(z\right)\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:help})}{=}D\left(z\right)A\left(z\right),

(7.16)

then we have

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\ln A\left(z\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\partial_{z}A\left(z\right)\right)A\left(z\right)^{-1}\underset{(\ref{eq:int_A})}{=}D\left(z\right),

\ln F\left(z\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:def_F})}{=}\ln\mathrm{det}\left(A\left(z\right)\right)=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\ln A\left(z\right)\right),

\partial_{z}\ln F\left(z\right)=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\partial_{z}\ln A\left(z\right)\right)=\mathrm{Tr}\left(D\left(z\right)\right).

We have obtained (
7.11
).
∎

For later use, we also notice that

\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:R-Rk})}{=}A\left(z\right)\left(z-X\right)^{-1}.

(7.17)

Lemma 7.9
.

There exists
C>0
, such that for every
z\in B\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)
,

\left|F\left(z\right)\right|\leq e^{C\left(\ln\delta\right)\omega^{d}}.

(7.18)

and for every
z\in B\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)
with
\mathrm{Re}\left(z\right)=\gamma_{k}^{-}-\epsilon
,

\left|F\left(z\right)\right|\geq e^{-C\left(\ln\delta\right)\omega^{d}}.

(7.19)

Démonstration
.

For
z\in B_{-}\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)
, writing
\lambda_{j}\left(z\right)
,

j=1,2,\cdots,J,
for the eigenvalues of the trace class operator

\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)\Lambda P
, we have by
Lidskii’s theorem

\displaystyle\log\left|F\left(z\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\log\left|\mathrm{det}\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)\Lambda P\right)\right|=\sum_{j}\log\left|1-\lambda_{j}\left(z\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j}\log\left(1+\left|\lambda_{j}\left(z\right)\right|\right)\leq\sum_{j}\left|\lambda_{j}\left(z\right)\right|\leq\left\|\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)\Lambda P\right\|_{\mathrm{Tr}}.

(7.20)

For the last inequality, see e.g.
[
24
, p.64]
. Notice
that
\left\|\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)\Lambda P\right\|_{\mathrm{Tr}}\underset{(\ref{eq:trace_bound},\ref{eq:R_bounded})}{\leq}C\delta\omega^{d}

but this is not enough to get (
7.18
). We will improve
the last bound as follows. We decompose the frequency interval
\left[\omega-\delta,\omega+\delta\right]

as the union of
\lfloor\delta\rfloor
(the integer part of
\delta
)
intervals of bounded length
l=\frac{2\delta}{\left[\delta\right]}
,
so that

\left[\omega-\delta,\omega+\delta\right]=\bigcup_{w\in\left\{1,2,\ldots,\lfloor\delta\rfloor\right\}}I_{w}

(7.21)

with

I_{w}:=\left[\omega-\delta+\left(w-1\right)l,\omega-\delta+wl\right].

(7.22)

Parallel to (
7.1
) we define

P_{w}:=\check{\mathcal{T}}_{\sigma}^{\Delta}\check{\mathrm{Op}}_{\Sigma}\left(T_{\left[0,k\right]}\right)\chi_{I_{w}}\check{\mathcal{T}}_{\sigma}\quad:C^{\infty}\left(M\right)\rightarrow C^{\infty}\left(M\right)

i.e. an approximate projector on frequency interval of size
l
and
we write

P=\sum_{w}P_{w}.

(7.23)

Similarly to (
7.2
) we have

\left\|P_{w}\right\|_{\mathrm{Tr}}\leq C\omega^{d}

and

\left\|\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)\Lambda P_{w}\right\|_{\mathrm{Tr}}\leq\frac{1}{w}C\omega^{d}

(7.24)

uniformly for
1\leq w\leq\lfloor\delta\rfloor
. Hence

\left\|\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)\Lambda P\right\|_{\mathrm{Tr}}\leq\sum_{w=1}^{\left[\delta\right]}\left\|\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)\Lambda P_{w}\right\|_{\mathrm{Tr}}\leq C\omega^{d}\ln\delta.

We get

\left|F\left(z\right)\right|\underset{(\ref{eq:log_F})}{\leq}e^{\left\|\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)\Lambda P\right\|_{\mathrm{Tr}}}\leq e^{C\omega^{d}\ln\delta}.

We have obtained (
7.18
). To prove the second claim
(
7.19
), we consider an arbitrary
z\in B\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)

with
\mathrm{Re}\left(z\right)=\gamma_{k}^{-}-\epsilon
. From Corollary

1.7
we have
\left\|\left(z-X\right)^{-1}\right\|\leq C
.
Then

\left(\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)\Lambda P\right)^{-1}=A\left(z\right)^{-1}\underset{(\ref{eq:R-Rk2})}{=}\left(z-X\right)^{-1}\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)^{-1}\underset{(\ref{eq:def_RR})}{=}\left(\mathrm{Id}+\left(z-X\right)^{-1}\Lambda P\right)

is uniformly bounded from (
7.2
). Hence
\left|1-\lambda_{j}\left(z\right)\right|>c

with some
c>0
independent on
\omega
and therefore we have
\left|\log\left|1-\lambda_{j}\left(z\right)\right|\right|<C^{\prime}\left|\lambda_{j}\left(z\right)\right|

for some
C^{\prime}>0
. Thus

-\log\left|F\left(z\right)\right|=-\sum_{j}\log\left|1-\lambda_{j}\left(z\right)\right|\leq C^{\prime}\sum_{j}\left|\lambda_{j}\left(z\right)\right|\leq C^{\prime}\left\|\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)\Lambda P\right\|_{\mathrm{Tr}}\leq C^{\prime}\omega^{d}\ln\delta,

giving (
7.19
).
∎

Let
\mathrm{spect}\left(X\right)
denotes the discrete Ruelle spectrum
of
X
.

Lemma 7.10
.

We have
\exists C>0,\forall\omega
,
\forall z\in B\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right),

\left|\partial_{z}\ln F\left(z\right)-\sum_{z_{j}\in\mathrm{spect}\left(X\right)\cap B\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)}\frac{1}{z-z_{j}}\right|\leq C\left(\ln\delta\right)\omega^{d}

(7.25)

where the sum over
z_{j}\in\mathrm{spect}\left(X\right)\cap B\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)

is counted with multiplicity of the Ruelle eigenvalues.

Démonstration
.

This is lemma
\alpha
in
[
44
, p.56]
. We
reproduce the proof here. From (
7.7
),
F\left(z\right)

is a well defined holomorphic function in the domains
B\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)

and from (
7.17
), the zeroes of
F\left(z\right)
coincide
up to multiplicities with the poles of
\left(z-X\right)^{-1}
, i.e.
Ruelle eigenvalues
z_{j}\in\mathrm{spect}\left(X\right)\cap B\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)
.
Using Weyl upper bound
O\left(\omega^{d}\right)
on the density
in
[
20
, Thm.3.6]
, note
that the number of such Ruelle eigenvalues is bounded by
C\delta^{\prime}\omega^{d}\leq C\delta\omega^{d}
.
From (
7.19
), we can fix some
z_{0}\in B\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)

with
\mathrm{Re}\left(z_{0}\right)=\gamma_{k}^{-}-\epsilon
so that

\left|F\left(z_{0}\right)\right|\underset{(\ref{eq:F_lower_bound})}{\geq}e^{-C^{\prime}\delta\omega^{d}}.

The function

G\left(z\right):=\frac{F\left(z\right)\prod_{z_{j}}\left(z_{0}-z_{j}\right)}{F\left(z_{0}\right)\prod_{z_{j}}\left(z-z_{j}\right)}.

(7.26)

G\left(z\right)
is holomorphic on
B\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)

and from estimate (
7.18
) on the boundary of
B\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)

and
maximum modulus principle

we get

\forall z\in B\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right),\quad\left|G\left(z\right)\right|\underset{(\ref{eq:F_upper_bound})}{\leq}e^{C\omega^{d}\ln\delta}.

(7.27)

Moreover
G\left(z\right)
has no zero on on the simply connected
domain
B\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)
hence

g\left(z\right):=\ln G\left(z\right)

(7.28)

is well defined as a holomorphic function on
B\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)

with
g\left(z_{0}\right)=0
and satisfies
\mathrm{Re}g\left(z\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:bound_G})}{\leq}C\omega^{d}\ln\delta
.
By
Borel-Carathéodory theorem
15
15

15

Here we do not need to give the constants and the proof is simple:

g
maps
B\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)
on the half-plane

\mathrm{Re}\left(z\right)<C\delta\omega^{d}
. We post-compose by
a
Moebius transformation

f
which maps this half-plane to the unit disk and maps
g\left(z_{0}\right)=0

to
0
. Then the image of
B\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)

by
f\circ g
is contained in a disk
\left|z\right|<r<1
. This
implies that the image
g\left(B\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)\right)

is contained in a (large) disk
\left|z\right|<C^{\prime}\delta\omega^{d}
.
this implies

\left|g\left(z\right)\right|<C^{\prime}\omega^{d}\ln\delta

on a region slightly larger than
B\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)

and therefore by
Cauchy integral formula

\left|\partial_{z}g\left(z\right)\right|\underset{(\ref{eq:def_g},\ref{eq:def_G})}{=}\left|\partial_{z}\ln F\left(z\right)-\sum_{z_{j}}\frac{1}{z-z_{j}}\right|<C^{\prime\prime}\omega^{d}\ln\delta

giving the conclusion.
∎

Now we finish with the proof of Lemma
7.7
. Using
Weyl upper bound on the density in
[
20
, Thm. 2.3]
,
we have, with
z=x+iy
, that

\sum_{z_{j}\in\mathrm{spect}\left(X\right)\cap B\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)}\int_{B\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)}\frac{1}{\left|z-z_{j}\right|}dxdy<C\left(\delta^{\prime}\omega^{d}\right)\ln\delta^{\prime}.

(7.29)

Then

\displaystyle\int_{B\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)}\left|\mathrm{Tr}\left(D\left(z\right)\right)\right|dxdy

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:Tr_D})}{=}\int_{B\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)}\left|\partial_{z}\ln F\left(z\right)\right|dxdy

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:lnF},\ref{eq:bound})}{\leq}C\delta^{\prime}\omega^{d}\ln\delta+C\delta^{\prime}\omega^{d}\ln\delta^{\prime}\leq 2C\delta^{\prime}\omega^{d}\ln\delta.

We have obtained (
7.10
).
∎

7.3
Counting resonances by argument principle

From Lemma
7.5
, the truncated resolvent
\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)

is bounded on the rectangle
\tilde{R}\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)

and hence by the Definition (
7.5
) the poles of
z\rightarrow\mathrm{Tr}\,D\left(z\right)

coincides with the poles of
\left(z-X\right)^{-1}
, i.e., the Ruelle
resonance. In the following, we count the number of Ruelle resonance
in the rectangle
\tilde{R}\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)
by
using a slightly generalized version of the argument principle. We
learned this method from the paper of S. Dyatlov
[
9
, Section 11]
.

In the following, we suppose that
\delta>0,\delta^{\prime}>0
is fixed so
that
2\delta^{\prime}<\delta
and we consider the limit
|\omega|\to\infty
.
We take a
C^{\infty}
function
f_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\left[0,1\right]

that is
f_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(\omega^{\prime}\right)=1
for
\left|\omega^{\prime}\right|<\delta-2\delta^{\prime}

and
f_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(\omega^{\prime}\right)=0
for
\left|\omega^{\prime}\right|>\delta-\delta^{\prime}

and
\left|\partial_{\omega^{\prime}}f\right|<C\delta^{\prime-1}
. Then for
\omega\in\mathbb{R}
,

z\in\mathbb{C}
, let

f_{\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(z\right):=f_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(\mathrm{Im}\left(z-i\omega\right)\right)

Let us define the rectangle in the spectral plane (see Figure
7.1
)

R\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right):=\left[\gamma_{k}^{-}-\epsilon,\gamma_{0}^{+}+\epsilon\right]\times i\left[\omega-\delta+2\delta^{\prime},\omega+\delta-2\delta^{\prime}\right].

(7.30)

In the following we suppose that the
k
-th band is separated from

(k+1)
-st band i.e.

\gamma_{k+1}^{+}<\gamma_{k}^{-}.

Note that
R\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)
is a proper subset
of
\tilde{R}\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)
defined in (
7.6
).
We have that
\forall z\in R\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)
,

f_{\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(z\right)=1
and
\forall z\in\tilde{R}\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)
,

\left|\partial_{\overline{z}}f_{\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(z\right)\right|<C\delta^{\prime-1}.

(7.31)

We consider the following paths
\Gamma:=\Gamma_{\mathrm{-}}\cup\Gamma_{+}

on
\mathbb{C}
, parameterized as

\Gamma_{-}:\omega\in\mathbb{R}\rightarrow z=\gamma_{k}^{-}-\epsilon+i\omega\in\mathbb{C},

\Gamma_{+}:\omega\in\mathbb{R}\rightarrow z=\gamma_{0}^{+}+\epsilon+i\omega\in\mathbb{C}.

From (
7.7
) and (
1.16
), there is no
pole of
D(z)
on
\Gamma\cap\mathrm{supp}\left(f_{\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\right)

supposing that
\left|\omega\right|
is sufficiently large. We know
that
D\left(z\right)
is in trace class. Hence the integral
\int_{\Gamma_{+}-\Gamma_{-}}f_{\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(z\right)\mathrm{Tr}\left(D\left(z\right)\right)dz

is well defined. The next lemma shows that this integral is close
to the number of Ruelle resonances in the rectangle
R\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)
.

Lemma 7.11
.

We have
\exists C>0,
\forall\delta>1,\forall\omega>\delta,\forall 0<\delta^{\prime}<\delta
,

\left|\left(\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\Gamma_{+}-\Gamma_{-}}f_{\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(z\right)\mathrm{Tr}\left(D\left(z\right)\right)dz\right)-\sharp\left(\mathrm{spect}\left(X\right)\cap\tilde{R}\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)\right)\right|<C\omega^{d}\max\left(\ln\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)

(7.32)

Démonstration
.

From the Definition of
f_{\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}}
above, we have

f_{\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(z\right)=0
if
\left|\mathrm{Im}\left(z\right)-\omega\right|>\delta-\delta^{\prime}
,
so we can close the contour integral on horizontal segments
\mathrm{Im}\left(z\right)=\omega\pm\left(\delta-\delta^{\prime}\right)
.
The residues of the poles of
D\left(z\right)
inside the contour
are the spectral projector onto eigenspaces. Hence
Cauchy integral formula for smooth functions

gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\Gamma_{+}-\Gamma_{-}}f_{\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(z\right)\mathrm{Tr}\left(D\left(z\right)\right)dz=

\displaystyle\sum_{z_{j}\in\mathrm{spect}\left(X\right)\cap\tilde{R}\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)}f_{\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(z_{j}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\tilde{R}\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)}\left(\partial_{\overline{z}}f_{\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\right)\left(z\right)\mathrm{Tr}\left(D\left(z\right)\right)dxdy

with
z=x+iy
. We have
\exists C>0,
\forall\delta>0,\forall\delta^{\prime}<\frac{1}{2}\delta,\forall\omega>\delta
,

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\tilde{R}\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)}\left(\partial_{\overline{z}}f_{\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\right)\left(z\right)\mathrm{Tr}\left(D\left(z\right)\right)dxdy\right|\leq

\displaystyle\delta^{\prime-1}\int_{B\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)}\left|\mathrm{Tr}\left(D\left(z\right)\right)\right|dxdy

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:almost_anal},\ref{eq:int_trace})}{\leq}

\displaystyle C\delta^{\prime-1}\left(\ln\delta\right)\delta^{\prime}\omega^{d}=C\omega^{d}\ln\delta.

(7.33)

From properties of
f_{\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}}
and using Weyl upper
bound on the density in
[
20
, Thm.2.3]

we have

\left|\sum_{z_{j}\in\mathrm{spect}\left(X\right)\cap\tilde{R}\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)}f_{\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(z_{j}\right)-\sharp\left\{z_{j}\in\mathrm{spect}\left(X\right)\cap\tilde{R}\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)\right\}\right|\leq C\omega^{d}\delta^{\prime}.

We have obtained (
7.32
).
∎

7.4
Relation with the symplectic volume

The last step is to relate the integral
\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\Gamma_{+}-\Gamma_{-}}f_{\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(z\right)\mathrm{Tr}\left(D\left(z\right)\right)dz

in (
7.32
) to the trace of
P
in (
7.3
). Recall
Definition (
7.1
) of
P
. We define the operator

P_{0}:=\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\text{$\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\left[\omega-\delta+\delta^{\prime}/2,\omega+\delta-\delta^{\prime}/2\right]}T_{\left[0,k\right]}\right)$}.

For
z\in R\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)
, let

d(z)=\min\left\{\mathrm{Im}(z)-(\omega+\delta+\delta^{\prime}/2),\mathrm{Im}(z)+(\omega-\delta-\delta^{\prime}/2)\right\}\geq\delta^{\prime}/2.

Lemma 7.12

(
Approximate expressions of
D\left(z\right)
)
.

There exists
C>0

such that for any
T>0
,
\omega>\delta>\delta^{\prime}>1
and any
z\in\mathbb{C}

with
\mathrm{Im}\left(z\right)\in\left[\omega-\delta+\delta^{\prime},\omega+\delta-\delta^{\prime}\right]

and
\mathrm{Re}\left(z\right)=\gamma_{0}^{+}+\epsilon
, we have

\left\|D\left(z\right)-\left(\int_{0}^{T}e^{-tz}e^{t\left(X-\Lambda\right)}dt\right)\left(\Lambda P_{0}\right)\left(\int_{0}^{T}e^{-tz}e^{tX}dt\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{Tr}}\leq C\omega^{d}\left(e^{-\epsilon T}+\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{\left(d\left(z\right)\right)^{2}}\right)

(7.34)

If instead
\mathrm{Re}\left(z\right)=\gamma_{k}^{-}-\epsilon
, we
have

\left\|D\left(z\right)-\left(\int_{0}^{T}e^{-tz}e^{t\left(X-\Lambda\right)}dt\right)\left(\Lambda P_{0}\right)\left(\int_{-T}^{0}e^{-tz}e^{tX}dt\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{Tr}}\leq C\omega^{d}\left(e^{-\epsilon T}+\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{\left(d\left(z\right)\right)^{2}}\right)

(7.35)

Démonstration
.

Let
z\in\mathbb{C}
with
\mathrm{Im}\left(z\right)\in\left[\omega-\delta+\delta^{\prime},\omega+\delta-\delta^{\prime}\right]
.
In the following we let
T>0
be sufficiently large. Suppose
\mathrm{Re}\left(z\right)=\gamma_{0}^{+}+\epsilon
.
As in (
7.23
), we decompose
P=\sum_{w\in W}P_{w}
with

W:=\left\{1,2,\ldots,\lfloor\delta\rfloor\right\}
. As in (
7.21
)
with interval
I_{w}
defined in (
7.22
) we decompose

\left[\omega-\delta+\delta^{\prime}/2,\omega+\delta-\delta^{\prime}/2\right]=\bigcup_{w\in W_{0}}I_{w}

with a subset
W_{0}\subset W
. So we can write

P_{0}=\sum_{w\in W_{0}}P_{w}.

We have

D\left(z\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:R-Rk})}{=}\sum_{w\in W}D_{w}\left(z\right)

with

D_{w}\left(z\right):=\Lambda\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)P_{w}\left(z-X\right)^{-1}.

For
w\in W_{0}
, let

\tilde{D}_{w}\left(z\right):=\Lambda\left(\int_{0}^{T}e^{-tz}e^{t\left(X-\Lambda\right)}dt\right)P_{w}\left(\int_{0}^{T}e^{-tz}e^{tX}dt\right).

Then

\displaystyle\left(z-X+\Lambda\right)\tilde{D}_{w}\left(z\right)\left(z-X\right)

\displaystyle=\Lambda\left[e^{-tz}e^{t\left(X-\Lambda\right)}\right]_{0}^{T}P_{w}\left[e^{-tz}e^{tX}\right]_{0}^{T}

\displaystyle=\Lambda P_{w}+\Lambda r_{w},

with

r_{w}=e^{T\left(-z+X-\Lambda\right)}P_{w}+P_{w}e^{T\left(-z+X\right)}+e^{T\left(-z+X-\Lambda\right)}P_{w}e^{T\left(-z+X\right)}.

We deduce that

\tilde{D}_{w}\left(z\right)=D_{w}\left(z\right)+\Lambda\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)r_{w}\left(z-X\right)^{-1}

with

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)r_{w}\left(z-X\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)e^{T\left(-z+X-\Lambda\right)}P_{w}\left(z-X\right)^{-1}+\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)P_{w}\left(z-X\right)^{-1}e^{T\left(-z+X\right)}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)e^{T\left(-z+X-\Lambda\right)}P_{w}\left(z-X\right)^{-1}e^{T\left(-z+X\right)}.

For the right hand side of the last equality, we have the following
estimates. We use the notation
\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\ast\right)

for an operator whose trace norm is bounded by a constant multiple
of
\ast
. If
w\in W_{0}
then as in (
7.24
), we have

\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)r_{w}\left(z-X\right)^{-1}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\omega^{d}e^{-\epsilon T}\frac{1}{\mathrm{dist}\left(I_{w},\mathrm{Im}\left(z\right)\right)^{2}}\right),

where
\mathrm{dist}\left(I_{w},\mathrm{Im}\left(z\right)\right)

is the distance between
I_{w}
and
\mathrm{Im}\left(z\right)
.
If
w\in W\backslash W_{0}
then

D_{w}\left(z\right)=\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\omega^{d}\frac{1}{\left(d\left(z\right)\right)^{2}}\right).

Taking the sum with respect to
w\in W_{0}
, we see

\sum_{w\in W_{0}}\left\|\mathcal{R}\left(z\right)r_{w}\left(z-X\right)^{-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{Tr}}=\sum_{w\in W_{0}}\mathcal{O}\left(\omega^{d}e^{-\epsilon T}\frac{1}{\mathrm{dist}\left(I_{w},\mathrm{Im}\left(z\right)\right)^{2}}\right)=\mathcal{O}\left(\omega^{d}e^{-\epsilon T}\right),

\sum_{w\in W\backslash W_{0}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\omega^{d}\frac{1}{\left(d\left(z\right)\right)^{2}}\right)=\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\omega^{d}\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{\left(d\left(z\right)\right)^{2}}\right).

Hence

\displaystyle\left\|D\left(z\right)-\left(\int_{0}^{T}e^{-tz}e^{t\left(X-\Lambda\right)}dt\right)\left(\Lambda P_{0}\right)\left(\int_{0}^{T}e^{-tz}e^{tX}dt\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{Tr}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\left(\omega^{d}\left(e^{-\epsilon T}+\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{\left(d\left(z\right)\right)^{2}}\right)\right).

∎

Now we give the key estimate.

Lemma 7.13
.

For any arbitrarily small
c>0
, if we take
\delta
large enough,

\delta^{\prime}>1
large enough and
\delta/\delta^{\prime}
large enough, we have

\left|\left(\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\Gamma_{+}-\Gamma_{-}}f_{\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(z\right)\mathrm{Tr}\left(D\left(z\right)\right)dz\right)-\mathrm{Tr}\left(P\right)\right|<c\omega^{d}\delta

(7.36)

Démonstration
.

We first consider the integration on
\Gamma_{+}
. From the last
lemma, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma_{+}}

\displaystyle f_{\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(z\right)\mathrm{Tr}\left(D\left(z\right)\right)dz=\int_{\mathbb{R}}f_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(\omega^{\prime}-\omega\right)\mathrm{Tr}\left(D\left(\gamma_{0}^{+}+\epsilon+i\omega^{\prime}\right)\right)d\left(i\omega^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:approx_D-4})}{=}i

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[\int_{\omega^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}}f_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(\omega^{\prime}-\omega\right)\left(\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T}e^{-t^{\prime}\left(\gamma_{0}^{+}+\epsilon+i\omega^{\prime}\right)}e^{t^{\prime}\left(X-\Lambda\right)}dt^{\prime}\right)\left(\Lambda P_{0}\right)\left(\int_{t=0}^{T}e^{-t\left(\gamma_{0}^{+}+\epsilon+i\omega^{\prime}\right)}e^{tX}dt\right)\right]d\omega^{\prime}

(7.37)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}_{Tr}\left(\omega^{d}\left(\delta e^{-\epsilon T}+\delta^{\prime}\right)\right).

Let

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(t\right)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-t\left(\gamma_{0}^{+}+\epsilon+i\omega^{\prime}\right)}f_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(\omega^{\prime}-\omega\right)d\omega^{\prime}=e^{-t\left(\gamma_{0}^{+}+\epsilon+i\omega\right)}\hat{f}_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(t\right),

where
\hat{f}_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(t\right):=\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-it\omega^{\prime}}f_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(\omega^{\prime}\right)d\omega^{\prime}

satisfies a fast decay for
\left|t\right|\gg\delta^{\prime-1}
as follows:

\forall N>0,\exists C_{N}>0,\forall t,

\left|\hat{f}_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(t\right)\right|\leq C_{N}\left\langle\delta^{\prime}t\right\rangle^{-N}\quad\text{{and}$\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hat{f}_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(t\right)dt=2\pi f_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(0\right)=2\pi.$}

(7.38)

Then, with the change of variables
\left(t,t^{\prime}\right)\rightarrow\left(t,s=t+t^{\prime}\right)
,
we see

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma_{+}}

\displaystyle f_{\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(z\right)\mathrm{Tr}\left(D\left(z\right)\right)dz

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:f_om_delt})}{=}i

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{T}\tilde{f}_{\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(t+t^{\prime}\right)\mathrm{Tr}\left(e^{t^{\prime}\left(X-\Lambda\right)}P_{0}\Lambda e^{tX}\right)dt^{\prime}dt+\mathcal{O}_{Tr}\left(\omega^{d}\left(\delta e^{-\epsilon T}+\delta^{\prime}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=i

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2T}\left(\int_{t=\max\left\{0,s-T\right\}}^{\min\left\{T,s\right\}}e^{-s\left(\gamma_{0}^{+}+\epsilon+i\omega\right)}\hat{f}_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(s\right)\mathrm{Tr}\left(e^{\left(s-t\right)\left(X-\Lambda\right)}P_{0}\Lambda e^{tX}\right)dt\right)ds

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}_{Tr}\left(\omega^{d}\left(\delta e^{-\epsilon T}+\delta^{\prime}\right)\right).

From (
7.38
), by letting
\delta^{\prime}
be large (and
\hat{f}_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(t\right)

be more concentrate to
0
), we get

\left|\int_{\Gamma_{+}}f_{\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(z\right)\mathrm{Tr}\left(D\left(z\right)\right)dz\right|=\mathcal{O}_{Tr}\left(\delta^{\prime-1}\omega^{d}\delta\right)+\mathcal{O}_{Tr}\left(\omega^{d}\left(\delta e^{-\epsilon T}+\delta^{\prime}\right)\right).

Next we consider the integration on
\Gamma_{-}
. Similarly to the
argument above, we see

\displaystyle\int_{-\Gamma_{-}}

\displaystyle f_{\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(z\right)\mathrm{Tr}\left(D\left(z\right)\right)dz=i\int f_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(\omega^{\prime}-\omega\right)\mathrm{Tr}\left(D\left(\gamma_{0}^{-}-\epsilon-i\omega^{\prime}\right)\right)d\omega^{\prime}

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:approx_D-4})}{=}

\displaystyle i\mathrm{Tr}\left[\int f_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(\omega^{\prime}-\omega\right)\left(\int_{0}^{T}e^{-t^{\prime}\left(\gamma_{0}^{-}-\epsilon-i\omega^{\prime}\right)}e^{t^{\prime}\left(X-\Lambda\right)}dt^{\prime}\right)\left(\Lambda P_{0}\right)\left(\int_{-T}^{0}e^{-t\left(\gamma_{0}^{-}-\epsilon-i\omega^{\prime}\right)}e^{tX}dt\right)\right]d\omega^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\omega^{d}\left(\delta e^{-\epsilon T}+\delta^{\prime}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\int_{s=-T}^{T}\left(\int_{t=\max\left\{-T,s-T\right\}}^{\min\left\{0,s\right\}}e^{-s\left(\gamma_{0}^{-}-\epsilon-i\omega^{\prime}\right)}\hat{f}_{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\left(s\right)\mathrm{Tr}\left(e^{s\left(X-\Lambda\right)}e^{-t\left(X-\Lambda\right)}\Lambda P_{0}e^{tX}\right)dt\right)ds

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\omega^{d}\left(\delta e^{-\epsilon T}+\delta^{\prime}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\pi i\int_{t=-T}^{0}\mathrm{Tr}\left(e^{-t\left(X-\Lambda\right)}\Lambda P_{0}e^{tX}\right)dt+\mathcal{O}_{Tr}\left(\delta^{\prime-1}\omega^{d}\delta\right)+\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\omega^{d}\left(\delta e^{-\epsilon T}+\delta^{\prime}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\pi i\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left[e^{t\Lambda}P_{0}\right]_{t=-T}^{0}\right)+\mathcal{O}_{Tr}\left(\delta^{\prime-1}\omega^{d}\delta\right)+\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\omega^{d}\left(\delta e^{-\epsilon T}+\delta^{\prime}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\pi i\mathrm{Tr}\left(P_{0}\right)+\mathcal{O}_{Tr}(e^{-\epsilon T}\omega^{d})+\mathcal{O}_{Tr}\left(\delta^{\prime-1}\omega^{d}\delta\right)+\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\omega^{d}\left(\delta e^{-\epsilon T}+\delta^{\prime}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\pi i\mathrm{Tr}\left(P\right)+\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\omega^{d}\delta^{\prime}\right)+\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Tr}}(e^{-\epsilon T}\omega^{d})+\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\delta^{\prime-1}\omega^{d}\delta\right)+\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Tr}}\left(\omega^{d}\left(\delta e^{-\epsilon T}+\delta^{\prime}\right)\right).

Therefore, by letting
T
large
\delta\gg\delta^{\prime}\gg 1
and then
letting
\omega
large, we obtain the required estimate.
∎

7.5
Weyl law

Using the previous estimates we get that

\displaystyle\left|\sharp\left(\mathrm{spect}\left(X\right)\cap\tilde{R}\left(\omega,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)\right)-\mathrm{rank}\left(\mathcal{F}_{\left[0,k\right]}\right)\frac{\mathrm{Vol}\left(M\right)\omega^{d}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d+1}}\left(2\delta\right)\right|

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:res4},\ref{eq:Int_TrP},\ref{eq:trace-1})}{<}C\omega^{d}\max\left\{\delta^{\prime},\ln\delta\right\}+c\omega^{d}\delta+C\omega^{d}\delta\left(\left|\omega\right|^{-\beta/2}+\frac{\delta}{\omega}\right)+C\omega^{d}

\displaystyle\left(C_{N}\sigma^{-N}+\left|\omega\right|^{-\beta/2}\sigma+\frac{\delta}{\omega}\right)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle c^{\prime}\omega^{d}\delta.

(7.39)

with arbitrarily small
c^{\prime}>0
and
\omega\gg\delta\gg\delta^{\prime}\gg 1
.
Comparing this estimate for adjacent
k
’s, we obtain Theorem
1.11
.

8
Proof of Theorem
1.13

(accumulation of eigenvalues on narrower bands)

The aim of this section is to prove Theorem
1.13
.
We consider the operator
X_{F}
defined in Section
2.2

where
X
is a contact Anosov flow. For
k\in\mathbb{N},
consider
the vector bundle
\pi:\mathcal{F}_{k}\rightarrow\Sigma
, with
\mathcal{F}_{k}=\left|\mathrm{det}N_{s}\right|^{-1/2}\otimes\mathrm{Pol}_{k}\left(N_{s}\right)\otimes F

defined in (
4.43
), twisted by
F
, and the flow induced
from
X_{F}
,

\phi_{k}^{t}:\mathcal{F}_{k}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}_{k},\quad t\in\mathbb{R},

that is a linear bundle map over
\tilde{\phi}^{t}:\Sigma\rightarrow\Sigma
.
For a point
\rho\in\Sigma
,
t\in\mathbb{R}
, let

g_{k,t}\left(\rho\right):=\ln\left(\left(\max_{v\in\pi^{-1}\left(\rho\right)}\frac{\left\|\phi_{k}^{t}\left(v\right)\right\|}{\left\|v\right\|}\right)^{1/t}\right),

that defines a continuous function
g_{k,t}
on
\Sigma
. Let
d\varrho

be the Liouville measure (
3.8
) on
\Sigma
and

\mu_{k,t}:=\left(g_{k,t}^{\circ}\right)^{\dagger}\left(d\varrho\right)

be the push-forward measure on
\mathbb{R}
by the map
g_{k,t}
.
We have already defined
\gamma_{k}^{\pm}
in (
4.45
)
that can be written as

\gamma_{k}^{+}:=\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\max_{\rho\in\Sigma}g_{k,t}\left(\rho\right)=\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\max\left(\mathrm{supp}\left(\mu_{k,t}\right)\right),

\gamma_{k}^{-}:=\lim_{t\rightarrow-\infty}\min_{\rho\in\Sigma}g_{k,t}\left(\rho\right)=\lim_{t\rightarrow-\infty}\min\left(\mathrm{supp}\left(\mu_{k,t}\right)\right).

Now we define the
maximal and minimal Lyapounov exponents
with respect to the Liouville measure
by

\check{\gamma}_{k}^{+}=\max\left(\mathrm{supp}\left(\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\mu_{k,t}\right)\right),

(8.1)

\check{\gamma}_{k}^{-}=\min\left(\mathrm{supp}\left(\lim_{t\rightarrow-\infty}\mu_{k,t}\right)\right).

One has

\gamma_{k}^{-}\leq\check{\gamma}_{k}^{-}\leq\check{\gamma}_{k}^{+}\leq\gamma_{k}^{+}.

From the definition of
\check{\gamma}_{k}^{-},\check{\gamma}_{k}^{+}
,
for any small
\epsilon>0
, if
I_{\epsilon}:=\left[\check{\gamma}_{k}^{-}-\epsilon,\check{\gamma}_{k}^{+}+\epsilon\right]

and

\Sigma_{\omega,\delta,I_{\epsilon},t}:=\left(g_{k,t}^{-1}\left(I_{\epsilon}\right)\cap\left[\omega,\omega+\delta\right]\right)\subset\Sigma,

then we have that for any
c>0
, there exists
T_{c}>0
such that

\left(d\varrho\right)\left(\Sigma\backslash\Sigma_{\omega,\delta,I_{\epsilon},T_{c}}\right)\leq c\delta\omega^{d}.

(8.2)

For the proof of Theorem
1.13
, we follow

[
18
, Thm 1.3.11 and Chapter 8,]
.
Let
c>0
. Take
T_{c}>0
large enough such that (
8.2
)
holds true. Then similarly to (
4.62
) one gets
\forall\epsilon>0,\exists C_{\epsilon}>0
,
\forall t\geq 0
,

\exists\sigma_{t}>0
,
\forall\sigma>\sigma_{t},

\exists\omega_{\sigma}>0,\forall\omega>\omega_{\sigma}
,

\left\|e^{tX}\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Sigma_{\omega,\delta,I_{\epsilon},T_{c}}}T_{k}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\leq C_{\epsilon}e^{t\left(\check{\gamma}_{k}^{+}+\epsilon\right)},

\left\|e^{-tX}\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Sigma_{\omega,\delta,I_{\epsilon},T_{c}}}T_{k}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{W}\left(M\right)}\leq C_{\epsilon}e^{-t\left(\check{\gamma}_{k}^{-}-\epsilon\right)},

\left\|\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\boldsymbol{1}_{\Sigma\backslash\Sigma_{\omega,\delta,I_{\epsilon},T_{c}}}T_{k}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{Tr}}=c\delta\omega^{d}+C\omega^{d}.

Using arguments with perturbation of the resolvent as in previous
proofs, we deduce the Theorem.

9
Proof of Theorem
1.18

(horocycle operators)

Suppose
s\in C^{\beta}\left(M;E_{s}\right)
is a Hölder continuous
section of the stable bundle over
M
and
u\in C^{\beta}\left(M;E_{u}\right)

a section of the unstable bundle. Let
\pi:T^{*}M\rightarrow M
be
the canonical projection. We have seen in (
4.25
) that
for
\rho\in\Sigma
, the differential
d\pi
gives a linear isomorphism

d\pi:N\left(\rho\right)=N_{s}\left(\rho\right)\oplus N_{u}\left(\rho\right)\rightarrow E_{u}\left(m\right)\oplus E_{s}\left(m\right)

with
m=\pi\left(\rho\right)
. By pull back we get sections
\tilde{s}:=\left(d\pi\right)^{-1}\left(s\right)\in C\left(\Sigma;N_{s}\right)

and
\tilde{u}:=\left(d\pi\right)^{-1}\left(u\right)\in C\left(\Sigma;N_{u}\right)
.
Recall from Section
4.1.2
that
d\mathcal{A}

is a symplectic form on
E_{u}\oplus E_{s}
and from Definition
3.4

that
N\left(\rho\right)=N_{s}\left(\rho\right)\oplus N_{u}\left(\rho\right)

is a
\Omega
-symplectic vector space and that
\Omega_{\rho}\left(\tilde{u},\tilde{s}\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:theta_A})}{=}\omega\left(\rho\right).\left(d\mathcal{A}\right)\left(u,s\right)
,
hence
\tilde{u}
(so then
u
) defines a dual vector

\tilde{u}^{*}:=\check{\Omega}\tilde{u}\underset{(\ref{eq:def_Omega_check})}{=}\Omega\left(\tilde{u},.\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:theta_A})}{=}\boldsymbol{\omega}\left(.\right)\,\left(d\mathcal{A}\right)\left(u,d\pi\left(.\right)\right)\in N_{s}^{*}\left(\rho\right).

(9.1)

For
k\in\mathbb{N}
, let
p\in C\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}_{k}\right)

a continuous section of
\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(N_{s}\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:def_F-1})}{=}\left|\mathrm{det}N_{s}\right|^{-1/2}\otimes\mathrm{Pol}_{k}\left(N_{s}\right)\otimes F

over
\Sigma
where
\mathrm{Pol}_{k}\left(N_{s}\right)\equiv\mathrm{Sym}^{k}\left(N_{s}^{*}\otimes\ldots\otimes N_{s}^{*}\right)

is equivalent to the
symmetric tensor algebra

of degree
k
.

Definition 9.1
.

For
s\in C^{\beta}\left(M;E_{s}\right)
we define

\iota_{s}:\begin{cases}C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}_{k}\right)&\rightarrow C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right)\\
p&\rightarrow\iota_{\tilde{s}}p=kp\left(\tilde{s},.,\ldots,.\right)\end{cases}

(9.2)

as the
interior product
,
i.e. the pointwise (i.e. independently in every fiber) contraction
in the first entry of
p
by
\tilde{s}:=\left(d\pi\right)^{-1}\left(s\right)\in C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;N_{s}\right)
,
but with a factor
k
. For
u\in C^{\beta}\left(M;E_{u}\right)

we define

u\varodot:\begin{cases}C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}_{k}\right)&\rightarrow C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}_{k+1}\right)\\
p&\rightarrow\tilde{u}^{*}\varodot p=\mathrm{Sym}\left(\tilde{u}^{*}\otimes p\right)\end{cases}

as the symmetric point-wise tensor product of
p
by
\tilde{u}^{*}:=\omega\left(d\mathcal{A}\right)\left(u,d\pi\left(.\right)\right)\in C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;N_{s}^{*}\right)
.

Lemma 9.2

(
Weyl algebra
)
.

For
s\in C^{\beta}\left(M;E_{s}\right)
,

u\in C^{\beta}\left(M;E_{u}\right)
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
we have

\left[\iota_{s},u\varodot\right]=\boldsymbol{\omega}\left(.\right)\left(d\mathcal{A}\right)\left(s,u\right)\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{F}_{k}}\quad:C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}_{k}\right)\rightarrow C^{\beta}\left(\Sigma;\mathcal{F}_{k}\right)

(9.3)

where
\omega
is the frequency function (
2.7
)
on
\Sigma
and
\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{F}_{k}}
is the identity
operator in fibers.

Démonstration
.

This is point wise relation so we consider a point
\rho\in\Sigma

with frequency
\omega\underset{(\ref{eq:omega_function})}{=}\boldsymbol{\omega}\left(\rho\right)

and the vector space
N_{s}\left(\rho\right)
defined in (
4.25
).
We use the isomorphism
\mathrm{Pol}_{k}\left(N_{s}\right)\equiv\mathrm{Sym}^{k}\left(N_{s}^{*}\otimes\ldots\otimes N_{s}^{*}\right)

to derive (
9.3
) that goes as follows. If
\left(e_{i}\right)_{i}

is a basis of
N_{s}\left(\rho\right)
, we write
\tilde{s}=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sigma_{i}e_{i}\in N_{s}\left(\rho\right)

with components
\sigma=\left(\sigma_{i}\right)_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
For
p\in\mathrm{Sym}^{k}\left(N_{s}^{*}\otimes\ldots\otimes N_{s}^{*}\right)
,
we associate a degree
k
polynomial
P\in\mathrm{Pol}_{k}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)

by
P\left(\sigma\right):=p\left(\tilde{s},\tilde{s},\ldots\tilde{s}\right)
.
We define
e_{i}^{*}\varodot p:=\mathrm{Sym}\left(e_{i}^{*}\otimes p\right)

and observe that
\left(e_{i}^{*}\varodot p\right)\left(\tilde{s}\right)=\sigma_{i}P\left(\sigma\right)

and
\left(\iota_{e_{i}}p\right)\left(\tilde{s}\right)=\left(\partial_{\sigma_{i}}P\right)\left(\sigma\right)
.
Then the “Weyl algebra relation ” on polynomials
\left[\partial_{\sigma_{i}},\sigma_{j}\right]=\delta_{i=j}\mathrm{Id}

gives
\left[\iota_{e_{i}},e_{j}^{*}\varodot\right]=\delta_{i=j}\mathrm{Id}

hence for
\tilde{s}\in N_{s}
,
\tilde{u}^{*}\in N_{s}^{*}
we
have
\left[\iota_{\tilde{s}},\tilde{u}^{*}\varodot\right]=\tilde{u}^{*}\left(\tilde{s}\right)\mathrm{Id}
.
With (
9.1
), we get (
9.3
).
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.18
:

Let
K\in\mathbb{N}
. We will use
\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}

to restrict to the finite rank bundle of homogeneous polynomials of
degree less than
K
. For
k<K
, let
\tilde{T}_{k}
the projector
on homogeneous polynomials of degree
k
. We have

\displaystyle\left[\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\iota_{s}\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}\right),\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\left(u\varodot\right)\tilde{T}_{\left[0,K\right]}\right)\right]\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\tilde{T}_{k}\right)

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:compos_operators})}{\approx}\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\left[\iota_{s},u\varodot\right]\tilde{T}_{k}\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:commut})}{=}\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\boldsymbol{\omega}\left(.\right)\left(d\mathcal{A}\right)\left(s,u\right)\tilde{T}_{k}\right)

that gives (
1.36
). We have

\displaystyle e^{tX_{F}}\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\iota_{s}\tilde{T}_{k}\right)

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:expression})}{\approx}\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\iota_{s}\tilde{T}_{k}\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:compos_operators})}{\approx}\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\iota_{s}\tilde{T}_{k}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\iota_{d\phi^{t}s}e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\tilde{T}_{k}\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:commut_T},\ref{eq:compos_operators})}{\approx}\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\iota_{d\phi^{t}s}\tilde{T}_{k}\right)\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(e^{tX_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:expression})}{\approx}\mathrm{Op}_{\Sigma}\left(\iota_{d\phi^{t}s}\tilde{T}_{k}\right)e^{tX_{F}},

giving (
1.37
) and similarly to get (
1.38
).

Annexe
A
General notations used in this
paper

•

Dual
*
.
If
E
is a finite dimensional vector space and

E^{*}
its
dual space
,

x\in E,\xi\in E^{*}
we denote the duality by
\langle\xi|x\rangle\in\mathbb{R}
.
If
E_{1},E_{2}
are vector spaces and
A:E_{1}\rightarrow E_{2}

is a linear map, we will denote
A^{*}:E_{2}^{*}\rightarrow E_{1}^{*}

the induced dual map on the dual spaces defined by
\left\langle A^{*}\xi|x\right\rangle=\langle\xi|Ax\rangle,\forall x\in E_{1},\xi\in E_{2}^{*}
.

•

Bilinear map.
Let
\Omega:E\times E\rightarrow\mathbb{R}

be a bilinear map on a vector space
E
. It defines a linear map

\check{\Omega}:E\rightarrow E^{*}
by

\check{\Omega}\left(x\right)\left(.\right)=\Omega\left(x,.\right).

(A.1)

–

If
\check{\Omega}
is invertible (
\Omega
is said to be non degenerated),
it induces a bilinear form on
E^{*}
denoted
\Omega^{-1}:E^{*}\times E^{*}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}

and defined by
\Omega^{-1}\left(\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\right):=\Omega\left(\check{\Omega}^{-1}\xi_{1},\check{\Omega}^{-1}\xi_{2}\right)=\xi_{1}\left(\check{\Omega}^{-1}\xi_{2}\right)
.

–

The bilinear map
\Omega:E\times E\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is anti-symmetric
iff

\check{\Omega}^{*}=-\check{\Omega}.

(A.2)

The bilinear map
\Omega:E\times E\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is symmetric
iff
\check{\Omega}^{*}=\check{\Omega}
.

•

Adjoint
\dagger
.
Suppose
A:E_{1}\rightarrow E_{2}
is
a linear map and
\Omega_{1}
(respect.
\Omega_{2}
) is a non degenerated
bilinear form on
E_{1}
(respect.
E_{2}
). The
\Omega
-adjoint
of
A
is
A^{\dagger_{\Omega}}:E_{2}\rightarrow E_{1}
defined
by

A^{\dagger_{\Omega}}=\check{\Omega}_{1}^{-1}A^{*}\check{\Omega}_{2}

(A.3)

equivalently given by

\Omega_{1}\left(A^{\dagger_{\Omega}}x_{1},x_{2}\right)=\Omega_{2}\left(x_{1},Ax_{2}\right),\forall x_{1}\in E_{1},\forall x_{2}\in E_{2}.

In this paper bilinear forms will be either a symplectic form
\Omega\left(.,.\right)

or a metric
g\left(.,.\right)
.

•

Pull-back
\circ
.
If
f:M\rightarrow N
is a smooth map
between two manifolds,

–

We denote
f^{\circ}
the
pull back
operator

f^{\circ}:\begin{cases}C^{\infty}\left(N\right)&\rightarrow C^{\infty}\left(M\right)\\
u&\rightarrow u\circ f\end{cases}

(A.4)

and if
f
is a diffeomorphism, we denote
f^{-\circ}:=\left(f^{-1}\right)^{\circ}

the
push forward
operator, so that
f^{-\circ}f^{\circ}=\mathrm{Id}
.

–

We denote
df:TM\rightarrow TN
the differential of
f
that is
a linear bundle map and denote
\left(df\right)^{*}:T^{*}N\rightarrow T^{*}M

its dual.

Annexe
B
More information about flows

B.1
Transfer operator

Suppose that
X
is a vector field on
M
and
\left(\phi^{t}\right)^{\circ}=e^{tX}

is the pull back operator defined in (
2.2
). For any
smooth measure
d\mu
on
M
, the
L^{2}\left(M,d\mu\right)
scalar
product is defined by
\langle v|u\rangle_{L^{2}}:=\int_{M}\overline{u}vd\mu

with
u,v\in C_{c}^{\infty}\left(M\right)
. Then the
L^{2}
-adjoint

\left(\phi^{t}\right)^{\circ\dagger}
of
\left(\phi^{t}\right)^{\circ}
,
defined by
\langle v|\left(\phi^{t}\right)^{\circ\dagger}u\rangle_{L^{2}}=\langle\left(\phi^{t}\right)^{\circ}v|u\rangle_{L^{2}},\forall u,v\in C_{c}^{\infty}\left(M\right)

is given by

\left(\phi^{t}\right)^{\circ\dagger}=\left|\mathrm{det}\left(d\phi^{-t}\right)\right|.\left(\phi^{-t}\right)^{\circ}=e^{tX^{\dagger}},\quad\text{with }X^{\dagger}=-X-\mathrm{div}_{\mu}X.

\left(\phi^{t}\right)^{\circ\dagger}
is called the
Ruelle-Perron-Frobenius
operator
or
transfer operator
.

Observe that
\left(\phi^{t}\right)^{\circ\dagger}

pushes
forward probability distributions
because

\int_{M}\left(\phi^{t}\right)^{\circ\dagger}ud\mu=\langle\mathrm{1}|\left(\phi^{t}\right)^{\circ\dagger}u\rangle_{L^{2}}=\langle\underbrace{\left(\phi^{t}\right)^{\circ}\mathrm{1}}_{1}|u\rangle_{L^{2}}=\int_{M}ud\mu.

In particular the evolution of Dirac measures gives
\left(\phi^{t}\right)^{\circ\dagger}\delta_{m}=\delta_{\phi^{t}\left(m\right)}

and is equivalent to evolution of points under the flow map
\phi^{t}
.

B.2
More general pull back operators

e^{tX_{F}}

Suppose that
X
is a smooth vector field on
M
and
F\rightarrow M

is a smooth vector bundle over
M
. Suppose that
X_{F}:C^{\infty}\left(M;F\right)\rightarrow C^{\infty}\left(M;F\right)

is a derivation over
X
fulfilling (
2.4
).

B.2.1
Expression in local frames

For a general vector bundle
F
of rank
r
, with respect to a local
frame
\left(e_{1},\ldots e_{r}\right)
, a section
u\in C^{\infty}\left(M;F\right)

is expressed as
u\left(m\right)=\sum_{j=1}^{r}u_{j}\left(m\right)e_{j}\left(m\right)

with
m\in M
and components
u_{j}\left(m\right)\in\mathbb{C}
.
We introduce a matrix of potential functions
V_{k,j}\left(m\right)\in\mathbb{C}

defined by

\left(X_{F}e_{k}\right)\left(m\right)=\sum_{j}V_{k,j}\left(m\right)e_{j}\left(m\right).

(B.1)

Then the operator
X_{F}
in (
2.3
) is expressed as

\left(X_{F}u\right)\left(m\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:def_XF})}{=}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\left(\left(Xu_{j}\right)\left(m\right)+\sum_{k=1}^{r}V_{k,j}\left(m\right)u_{k}\left(m\right)\right)e_{j}\left(m\right).

(B.2)

We see that the expression (
B.2
) generalizes (
2.5
).

B.2.2
Expression of
e^{tX_{F}}
from a flow
\tilde{\phi}_{F}^{t}
on

F

An equivalent way to present the operator
X_{F}
in (
2.4
),
is to consider a vector field
\tilde{X}_{F}
on
F
, a lift of

X
, i.e.
\left(d\pi\right)\tilde{X}_{F}=X
, with the projection

\pi:F\rightarrow M
, and such that the flow map generated by
\tilde{X}_{F}

\tilde{\phi}_{F}^{t}:F\rightarrow F

i.e.
\left(\tilde{\phi}_{F}^{t}\right)^{\circ}=e^{t\tilde{X}_{F}}:\mathcal{S}\left(F\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(F\right)
,
is a smooth linear bundle map over
\phi^{t}
which means that for
every
m\in M,t\in\mathbb{R}
,
\tilde{\phi}_{F}^{t}\left(m\right):F\left(m\right)\rightarrow F\left(\phi^{t}\left(m\right)\right)

is a linear map. See Figure
B.1
. This defines
a
group of pull back operators
with generator
X_{F}
acting
on a section
u\in C^{\infty}\left(M;F\right)
of the vector bundle

F
:

e^{tX_{F}}:C^{\infty}\left(M;F\right)\rightarrow C^{\infty}\left(M;F\right),\quad t\in\mathbb{R}.

by

e^{tX_{F}}u:=\tilde{\phi}_{F}^{-t}\left(u\circ\phi^{t}\right).

(B.3)

We get
e^{tX_{F}}\left(fu\right)=\tilde{\phi}_{F}^{-t}\left(\left(e^{tX}f\right)\left(u\circ\phi^{t}\right)\right)=\left(e^{tX}f\right)\left(e^{tX_{F}}u\right)

and deduce (
2.4
) by derivation w.r.t.
t
. Conversely
the flow map
\tilde{\phi}_{F}^{t}
and its generator
\tilde{X}_{F}

is defined from
X_{F}
by (
B.3
) and use of any section

u
.

Figure
B.1:
pull back operator
e^{tX_{F}}

Annexe
C
Bargmann transform and Metaplectic
operators

In this section we collect results that concerns the
Bargmann transform

on a vector space
E
, the quantization of affine and linear symplectic
map, that are the unitary representations of the
Heisenberg group

and the
metaplectic operators
.
All these definitions and results are well known and important in
many fields of mathematics and physics. They are at the core of micro-local
analysis. We present these definitions and results in a form that
is adapted to our paper that relies strongly on them. References are

[
22
]
,
[
29
]
,
[
32
, appendix 4.4]
,
[
18
, chap.3, chap.4]
.

C.1
Weyl Heisenberg group

Let
E
be a real vector space,
n=\mathrm{dim}E
. For
x\in E
,

\xi\in E^{*}
we denote
\langle\xi|x\rangle\in\mathbb{R}
the
duality. Let
dx\in\left|\Lambda^{n}\left(E\right)\right|
be a
density

on
E
let
d\xi
be the induced density on
E^{*}
. Let us denote
the
Fourier transform

\mathcal{F}:\begin{cases}\mathcal{S}\left(E\right)&\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(E^{*}\right)\\
u&\rightarrow v\left(\xi\right)=\frac{1}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n/2}}\int e^{-i\langle\xi|x\rangle}u\left(x\right)dx\end{cases}

Its
L^{2}
-adjoint is

\mathcal{F}^{\dagger}:\begin{cases}\mathcal{S}\left(E^{*}\right)&\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(E\right)\\
v&\rightarrow u\left(x\right)=\frac{1}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n/2}}\int e^{i\langle\xi|x\rangle}v\left(\xi\right)d\xi\end{cases}

and we have that
\mathcal{F}^{\dagger}\mathcal{F}=\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{S}\left(E\right)}

hence
\mathcal{F}^{-1}=\mathcal{F}^{\dagger}
.

For
x\in E
,
\xi\in E^{*}
we define the translation maps

T_{x}:\begin{cases}E&\rightarrow E\\
y&\rightarrow y+x\end{cases},\qquad T_{\xi}:\begin{cases}E^{*}&\rightarrow E^{*}\\
\xi^{\prime}&\rightarrow\xi^{\prime}+\xi\end{cases}

And denote
T_{x}^{-\circ},T_{\xi}^{-\circ}
the push forward operators.
We will denote

\hat{T}_{x,\xi}:=T_{x}^{-\circ}\left(\mathcal{F}^{-1}T_{\xi}^{-\circ}\mathcal{F}\right)

(C.1)

Lemma C.1
.

On
\mathcal{S}\left(E\right)
, one has

\left(\mathcal{F}^{-1}T_{\xi}^{-\circ}\mathcal{F}\right)T_{x}^{-\circ}=e^{i\langle\xi|x\rangle}T_{x}^{-\circ}\left(\mathcal{F}^{-1}T_{\xi}^{-\circ}\mathcal{F}\right).

(C.2)

\hat{T}_{x,\xi}\hat{T}_{x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}}=e^{i\langle\xi|x^{\prime}\rangle}\hat{T}_{x+x^{\prime},\xi+\xi^{\prime}}.

In particular
\hat{T}_{x,\xi}
is a unitary operator in
L^{2}\left(E,dx\right)

and

\hat{T}_{x,\xi}^{-1}=\hat{T}_{x,\xi}^{\dagger}=\hat{T}_{-\left(x,\xi\right)}e^{i\xi x}.

(C.3)

Remark C.2
.

The family of operators
\hat{T}_{x,\xi}
with
\left(x,\xi\right)\in T^{*}E=E\oplus E^{*}

are called the Schrödinger unitary representation of the
Weyl Heisenberg group
.

Démonstration
.

Let
x\in E
. Then

\left(\mathcal{F}\delta_{x}\right)\left(\xi^{\prime}\right)=\frac{1}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n/2}}e^{-i\xi^{\prime}x},\quad\left(T_{\xi}^{-\circ}\mathcal{F}\delta_{x}\right)\left(\xi^{\prime}\right)=\frac{1}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n/2}}e^{-i\left(\xi^{\prime}-\xi\right)x},

\left(\mathcal{F}^{-1}T_{\xi}^{-\circ}\mathcal{F}\delta_{x}\right)\left(x^{\prime}\right)=\frac{1}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}\int e^{i\xi^{\prime}x}e^{-i\left(\xi^{\prime}-\xi\right)x}d\xi^{\prime}=e^{i\xi x}\delta_{x}\left(x^{\prime}\right)

Hence
\mathcal{F}^{-1}T_{\xi}^{-\circ}\mathcal{F}\delta_{x}=e^{i\xi x}\delta_{x}
.
Hence

T_{x}^{-\circ}\left(\mathcal{F}^{-1}T_{\xi}^{-\circ}\mathcal{F}\right)\delta_{x^{\prime}}=e^{i\xi x^{\prime}}\delta_{x+x^{\prime}},

\left(\mathcal{F}^{-1}T_{\xi}^{-\circ}\mathcal{F}\right)T_{x}^{-\circ}\delta_{x^{\prime}}=e^{i\xi\left(x+x^{\prime}\right)}\delta_{x+x^{\prime}}=e^{i\langle\xi|x\rangle}T_{x}^{-\circ}\left(\mathcal{F}^{-1}T_{\xi}^{-\circ}\mathcal{F}\right)\delta_{x^{\prime}}.

Then

\displaystyle\hat{T}_{x,\xi}\hat{T}_{x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=T_{x}^{-\circ}\left(\mathcal{F}^{-1}T_{\xi}^{-\circ}\mathcal{F}\right)T_{x^{\prime}}^{-\circ}\left(\mathcal{F}^{-1}T_{\xi^{\prime}}^{-\circ}\mathcal{F}\right)

\displaystyle=e^{i\langle\xi|x^{\prime}\rangle}T_{x}^{-\circ}T_{x^{\prime}}^{-\circ}\left(\mathcal{F}^{-1}T_{\xi}^{-\circ}\mathcal{F}\right)\left(\mathcal{F}^{-1}T_{\xi^{\prime}}^{-\circ}\mathcal{F}\right)

\displaystyle=e^{i\langle\xi|x^{\prime}\rangle}\hat{T}_{x+x^{\prime},\xi+\xi^{\prime}}.

∎

C.2
Bargmann Transform on a Euclidean
vector space
\left(E,g\right)

Let
E
be a vector space with an Euclidean metric
g
. We have
the canonical identification

T^{*}E=E\oplus E^{*},

with the
canonical symplectic form

\Omega
on
E\oplus E^{*}

given by: for
\left(x_{1},\xi_{1}\right),\left(x_{2},\xi_{2}\right)\in E\oplus E^{*}
,

\Omega\left(\left(x_{1},\xi_{1}\right),\left(x_{2},\xi_{2}\right)\right)=\langle\xi_{1}|x_{2}\rangle-\langle\xi_{2}|x_{1}\rangle.

(C.4)

We denote
dx
the density on
E
induced by
g
.

Definition C.3
.

A
Gaussian wave packet in vertical gauge (V)
with parameters

\left(x,\xi\right)\in E\oplus E^{*}
, is the function
\varphi_{x,\xi}^{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}\in\mathcal{S}\left(E\right)

given by: for
y\in E
,

\varphi_{x,\xi}^{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}\left(y\right):=\pi^{-\frac{n}{4}}e^{i\langle\xi|y-x\rangle}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\left\|y-x\right\|_{g}^{2}}

(C.5)

Similarly a
Gaussian wave packet in radial gauge (R)
is

\varphi_{x,\xi}^{\left(\mathrm{R}\right)}\left(y\right):=e^{\frac{i}{2}\langle\xi|x\rangle}\varphi_{x,\xi}^{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}\left(y\right)=\pi^{-\frac{n}{4}}e^{i\langle\xi|y-\frac{1}{2}x\rangle}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\left\|y-x\right\|_{g}^{2}}

(C.6)

Remark C.4
.

We have

•

The terms ’vertical gauge’ or ’radial gauge’ come from a geometrical
construction of these waves packets using prequantization that we
do not describe here. But we can explain the terminology as follows.
The wave packet is seen as a map
\varphi^{\left(V\right)}:\left(x,\xi\right)\in E\oplus E^{*}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}=L^{2}\left(E\right)

that induces a map to the projective space
\left[\varphi^{\left(V\right)}\right]:E\oplus E^{*}\rightarrow\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{H}\right)

(i.e. ignoring phase) and a pull back line bundle
L\rightarrow E\oplus E^{*}

with Hermitian connection (Levi Civita). The map
\varphi^{\left(V\right)}

can be seen as a trivialization of this line bundle
L
. Its covariant
derivative is given by
D\varphi^{\left(V\right)}=Pd\varphi^{\left(V\right)}=\eta^{\left(V\right)}\varphi^{\left(V\right)}

[
43
, (1.5) p.540]
, with projector onto the fiber
P=\varphi^{\left(V\right)}\langle\varphi^{\left(V\right)}|.\rangle

hence connection one-form

\eta^{\left(V\right)}=\langle\varphi^{\left(V\right)}|d\varphi^{\left(V\right)}\rangle\underset{(\ref{eq:Gaussian_wave_packet})}{=}-i\xi dx.

We observe that the kernel of this one-form is along
x=\mathrm{cste}

on phase space
E\oplus E^{*}
, i.e. ’vertical’. Similarly for the
wave packet (
C.6
), considered as another
trivialization, we compute the connection one-form

\eta^{\left(R\right)}=\langle\varphi^{\left(R\right)}|d\varphi^{\left(R\right)}\rangle\underset{(\ref{eq:Gaussian_wave_packet-V-R})}{=}\frac{i}{2}\left(-\xi dx+xd\xi\right),

for which the kernel at point
\left(x,\xi\right)
contains the ’radial’
vector
\left(x,\xi\right)
.

•

\left\|\varphi_{x,\xi}^{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(E,dx\right)}=\left\|\varphi_{x,\xi}^{\left(\mathrm{R}\right)}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(E,dx\right)}=1

with
dx
the density on
E
associated to the metric
g
.

•

We have

\varphi_{x,\xi}^{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}\underset{(\ref{eq:def_T_rho})}{=}\hat{T}_{x,\xi}\varphi_{0,0}^{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}

(C.7)

Definition C.5
.

The
Bargmann transform
in vertical gauge is the continuous
operator

\mathcal{B}_{E,\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}:\begin{cases}\mathcal{S}\left(E\right)&\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(E\oplus E^{*}\right)\\
u&\rightarrow\left(\left(x,\xi\right)\rightarrow\langle\varphi_{x,\xi}^{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}|u\rangle_{L^{2}\left(E;dx\right)}\right).\end{cases}

(C.8)

Lemma C.6
.

With respect to the density
\frac{1}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}dxd\xi

on
E\oplus E^{*}
, the Bargmann transform
\mathcal{B}_{E,\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}:L^{2}\left(E;dx\right)\rightarrow L^{2}\left(E\oplus E^{*};\frac{1}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}dxd\xi\right)

is an isometry. Its
L^{2}
-adjoint operator
\mathcal{B}_{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}^{\dagger}:L^{2}\left(E\oplus E^{*};\frac{1}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}dxd\xi\right)\rightarrow L^{2}\left(E;dx\right)

is a smoothing operator given by

\left(\mathcal{B}_{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}^{\dagger}v\right)\left(y\right)=\int_{E\oplus E^{*}}\varphi_{x,\xi}^{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}\left(y\right)v\left(x,\xi\right)\frac{dxd\xi}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}

(C.9)

and satisfies the
resolution of identity

\mathrm{Id}_{L^{2}\left(E\right)}=\mathcal{B}_{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}^{\dagger}\mathcal{B}_{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}=\int_{E\oplus E^{*}}\pi\left(x,\xi\right)\frac{dxd\xi}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}

(C.10)

with
\pi\left(x,\xi\right):=\varphi_{x,\xi}^{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}\langle\varphi_{x,\xi}^{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}|.\rangle

that is the rank one orthogonal projector in
L^{2}\left(E\right)

onto
\mathbb{C}\varphi_{x,\xi}^{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}
.

Démonstration
.

We first check (
C.9
). For
u\in\mathcal{S}\left(E\right)

and
v\in\mathcal{S}\left(E\oplus E^{*}\right)
we have

\displaystyle\langle u|\mathcal{B}_{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}^{\dagger}v\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathcal{B}_{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}u|v\rangle_{L^{2}}=\int_{E\oplus E^{*}}\overline{\langle\varphi_{x,\xi}^{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}|u\rangle_{L^{2}}}v\left(x,\xi\right)\frac{dxd\xi}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}

\displaystyle=\int_{E}\overline{u\left(y\right)}\int_{E\oplus E^{*}}\varphi_{x,\xi}^{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}\left(y\right)v\left(x,\xi\right)\frac{dxd\xi}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}dy.

Now we check that
\left\|\mathcal{B}_{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}u\right\|^{2}=\left\|u\right\|^{2}

that is equivalent to (
C.10
). We have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{B}_{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}u\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{E\oplus E^{*}}\left|\langle\varphi_{x,\xi}^{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}|u\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|^{2}\frac{dxd\xi}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}

\displaystyle=\pi^{-\frac{n}{2}}\int e^{-i\langle\xi|y-x\rangle}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\left\|y-x\right\|_{g}^{2}}u\left(y\right)dye^{i\langle\xi|y^{\prime}-x\rangle}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\left\|y^{\prime}-x\right\|_{g}^{2}}\overline{u\left(y^{\prime}\right)}dy^{\prime}\frac{dxd\xi}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}

But

\int e^{i\langle\xi|y^{\prime}-y\rangle}d\xi=\left(2\pi\right)^{n}\delta\left(y^{\prime}-y\right)

\int_{E}e^{-\left\|y\right\|^{2}}dy=\pi^{\frac{n}{2}}

hence

\left\|\mathcal{B}_{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}u\right\|^{2}=\pi^{-\frac{n}{2}}\int e^{-\left\|y-x\right\|_{g}^{2}}\left|u\left(y\right)\right|^{2}dydx=\int\left|u\left(y\right)\right|^{2}dy=\left\|u\right\|^{2}.

∎

By using
\varphi_{x,\xi}^{\left(\mathrm{R}\right)}
in radial gauge
instead of
\varphi_{x,\xi}^{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}
, we can define
similarly operators
\mathcal{B}_{\left(\mathrm{R}\right)},\mathcal{B}_{\left(\mathrm{R}\right)}^{\dagger}
.

Let

\mathcal{P}_{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}:=\mathcal{B}_{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}\mathcal{B}_{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}^{\dagger}\quad:\mathcal{S}\left(E\oplus E^{*}\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(E\oplus E^{*}\right)

(C.11)

\mathcal{P}_{\left(\mathrm{R}\right)}:=\mathcal{B}_{\left(\mathrm{R}\right)}\mathcal{B}_{\left(\mathrm{R}\right)}^{\dagger}\quad:\mathcal{S}\left(E\oplus E^{*}\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(E\oplus E^{*}\right)

(C.12)

be the orthogonal projector in
L^{2}\left(E\oplus E^{*}\right)

onto their closed images
\mathrm{Im}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}\right),\mathrm{Im}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\left(\mathrm{R}\right)}\right)

respectively, called the
Bergman projector
. Remark that Lemma

C.6
holds true for
\mathcal{B}_{\left(\mathrm{R}\right)}

as well.

The metric
g
on
E
induces a canonical metric on
E\oplus E^{*}

denoted
\boldsymbol{g}:=g\oplus g^{-1}
.

Lemma C.7
.

The Schwartz kernel of
\mathcal{P}_{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)},\mathcal{P}_{\left(\mathrm{R}\right)}

are given by

\langle\delta_{x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}}|\mathcal{P}_{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}\delta_{x,\xi}\rangle=\exp\left(\frac{i}{2}\left(\xi^{\prime}+\xi\right)\left(x^{\prime}-x\right)-\frac{1}{4}\left\|\left(x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}\right)-\left(x,\xi\right)\right\|_{g\oplus g^{-1}}^{2}\right)

(C.13)

\langle\delta_{x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}}|\mathcal{P}_{\left(\mathrm{R}\right)}\delta_{x,\xi}\rangle=\exp\left(-\frac{i}{2}\Omega\left(\left(x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}\right),\left(x,\xi\right)\right)-\frac{1}{4}\left\|\left(x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}\right)-\left(x,\xi\right)\right\|_{g\oplus g^{-1}}^{2}\right)

(C.14)

Démonstration
.

We have

\displaystyle\langle\delta_{x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}}|\mathcal{P}_{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}\delta_{x,\xi}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\varphi_{x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}}^{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}|\varphi_{x,\xi}^{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}\rangle=\pi^{-\frac{n}{2}}\int e^{-i\langle\xi^{\prime}|y-x^{\prime}\rangle}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\left\|y-x^{\prime}\right\|_{g}^{2}}e^{i\langle\xi|y-x\rangle}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\left\|y-x\right\|_{g}^{2}}dy

\displaystyle=\pi^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{i\left(\langle\xi^{\prime}|x^{\prime}\rangle-\langle\xi|x\rangle\right)-\frac{1}{2}\left\|x^{\prime}\right\|_{g}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\left\|x\right\|_{g}^{2}}\int e^{-\left\|y\right\|_{g}^{2}+\langle b|y\rangle}dy

with
b=\check{g}\left(x^{\prime}+x\right)+i\left(\xi-\xi^{\prime}\right)\in E^{*}
.
We use the Gaussian integral

\int_{E}e^{-\alpha\left\|y\right\|_{g}^{2}+\langle b|y\rangle}dy=\left(\frac{\pi}{\alpha}\right)^{\frac{n}{2}}e^{\frac{1}{4\alpha}\left\|b\right\|_{g^{-1}}^{2}},

(C.15)

with
\alpha=1
, giving

\displaystyle\langle\delta_{x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}}|\mathcal{P}_{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}\delta_{x,\xi}\rangle

\displaystyle=e^{i\left(\langle\xi^{\prime}|x^{\prime}\rangle-\langle\xi|x\rangle\right)-\frac{1}{2}\left\|x^{\prime}\right\|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\left\|x\right\|^{2}}e^{\frac{1}{4}\left(\left\|x^{\prime}+x\right\|^{2}-\left\|\xi-\xi^{\prime}\right\|^{2}+2i\langle\xi-\xi^{\prime}|x^{\prime}+x\rangle\right)}

\displaystyle=e^{i\left(\langle\xi^{\prime}|x^{\prime}\rangle-\langle\xi|x\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\langle\xi-\xi^{\prime}|x^{\prime}+x\rangle\right)}e^{-\frac{1}{4}\left(\left\|x^{\prime}-x\right\|^{2}+\left\|\xi-\xi^{\prime}\right\|^{2}\right)}

\displaystyle=e^{i\frac{1}{2}\langle\xi^{\prime}+\xi|x^{\prime}-x\rangle}e^{-\frac{1}{4}\left\|\left(x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}\right)-\left(x,\xi\right)\right\|_{g+g^{-1}}^{2}}

We have

\displaystyle\langle\delta_{x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}}|\mathcal{P}_{\left(\mathrm{R}\right)}\delta_{x,\xi}\rangle

\displaystyle=e^{i\frac{1}{2}\left(\langle\xi|x\rangle-\langle\xi^{\prime}|x^{\prime}\rangle\right)}\langle\delta_{x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}}|\mathcal{P}_{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}\delta_{x,\xi}\rangle

\displaystyle=e^{i\frac{1}{2}\left(-\langle\xi^{\prime}|x\rangle+\langle\xi|x^{\prime}\rangle\right)}e^{-\frac{1}{4}\left\|\left(x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}\right)-\left(x,\xi\right)\right\|_{g+g^{-1}}^{2}}

\displaystyle=e^{-i\frac{1}{2}\Omega\left(\left(x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}\right),\left(x,\xi\right)\right)}e^{-\frac{1}{4}\left\|\left(x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}\right)-\left(x,\xi\right)\right\|_{g+g^{-1}}^{2}}

∎

Lemma C.8
.

Let
\pi:E\oplus E^{*}\rightarrow E
denotes the projector on the
first component, and
\pi^{\circ}:\mathcal{S}\left(E\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(E\oplus E^{*}\right)

the pull back operator. We have

\mathcal{B}_{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}=\pi^{\frac{n}{4}}\mathcal{P}_{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}\pi^{\circ}

Démonstration
.

We compute the Schwartz kernel of both sides for
y\in E,\left(x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}\right)\in E\oplus E^{*}
.
We have
\langle\delta_{x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}}|\mathcal{B}_{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}\delta_{y}\rangle=\overline{\varphi_{x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}}^{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}\left(y\right)}

and

\displaystyle\langle\delta_{x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}}|\mathcal{P}_{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}\pi^{\circ}\delta_{y}\rangle

\displaystyle=\int e^{\frac{i}{2}\left(\xi^{\prime}+\xi\right)\left(x^{\prime}-x\right)-\frac{1}{4}\left(\left\|x^{\prime}-x\right\|^{2}+\left\|\xi-\xi^{\prime}\right\|^{2}\right)}\delta\left(x-y\right)\frac{dxd\xi}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}

\displaystyle=\left(2\pi\right)^{-n}e^{\frac{i}{2}\xi^{\prime}\left(x^{\prime}-y\right)-\frac{1}{4}\left\|x^{\prime}-y\right\|^{2}-\frac{1}{4}\left\|\xi^{\prime}\right\|^{2}}\int e^{\frac{i}{2}\xi\left(x^{\prime}-y\right)+\frac{1}{2}\xi.\xi^{\prime}-\frac{1}{4}\left\|\xi\right\|^{2}}d\xi

We use the Gaussian integral (
C.15
) with
\alpha=\frac{1}{4}
,

b=\frac{i}{2}\left(x^{\prime}-y\right)+\frac{1}{2}\xi^{\prime}
,

\frac{1}{4\alpha}\left\|b\right\|^{2}=\frac{1}{4}\left(\left\|\xi^{\prime}\right\|^{2}-\left\|x^{\prime}-y\right\|^{2}\right)+\frac{i}{2}\left(x^{\prime}-y\right)\xi^{\prime}

\displaystyle\langle\delta_{x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}}|\mathcal{P}_{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}\pi^{\circ}\delta_{y}\rangle

\displaystyle=\left(2\pi\right)^{-n}e^{\frac{i}{2}\xi^{\prime}\left(x^{\prime}-y\right)-\frac{1}{4}\left\|x^{\prime}-y\right\|^{2}-\frac{1}{4}\left\|\xi^{\prime}\right\|^{2}}\left(\frac{\pi}{\alpha}\right)^{\frac{n}{2}}e^{\frac{1}{4\alpha}\left\|b\right\|^{2}}

\displaystyle=\left(4\pi\right)^{\frac{n}{2}}\left(2\pi\right)^{-n}\pi^{\frac{n}{4}}\pi^{-\frac{n}{4}}e^{i\xi^{\prime}\left(x^{\prime}-y\right)-\frac{1}{2}\left\|x^{\prime}-y\right\|^{2}}

\displaystyle=\pi^{-\frac{n}{4}}\overline{\varphi_{x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}}^{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}\left(y\right)}

∎

C.3
Linear map
\phi:\left(E_{2},g_{2}\right)\rightarrow\left(E_{1},g_{1}\right)

Suppose
\phi:\left(E_{2},g_{2}\right)\rightarrow\left(E_{1},g_{1}\right)

is a linear invertible map between two metric spaces. Let

\Phi:=\phi^{-1}\oplus\phi^{*}:E_{1}\oplus E_{1}^{*}\rightarrow E_{2}\oplus E_{2}^{*}

be the induced map (i.e. pull back of differential forms
\Phi:T^{*}E_{1}\rightarrow T^{*}E_{2}
).
We have defined in (
C.8
),
\mathcal{B}_{E_{1}}:\mathcal{S}\left(E_{1}\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(E_{1}\oplus E_{1}^{*}\right)

and
\mathcal{B}_{E_{2}}:\mathcal{S}\left(E_{2}\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(E_{2}\oplus E_{2}^{*}\right)

where
\mathcal{B}_{E_{j}}
is either
\mathcal{B}_{\left(\mathrm{V}\right)}

or
\mathcal{B}_{\left(R\right)}
.

Lemma C.9
.

Suppose
\phi:\left(E_{2},g_{2}\right)\rightarrow\left(E_{1},g_{1}\right)

is a linear invertible map between two Euclidean vector spaces. We
have that

\phi^{\circ}=\Upsilon\left(\phi\right)\mathcal{B}_{E_{2}}^{\dagger}\Phi^{-\circ}\mathcal{B}_{E_{1}}

(C.16)

with

\Upsilon\left(\phi\right):=\left(\mathrm{det}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathrm{Id}+\left(\phi^{-1}\right)^{\dagger}\phi^{-1}\right)\right)\right)^{1/2}

(C.17)

where
\left(\phi^{-1}\right)^{\dagger}
is the metric-adjoint of

\phi^{-1}
defined by
\langle x_{1}|\left(\phi^{-1}\right)^{\dagger}x_{2}\rangle_{g_{1}}=\langle\phi^{-1}x_{1}|x_{2}\rangle_{g_{2}}

for any
x_{1}\in E_{1}
,
x_{2}\in E_{2}
.

Remark C.10
.

If
\phi
is an isometry then
\phi^{\dagger}\phi=\mathrm{Id}
and

\Upsilon\left(\phi\right)=1
.

Démonstration
.

We compute the Schwartz kernels. For
y^{\prime}\in E_{1},z^{\prime}\in E_{2}
,

\langle\delta_{z^{\prime}}|\mathcal{B}_{E_{2}}^{\dagger}\Phi^{-\circ}\mathcal{B}_{E_{1}}\delta_{y^{\prime}}\rangle=\langle\mathcal{B}_{E_{2}}\delta_{z^{\prime}}|\Phi^{-\circ}\mathcal{B}_{E_{1}}\delta_{y^{\prime}}\rangle

For
y\in E_{1},\eta\in E_{1}^{*}
,

\left(\mathcal{B}_{E_{1}}\delta_{y^{\prime}}\right)\left(y,\eta\right)=\langle\varphi_{y,\eta}^{\left(1\right)}|\delta_{y^{\prime}}\rangle=\pi^{-\frac{n}{4}}e^{-i\langle\eta|y^{\prime}-y\rangle}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\left\|y^{\prime}-y\right\|_{g_{1}}^{2}}

For
z\in E_{2},\xi\in E_{2}^{*}
,

\left(\Phi^{-\circ}\mathcal{B}_{E_{1}}\delta_{y^{\prime}}\right)\left(z,\xi\right)=\pi^{-\frac{n}{4}}e^{-i\langle\phi^{*-1}\xi|y^{\prime}-\phi z\rangle}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\left\|y^{\prime}-\phi z\right\|_{g_{1}}^{2}}

\left(\mathcal{B}_{E_{2}}\delta_{z^{\prime}}\right)\left(z,\xi\right)=\pi^{-\frac{n}{4}}e^{-i\langle\xi|z^{\prime}-z\rangle}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\left\|z^{\prime}-z\right\|_{g_{2}}^{2}}

Hence

\langle\mathcal{B}_{E_{2}}\delta_{z^{\prime}}|\Phi^{-\circ}\mathcal{B}_{E_{1}}\delta_{y^{\prime}}\rangle=\pi^{-\frac{n}{2}}\int e^{i\langle\xi|z^{\prime}-z\rangle}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\left\|z^{\prime}-z\right\|_{g_{2}}^{2}}e^{-i\langle\phi^{*-1}\xi|y^{\prime}-\phi z\rangle}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\left\|y^{\prime}-\phi z\right\|_{g_{1}}^{2}}\frac{dzd\xi}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}

We do the symplectic change of variables
