\left(z,\xi\right)\in E_{2}\oplus E_{2}^{*}
\rightarrow
\left(y,\eta\right)=\Phi^{-1}\left(z,\xi\right)=\left(\phi\left(z\right),\phi^{*^{-1}}\xi\right)\in\left(E_{1}\oplus E_{1}^{*}\right)
,
i.e.
\xi=\phi^{*}\eta
,
z=\phi^{-1}y
, and get

\displaystyle\langle\delta_{z^{\prime}}|\mathcal{B}_{E_{2}}^{\dagger}\Phi^{-\circ}\mathcal{B}_{E_{1}}\delta_{y^{\prime}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\pi^{-\frac{n}{2}}\int e^{i\langle\phi^{*}\eta|z^{\prime}-\phi^{-1}y\rangle}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\left\|z^{\prime}-\phi^{-1}y\right\|_{g_{2}}^{2}}e^{-i\langle\eta|y^{\prime}-y\rangle}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\left\|y^{\prime}-y\right\|_{g_{1}}^{2}}\frac{dyd\eta}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}

\displaystyle=\pi^{-\frac{n}{2}}\int e^{i\langle\eta|\phi z^{\prime}-y^{\prime}\rangle}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\left\|\phi^{-1}\left(\phi z^{\prime}-y\right)\right\|_{g_{2}}^{2}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\left\|y^{\prime}-y\right\|_{g_{1}}^{2}}\frac{dyd\eta}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}

We have

\int e^{i\langle\eta|\phi z^{\prime}-y^{\prime}\rangle}\frac{d\eta}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}=\delta\left(\phi z^{\prime}-y^{\prime}\right)=\langle\delta_{z^{\prime}}|\phi^{\circ}\delta_{y^{\prime}}\rangle

hence

\displaystyle\langle\delta_{z^{\prime}}|\mathcal{B}_{E_{2}}^{\dagger}\Phi^{-\circ}\mathcal{B}_{E_{1}}\delta_{y^{\prime}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\delta_{z^{\prime}}|\phi^{\circ}\delta_{y^{\prime}}\rangle\pi^{-\frac{n}{2}}\int e^{-\frac{1}{2}\left\|\phi^{-1}\left(y^{\prime}-y\right)\right\|_{g_{2}}^{2}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\left\|y^{\prime}-y\right\|_{g_{1}}^{2}}dy

\displaystyle=\langle\delta_{z^{\prime}}|\phi^{\circ}\delta_{y^{\prime}}\rangle\pi^{-\frac{n}{2}}\int_{E}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\left\|\phi^{-1}Y\right\|_{g_{2}}^{2}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\left\|Y\right\|_{g_{1}}^{2}}dY

\displaystyle=\langle\delta_{z^{\prime}}|\phi^{\circ}\delta_{y^{\prime}}\rangle\pi^{-\frac{n}{2}}\int e^{-\frac{1}{2}\langle Y|\left(\mathrm{Id}+\left(\phi^{-1}\right)^{\dagger}\phi^{-1}\right)Y\rangle}dY

\displaystyle=\langle\delta_{z^{\prime}}|\phi^{\circ}\delta_{y^{\prime}}\rangle\pi^{-\frac{n}{2}}\left(\frac{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}{\mathrm{det}\left(\mathrm{Id}+\left(\phi^{-1}\right)^{\dagger}\phi^{-1}\right)}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\langle\delta_{z^{\prime}}|\phi^{\circ}\delta_{y^{\prime}}\rangle\Upsilon\left(\phi\right)^{-1}

Giving (
C.16
).
∎

Later we will use the following Lemma, where the determinant is measured
with respect to local densities.

Lemma C.11
.

With
\Upsilon\left(\phi\right)
defined in (
C.17
), we
have

\Upsilon\left(\phi^{-1}\right)=\left|\mathrm{det}\phi\right|\Upsilon\left(\phi\right).

(C.18)

If
\phi=\phi_{1}\oplus\phi_{2}
on
E=E_{1}\overset{\perp}{\oplus}E_{2}
,
then

\Upsilon\left(\phi\right)=\Upsilon\left(\phi_{1}\right)\Upsilon\left(\phi_{2}\right).

(C.19)

Démonstration
.

Notice that
\mathrm{det}\left(\phi^{\dagger}\phi\right)=\left|\mathrm{det}\phi\right|^{2}
.
Then

\displaystyle\Upsilon\left(\phi\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{det}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathrm{Id}+\phi^{-1\dagger}\phi^{-1}\right)\right)=\left(\mathrm{det}\left(\phi^{\dagger}\phi\right)\right)^{-1}\mathrm{det}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left(\phi^{\dagger}\phi+\mathrm{Id}\right)\right)=\left|\mathrm{det}\phi\right|^{-2}\Upsilon\left(\phi^{-1}\right)^{2}

∎

Corollary C.12
.

If
E
is endowed with two metrics
g_{1},g_{2}
then

\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{S}\left(E\right)}=\Upsilon_{g_{2},g_{1}}\mathcal{B}_{g_{2}}^{\dagger_{g_{2}}}\mathcal{B}_{g_{1}}

with

\Upsilon_{g_{2},g_{1}}=\left(\mathrm{det}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathrm{Id}+\mathrm{Id}^{\dagger}\right)\right)\right)^{1/2}=\prod_{j=1}^{n}\left(\frac{1+I_{j}}{2}\right)^{1/2}

(C.20)

\left(I_{j}\right)_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
are inertial moments of

g_{2}
with respect to
g_{1}
i.e. in suitable coordinates,
g_{1}=\sum_{j}dy_{j}^{2}
,

g_{2}=\sum_{j}I_{j}dy_{j}^{2}
. Consequently

\mathcal{P}_{g_{1},g_{2}}:=\Upsilon_{g_{2},g_{1}}\mathcal{B}_{g_{1}}\mathcal{B}_{g_{2}}^{\dagger_{g_{2}}}\quad:\mathcal{S}\left(E\oplus E^{*}\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(E\oplus E^{*}\right)

is a projector, with
\mathrm{Im}\left(\mathcal{P}_{g_{1},g_{2}}\right)=\mathrm{Im}\left(\mathcal{B}_{g_{1}}\right)
,

\mathrm{Ker}\left(\mathcal{P}_{g_{1},g_{2}}\right)=\left(\mathrm{Im}\left(\mathcal{B}_{g_{2}}\right)\right)^{\perp}
.

Démonstration
.

We apply Lemma
C.9
with
\phi=\mathrm{Id}:\left(E,g_{2}\right)\rightarrow\left(E,g_{1}\right)

and
\Phi=\mathrm{Id}
. We have

\langle u|\left(\phi^{-1}\right)^{\dagger}v\rangle_{g_{1}}=\langle\phi^{-1}u|v\rangle_{g_{2}}=\langle u|v\rangle_{g_{2}}

In coordinates such that
g_{1}=\sum_{j}dy_{j}^{2}
,
g_{2}=\sum_{j}I_{j}dy_{j}^{2}
,

\left(\mathrm{Id}\right)_{j,k}^{\dagger}=\left(\phi^{-1}\right)_{j,k}^{\dagger}=I_{j}\delta_{j=k}

giving (
C.20
).
∎

C.4
Compatible triple
g,\Omega,J

Let us recall the definition of a
compatible triple

g,\Omega,J
. Let
F
be a vector space endowed with a symplectic
bilinear form
\Omega
and an Euclidean metric
g
. Let

J:=\check{g}^{-1}\check{\Omega}\quad:F\rightarrow F

(C.21)

with
\check{\Omega}:F\rightarrow F^{*}
,
\check{g}:F\rightarrow F^{*}

defined in Section
A
.

Definition C.13
.

We say that
\Omega,g
are compatible structures
on
F
if
J:=\check{g}^{-1}\check{\Omega}:F\rightarrow F
is an

almost complex structure

on
F
, i.e.

J^{2}=-\mathrm{Id}

(C.22)

.

Proposition C.14
.

From (
C.21
), we have that for any
u,v\in F

\displaystyle\Omega\left(u,v\right)=g\left(Ju,v\right).

(C.23)

If moreover (
C.22
) holds true, we have that for any
u,v\in F
,

g\left(Ju,Jv\right)=g\left(u,v\right),\qquad\Omega\left(Ju,Jv\right)=\Omega\left(u,v\right).

(C.24)

Démonstration
.

We have

g\left(Ju,v\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:def_J})}{=}\langle\check{g}\check{g}^{-1}\check{\Omega}u|v\rangle=\Omega\left(u,v\right).

Suppose
J^{2}=\check{g}^{-1}\check{\Omega}\check{g}^{-1}\check{\Omega}=-\mathrm{Id}

hence
\check{g}=-\check{\Omega}\check{g}^{-1}\check{\Omega}
. Then

g\left(Ju,Jv\right)=\langle\check{g}\check{g}^{-1}\check{\Omega}u|\check{g}^{-1}\check{\Omega}v\rangle=\langle-\check{\Omega}\check{g}^{-1}\check{\Omega}u|v\rangle=g\left(u,v\right)

\Omega\left(Ju,Jv\right)=\langle\check{\Omega}\check{g}^{-1}\check{\Omega}u|\check{g}^{-1}\check{\Omega}v\rangle=\langle-\check{\Omega}\check{g}^{-1}\check{\Omega}\check{g}^{-1}\check{\Omega}u|v\rangle=\Omega\left(u,v\right)

∎

Example C.15
.

Let
\left(E,g\right)
be a vector space with Euclidean metric
g
.
On
E\oplus E^{*}
, the canonical symplectic form
\Omega
in (
C.4
),
is compatible with the induced metric
\boldsymbol{g}:=g\oplus g^{-1}
.

Lemma C.16
.

Let
\left(F,\Omega,g\right)
be a vector space
with a symplectic structure
\Omega
and compatible metric
g
.
If
E\subset F
is a linear Lagrangian subspace, let
E^{\perp_{g}}

be the orthogonal subspace with respect to
g
. Then
E^{\perp_{g}}=J\left(E\right)

is also Lagrangian and
E=J\left(E^{\perp_{g}}\right)
. Hence we
have an isomorphism
\varPsi
that is symplectic (for
\Omega
)
and isometric (for
g
)

\varPsi:\begin{cases}F=E\oplus E^{\perp_{g}}&\rightarrow E\oplus E^{*}\\
\left(v,u\right)&\mapsto\left(v,\check{\Omega}u\right)\end{cases}

(C.25)

where
E\oplus E^{*}
is endowed with the canonical symplectic form
(
C.4
) and with the metric
\boldsymbol{g}=g\oplus g^{-1}
.

Démonstration
.

Let
v_{1},v_{2}\in E
Lagrangian. Then

g\left(Jv_{1},v_{2}\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:g_Omega})}{=}\Omega\left(Jv_{1},Jv_{2}\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:J_comp})}{=}\Omega\left(v_{1},v_{2}\right)\underset{(\mathrm{Lag.})}{=}0.

Considering dimensions,
\mathrm{dim}E=\mathrm{dim}E^{\perp_{g}}=\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{dim}F
,
we deduce
E^{\perp_{g}}=J\left(E\right)
and
E=J\left(E^{\perp_{g}}\right)
,
since
J^{2}=-\mathrm{Id}
. From compatibility
J
is a symplectic
map hence
E^{\perp_{g}}
is Lagrangian as well.
∎

C.5
Bergman projector on a symplectic vector space
\left(F,\Omega,g\right)

Let
\left(F,\Omega,g\right)
be a vector space with a symplectic
structure
\Omega
and compatible metric
g
.

Definition C.17
.

Let
\mathcal{P}_{F}:\mathcal{S}\left(F\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(F\right)

be the operator defined by its Schwartz kernel: for any
\rho,\rho^{\prime}\in F
,

\langle\delta_{\rho^{\prime}}|\mathcal{P}_{F}\delta_{\rho}\rangle=\exp\left(-\frac{i}{2}\Omega\left(\rho^{\prime},\rho\right)-\frac{1}{4}\left\|\rho^{\prime}-\rho\right\|_{g}^{2}\right).

(C.26)

Lemma C.18
.

\mathcal{P}_{F}
is an orthogonal projector in
L^{2}\left(F,\frac{d\rho}{\left(2\pi\right)^{n}}\right)
,
called the
Bergman projector
.

Démonstration
.

We choose any
E\subset F
Lagrangian linear subspace. Then
\mathcal{P}_{F}\underset{(\ref{eq:identif_F_E})}{=}\Psi^{\circ}\circ\mathcal{P}_{E\oplus E^{*},\left(\mathrm{R}\right)}\circ\Psi^{-\circ}

is conjugated to the Bergman projector in radial gauge defined in
(
C.14
).
∎

C.6
The metaplectic decomposition of
F\oplus F^{*}

Let
\left(F,\Omega\right)
be a linear symplectic space. Let us
denote
\boldsymbol{\Omega}
the canonical symplectic form on
F\oplus F^{*}

as in (
C.4
). Recall
\check{\Omega}:F\rightarrow F^{*}

defined in Section
A
and that
\check{\Omega}^{*}=-\check{\Omega}
.

Lemma C.19

(Metaplectic decomposition)
.

Let
\left(F,\Omega\right)
be a linear symplectic
space and

K:=\mathrm{graph}\left(\check{\Omega}\right),\quad N:=\mathrm{graph}\left(-\check{\Omega}\right).

(C.27)

We have

F\oplus F^{*}=K\overset{\perp_{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}}{\oplus}N.

(C.28)

where the right hand side is an
\boldsymbol{\Omega}
-orthogonal
decomposition into linear symplectic subspaces. See Figure
C.1
.
Explicitly for any
\left(x,\xi\right)\in F\oplus F^{*}
,
\left(x,\xi\right)=\left(\nu,\check{\Omega}\left(\nu\right)\right)+\left(\zeta,-\check{\Omega}\left(\zeta\right)\right)\in K\oplus N

is given by

\displaystyle\nu

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(x+\check{\Omega}^{-1}\left(\xi\right)\right)

(C.29)

\displaystyle\zeta

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(x-\check{\Omega}^{-1}\left(\xi\right)\right)

(C.30)

Figure
C.1:
Picture for the orthogonal decomposition

F\oplus F^{*}=K\overset{\perp}{\oplus}N
in (
C.28
).

Remark C.20
.

The metaplectic decomposition (
C.28
) is central in this
paper. It has also been used in
[
13
, Prop.6]
and in

[
18
, Prop.2.2.9]
.

Démonstration
.

If
\left(x,\xi\right)=\left(x,\check{\Omega}\left(x\right)\right)\in\mathrm{graph}\left(\check{\Omega}\right)

and
\left(x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}\right)=\left(x^{\prime},-\check{\Omega}\left(x^{\prime}\right)\right)\in\mathrm{graph}\left(-\check{\Omega}\right)

then

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Omega}\left(\left(x,\xi\right),\left(x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\langle\xi|x^{\prime}\rangle-\langle x|\xi^{\prime}\rangle=\langle\check{\Omega}\left(x\right)|x^{\prime}\rangle+\langle x|\check{\Omega}\left(x^{\prime}\right)\rangle

\displaystyle=\Omega\left(x,x^{\prime}\right)+\Omega\left(x^{\prime},x\right)=0

Moreover if
\left(x,\xi\right)=\left(x,\check{\Omega}\left(x\right)\right)=\left(x,-\check{\Omega}\left(x\right)\right)\in\mathrm{graph}\left(\check{\Omega}\right)\cap\mathrm{graph}\left(-\check{\Omega}\right)

then
\check{\Omega}\left(x\right)=-\check{\Omega}\left(x\right)

hence
\check{\Omega}\left(x\right)=0
hence
x=0
. This orthogonality
and transversality implies that
\mathrm{graph}\left(\check{\Omega}\right),\mathrm{graph}\left(-\check{\Omega}\right)

are symplectic. Let us prove (
C.29
). We write

\begin{cases}x&=\nu+\zeta\\
\xi&=\check{\Omega}\left(\nu\right)-\check{\Omega}\left(\zeta\right)=\check{\Omega}\left(\nu-\zeta\right)\end{cases}\Leftrightarrow\begin{cases}\nu&=\frac{1}{2}\left(x+\check{\Omega}^{-1}\left(\xi\right)\right)\\
\zeta&=\frac{1}{2}\left(x-\check{\Omega}^{-1}\left(\xi\right)\right)\end{cases}

(C.31)

∎

Lemma C.21
.

Let
\left(F,\Omega,g\right)
be a vector space
with a symplectic structure
\Omega
and a compatible metric
g
.
Then (
C.28
) is orthogonal for the metric
\boldsymbol{g=}g\oplus g^{-1}

as well. The maps

\sqrt{2}\pi_{K}:\left(x,\check{\Omega}\left(x\right)\right)\in\left(K,\boldsymbol{\Omega},\boldsymbol{g}\right)\rightarrow x\in\left(F,\Omega,g\right)

(C.32)

\sqrt{2}\pi_{N}:\left(x,\check{\Omega}^{*}\left(x\right)\right)\in\left(N,\boldsymbol{\Omega},\boldsymbol{g}\right)\rightarrow x\in\left(F,-\Omega,g\right)

(C.33)

are isomorphism for the respective bilinear forms.

Démonstration
.

K\overset{\perp}{\oplus}N
is also orthogonal for the metric
\boldsymbol{g}

because

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{g}\left(\left(x,\xi\right),\left(x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=g\left(x,x^{\prime}\right)+g^{-1}\left(\xi,\xi^{\prime}\right)=g\left(x,x^{\prime}\right)+g^{-1}\left(\check{\Omega}\left(x\right),-\check{\Omega}\left(x^{\prime}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=g\left(x,x^{\prime}\right)-g^{-1}\left(\check{\Omega}\left(x\right),\check{\Omega}\left(x^{\prime}\right)\right)=0

because
g^{-1}\left(\check{\Omega}\left(x\right),\check{\Omega}\left(x^{\prime}\right)\right)=g\left(x,x^{\prime}\right)

from compatibility between
\Omega
and
g
.

If
\left(x,\xi\right)=\left(x,\check{\Omega}\left(x\right)\right)\in K

and
\left(x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}\right)=\left(x^{\prime},\check{\Omega}\left(x^{\prime}\right)\right)\in K

then

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Omega}\left(\left(x,\xi\right),\left(x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\langle\xi|x^{\prime}\rangle-\langle x|\xi^{\prime}\rangle=\langle\check{\Omega}\left(x\right)|x^{\prime}\rangle-\langle x|\check{\Omega}\left(x^{\prime}\right)\rangle=\langle\check{\Omega}\left(x\right)|x^{\prime}\rangle-\langle\check{\Omega}^{*}\left(x\right)|x^{\prime}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\check{\Omega}\left(x\right)|x^{\prime}\rangle+\langle\check{\Omega}\left(x\right)|x^{\prime}\rangle=2\Omega\left(x,x^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{g}\left(\left(x,\xi\right),\left(x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=g\left(x,x^{\prime}\right)+g^{-1}\left(\xi,\xi^{\prime}\right)=g\left(x,x^{\prime}\right)+g^{-1}\left(\check{\Omega}\left(x\right),\check{\Omega}\left(x^{\prime}\right)\right)=2g\left(x,x^{\prime}\right)

and a similar computation on
N
, if
\check{\Omega}
is replaced
by
-\check{\Omega}
.
∎

Let
\mathcal{B}_{F}:\mathcal{S}\left(F\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(F\oplus F^{*}\right)

the Bargman transform in radial Gauge. Let
\mathcal{P}_{F\oplus F^{*}}:=\mathcal{B}_{F}\mathcal{B}_{F}^{\dagger}
.
Let
\mathcal{P}_{K},\mathcal{P}_{N}
defined as in (
C.26
)
for the respective spaces
\left(K,\Omega_{K},g_{K}\right)
,
\left(N,\Omega_{N},g_{N}\right)

defined in (
C.27
) with metrics induced from
\boldsymbol{\Omega},\boldsymbol{g}
.
From (
C.28
) we have the natural identification

\mathcal{S}\left(F\oplus F^{*}\right)=\mathcal{S}\left(K\oplus N\right)=\mathcal{S}\left(K\right)\otimes\mathcal{S}\left(N\right).

Lemma C.22
.

We have

\mathcal{P}_{F\oplus F^{*}}=\mathcal{P}_{K}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{N}

(C.34)

Démonstration
.

Since
\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{F\oplus F^{*}}=\Omega_{K}\oplus\Omega_{N}

and
\boldsymbol{g}=g_{K}\oplus g_{N}
, (
C.34
) follows
from the expression of the kernels (
C.14
)
and (
C.26
) that coincide for both sides.
∎

Lemma C.23
.

From the linear maps
\pi_{K},\pi_{N}

in (
C.32
), we have
L^{2}-
unitary operators

U_{K}:=\left(\sqrt{2}\pi_{K}\right)^{\circ}\,:\mathrm{Im}\left(\mathcal{P}_{F}\right)\rightarrow\mathrm{Im}\left(\mathcal{P}_{K}\right)

(C.35)

U_{N}:=\left(\sqrt{2}\pi_{N}\right)^{\circ}\,:\mathrm{Im}\left(C\mathcal{P}_{F}\mathcal{C}\right)\rightarrow\mathrm{Im}\left(\mathcal{P}_{N}\right)

where
\mathcal{C}:u\in\mathcal{S}\left(F\right)\rightarrow\overline{u}\in\mathcal{S}\left(F\right)

is the conjugation operator.

Démonstration
.

This is a direct consequence of Lemma
C.21
and expression
(
C.26
).
∎

C.7
Metaplectic decomposition
of a linear symplectic map

In this section, let

\Phi:\left(F_{1},\Omega_{1}\right)\rightarrow\left(F_{2},\Omega_{2}\right)

a linear invertible symplectic map and
g_{j}
a compatible metric
on
F_{j}
for
j=1,2
.

Let us first give a definition of metaplectic operator that will be
useful. The metaplectic correction
\Upsilon\left(\Phi\right)>0

has been defined in (
C.17
). The reader may consult
[
45
, section 13.2]

for a general reference about metaplectic operators.

Definition C.24
.

•

We define the
metaplectic operator (or quantization) of
\Phi

by

\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi\right):=\left(\Upsilon\left(\Phi\right)\right)^{1/2}\mathcal{P}_{F_{2}}\Phi^{-\circ}\mathcal{P}_{F_{1}}\quad:\mathrm{Im}\left(\mathcal{P}_{F_{1}}\right)\rightarrow\mathrm{Im}\left(\mathcal{P}_{F_{2}}\right)

(C.36)

with the metaplectic correction
\Upsilon\left(\Phi\right)>0
defined
in (
C.17
).

•

If
E_{1}\subset F_{1}
,
E_{2}\subset F_{2}
are Lagrangian linear
subspace, and using the identification (
C.25
), we
define

\displaystyle\mathrm{Op}\left(\Phi\right):

\displaystyle=\left(\Upsilon\left(\Phi\right)\right)^{1/2}\mathcal{B}_{E_{2}}^{\dagger}\Phi^{-\circ}\mathcal{B}_{E_{1}}\quad:\mathcal{S}\left(E_{1}\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(E_{2}\right)

(C.37)

that is conjugated to
\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi\right)_{/\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{B}_{E_{1}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{B}_{E_{2}}}

since

\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi\right)=\mathcal{B}_{E_{2}}\mathrm{Op}\left(\Phi\right)\mathcal{B}_{E_{1}}^{\dagger}.

(C.38)

Remark C.25
.

We will show in Proposition
C.29
that
\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi\right):L^{2}\left(F_{1}\right)\rightarrow L^{2}\left(F_{2}\right)

is
L^{2}-
unitary (hence
\mathrm{Op}\left(\Phi\right):L^{2}\left(E_{1}\right)\rightarrow L^{2}\left(E_{2}\right)

is also unitary).

Let

\tilde{\Phi}:=\Phi^{-1}\oplus\Phi^{*}:\quad F_{2}\oplus F_{2}^{*}\rightarrow F_{1}\oplus F_{1}^{*}

(C.39)

the induced map on cotangent spaces. Since
\Phi
is symplectic
we have that
\tilde{\Phi}
preserves the decompositions (
C.28
),

F_{j}\oplus F_{j}^{*}=K_{j}\oplus N_{j}
, with
j=1,2
and we denote
its components by

\tilde{\Phi}=\Phi_{K}\oplus\Phi_{N}.

(C.40)

Beware that
\Phi_{K},\Phi_{N}
are conjugated to
\Phi^{-1}
, i.e.

\Phi^{-1}\pi_{N_{2}}=\pi_{N_{1}}\Phi_{N}:N_{2}\rightarrow F_{1}

and
\Phi^{-1}\pi_{K_{2}}=\pi_{K_{1}}\Phi_{K}:K_{2}\rightarrow F_{1}
.

Theorem C.26
.

[
13
]
[
18
]
We
have

\mathcal{B}_{F_{1}}\Phi^{\circ}\mathcal{B}_{F_{2}}^{\dagger}=\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\tilde{\Phi}\right)=\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi_{K}\right)\otimes\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi_{N}\right)\quad:\mathrm{Im}\left(\mathcal{P}_{F_{2}\oplus F_{2}^{*}}\right)\rightarrow\mathrm{Im}\left(\mathcal{P}_{F_{1}\oplus F_{1}^{*}}\right).

(C.41)

Démonstration
.

We have

\Upsilon\left(\tilde{\Phi}\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:Phi_phi_minus},\ref{eq:product})}{=}\Upsilon\left(\Phi^{-1}\right)\Upsilon\left(\Phi^{*}\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:dphi_inv})}{=}\left(\Upsilon\left(\Phi\right)\right)^{2}.

(C.42)

Hence

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{F_{1}}\Phi^{\circ}\mathcal{B}_{F_{2}}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:def_Upsilon},,\ref{eq:Bergman_projector})}{=}\Upsilon\left(\Phi\right)\mathcal{P}_{F_{1}\oplus F_{1}^{*}}\tilde{\Phi}^{-\circ}\mathcal{P}_{F_{2}\oplus F_{2}^{*}}

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:def_op_tilde})}{=}\Upsilon\left(\Phi\right)\left(\Upsilon\left(\tilde{\Phi}\right)\right)^{-1/2}\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\tilde{\Phi}\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:upsilon_phi_tilde})}{=}\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\tilde{\Phi}\right),

and

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\tilde{\Phi}\right)

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:def_op_tilde})}{=}\left(\Upsilon\left(\tilde{\Phi}\right)\right)^{1/2}\mathcal{P}_{F_{2}\oplus F_{2}^{*}}\tilde{\Phi}^{-\circ}\mathcal{P}_{F_{1}\oplus F_{1}^{*}}

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:phi_tilde},\ref{eq:PKNB},\ref{eq:product})}{=}\left(\Upsilon\left(\Phi_{K}\right)\Upsilon\left(\Phi_{N}\right)\right)^{1/2}\left(\mathcal{P}_{K_{1}}^{\dagger}\Phi_{K}^{-\circ}\mathcal{P}_{K_{2}}\right)\otimes\left(\mathcal{P}_{N_{1}}^{\dagger}\Phi_{N}^{-\circ}\mathcal{P}_{N_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:def_op_tilde})}{=}\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi_{K}\right)\otimes\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi_{N}\right).

∎

Remark C.27
.

Lemma
C.26
is central to the analysis in
this paper. It is a factorization formula for the map
\Phi
and
somehow gives a square root of
\Phi^{\circ}
.

Proposition C.28
.

For any linear symplectic maps
\Phi_{2,1}:\left(F_{1},\Omega_{1}\right)\rightarrow\left(F_{2},\Omega_{2}\right)
,

\Phi_{3,2}:\left(F_{2},\Omega_{2}\right)\rightarrow\left(F_{3},\Omega_{3}\right)

with compatible metrics
g_{j}
on
F_{j}
,
j=1,2,3
, we have

\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi_{3,2}\Phi_{2,1}\right)=\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi_{3,2}\right)\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi_{2,1}\right).

(C.43)

Démonstration
.

We have

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\left(\Phi_{3,2}\Phi_{2,1}\right)_{K}\right)\otimes\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\left(\Phi_{3,2}\Phi_{2,1}\right)_{N}\right)

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:decomp_phi})}{=}\mathcal{B}_{F_{3}}\left(\Phi_{3,2}\Phi_{2,1}\right)^{-\circ}\mathcal{B}_{F_{1}}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{B}_{F_{3}}\Phi_{3,2}^{-\circ}\Phi_{2,1}^{-\circ}\mathcal{B}_{F_{1}}^{\dagger}\underset{(\ref{eq:resol_ident_bargamnn})}{=}\left(\mathcal{B}_{F_{3}}\Phi_{3,2}^{-\circ}\mathcal{B}_{F_{2}}^{\dagger}\right)\left(\mathcal{B}_{F_{2}}\Phi_{2,1}^{-\circ}\mathcal{B}_{F_{1}}^{\dagger}\right)

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:decomp_phi})}{=}\left(\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\left(\Phi_{3,2}\right)_{K}\right)\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\left(\Phi_{2,1}\right)_{K}\right)\right)\otimes\left(\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\left(\Phi_{3,2}\right)_{N}\right)\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\left(\Phi_{2,1}\right)_{N}\right)\right)

Considering each factor gives (
C.43
).
∎

Proposition C.29
.

The operator
\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi\right):\mathrm{Im}\left(\mathcal{P}_{F_{1}}\right)\rightarrow\mathrm{Im}\left(\mathcal{P}_{F_{2}}\right)

defined in (
C.36
) is
L^{2}-
unitary.

Remark C.30
.

Since
\mathcal{B}_{E_{j}}:L^{2}\left(E_{j}\right)\rightarrow\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{B}_{E_{j}}=\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{P}_{F_{j}}

is unitary this implies that
\mathrm{Op}\left(\Phi\right):L^{2}\left(E_{1}\right)\rightarrow L^{2}\left(E_{2}\right)

is also unitary.

Démonstration
.

We have
\Upsilon\left(\mathrm{Id}\right)=1
hence
\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi\right)\left(\mathrm{Id}\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:def_op_tilde})}{=}\mathcal{P}=\mathrm{Id}_{/\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{P}}
.
Also

\Upsilon\left(\Phi^{-1}\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:dphi_inv})}{=}\left|\mathrm{det}\Phi\right|\Upsilon\left(\Phi\right)=\Upsilon\left(\Phi\right).

(C.44)

We have

\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi^{-1}\right)\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:homom})}{=}\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\mathrm{Id}\right)=\mathrm{Id}_{/\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{P}},

so
\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi\right)^{-1}=\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi^{-1}\right)

and

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi\right)^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:def_op_tilde})}{=}\left(\Upsilon\left(\Phi\right)\right)^{1/2}\mathcal{P}\Phi^{\circ}\mathcal{P}\underset{(\ref{eq:ups_inv})}{=}\left(\Upsilon\left(\Phi^{-1}\right)\right)^{1/2}\mathcal{P}\left(\Phi^{-1}\right)^{-\circ}\mathcal{P}

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:def_op_tilde})}{=}\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi^{-1}\right)=\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi\right)^{-1}.

∎

The next lemma considers a special case for the symplectic map
\Phi
.

Lemma C.31
.

[
18
, proof of Prop 4.3.1 p.79]
Let

\left(E_{1},g_{1}\right)
,
\left(E_{2},g_{2}\right)
be Euclidean
vector spaces, and
\phi:E_{2}\rightarrow E_{1}
an invertible linear
map. We denote
\phi^{\circ}:\mathcal{S}\left(E_{1}\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(E_{2}\right)

the pull-back operator and
\Phi:=\phi^{-1}\oplus\phi^{*}:E_{1}\oplus E_{1}^{*}\rightarrow E_{2}\oplus E_{2}^{*}

the induced symplectic map on cotangent spaces.
\mathrm{Op}\left(\Phi\right)

has been defined in (
C.37
) and
\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi\right)

in (
C.36
). We have

\mathrm{Op}\left(\Phi\right)=\left|\mathrm{det}\phi\right|^{1/2}\phi^{\circ}.

(C.45)

\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi\right)=\left|\mathrm{det}\phi\right|^{1/2}\mathcal{B}_{E_{2}}\phi^{\circ}\mathcal{B}_{E_{1}}^{\dagger}

(C.46)

Démonstration
.

We have

\displaystyle\Upsilon\left(\Phi\right)

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:product})}{=}\Upsilon\left(\phi\right)\Upsilon\left(\phi^{-1}\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:dphi_inv})}{=}\left|\mathrm{det}\phi\right|\left(\Upsilon\left(\phi\right)\right)^{2}

(C.47)

Then

\mathrm{Op}\left(\Phi\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:def_Op})}{=}\left(\Upsilon\left(\Phi\right)\right)^{1/2}\mathcal{B}_{E_{2}}^{\dagger}\Phi^{-\circ}\mathcal{B}_{E_{1}}\underset{(\ref{eq:def_Upsilon})}{=}\left(\Upsilon\left(\Phi\right)\right)^{1/2}\left(\Upsilon\left(\phi\right)\right)^{-1}\phi^{\circ}\underset{(\ref{eq:dPhi_dphi})}{=}\left|\mathrm{det}\phi\right|^{1/2}\phi^{\circ}

∎

C.8
Symplectic spinors

The previous constructions correspond to what is sometimes called

symplectic spinors

in the literature. We summarize what we have obtained and comment
on this.

We start from a linear symplectic space
\left(F,\Omega\right)
.
We have the decomposition
F\oplus F^{*}=K\overset{\perp_{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}}{\oplus}N

in (
C.28
). We take a
\Omega-
compatible metric
g
on

F
, so that we can define
\mathcal{B}_{F}:L^{2}\left(F\right)\rightarrow\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{B}_{F}\subset L^{2}\left(F\oplus F^{*}\right)

in radial Gauge from (
C.8
), but also the operators

\mathcal{P}_{F},\mathcal{P}_{F\oplus F*},\mathcal{P}_{K},\mathcal{P}_{N}
.

Recall from Lemma
C.23
, we have
L^{2}-
unitary
isomorphisms

U_{K}:\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{P}_{F}\rightarrow\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{P}_{K},\quad U_{N}:\mathrm{Im}\left(\mathcal{C}\mathcal{P}_{F}\mathcal{C}\right)\rightarrow\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{P}_{N},

and from (
C.34
), we have a
L^{2}-
unitary isomorphism

\mathcal{B}_{F}:L^{2}\left(F\right)\rightarrow\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{P}_{K}\otimes\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{P}_{N}.

This suggests to give the following definition and Theorem.

Definition C.32
.

For a linear symplectic space
\left(F,\Omega\right)
with
\Omega-
compatible
metric
g
, we define the space of
symplectic spinors

\mathrm{Spin}_{\pm}\left(F\right)

by

\mathrm{Spin}_{+}\left(F\right):=\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{P}_{F}\subset L^{2}\left(F\right),\qquad\mathrm{Spin}_{-}\left(F\right):=\mathrm{Im}\left(\mathcal{C}\mathcal{P}_{F}\mathcal{C}\right)\subset L^{2}\left(F\right).

We define the
L^{2}-
unitary operator

U_{\mathrm{spin}}:=\left(U_{K}^{\dagger}\otimes U_{N}^{\dagger}\right)\circ\mathcal{B}_{F}\quad:L^{2}\left(F\right)\rightarrow\mathrm{Spin}_{+}\left(F\right)\otimes\mathrm{Spin}_{-}\left(F\right).

(C.48)

Theorem C.33
.

Let
\Phi:\left(F_{1},\Omega_{1}\right)\rightarrow\left(F_{2},\Omega_{2}\right)

a linear invertible symplectic map. We have the commutative diagram
with unitary operators

\textstyle{L^{2}\left(F_{1}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi^{-\circ}}
\scriptstyle{U_{\mathrm{spin}}}
\textstyle{L^{2}\left(F_{2}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{U_{\mathrm{spin}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Spin}_{+}\left(F_{1}\right)\otimes\mathrm{Spin}_{-}\left(F_{1}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi\right)\otimes\left(\mathcal{C}\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi\right)\mathcal{C}\right)}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Spin}_{+}\left(F_{2}\right)\otimes\mathrm{Spin}_{-}\left(F_{2}\right)}

(C.49)

Remark C.34
.

The operator
\Phi^{-\circ}
in the upper line is the push forward
of the symplectic map and can be interpreted as the “classical dynamics”.
The lower line is the product of two metaplectic operators or quantum
operators. So this diagram shows that

•

In Eq.(
C.48
), the classical space
L^{2}\left(F\right)

is identified as the product of two quantum spinor spaces.

•

In Eq. (
C.49
), the “classical dynamics” can be
factorized as the product of the quantum dynamics times its complex
conjugation.

Démonstration
.

Since
\Phi\pi_{N_{1}}=\pi_{N_{2}}\Phi_{N}^{-1}:N_{1}\rightarrow F_{2}
,
we have
\Phi^{\circ}=U_{N_{1}}^{\dagger}\Phi_{N}^{-\circ}U_{N_{2}}=U_{K_{1}}^{\dagger}\Phi_{K}^{-\circ}U_{K_{2}}:L^{2}\left(F_{2}\right)\rightarrow L^{2}\left(F_{1}\right)
.
From Lemma
C.23
, we have

\mathcal{C}\mathcal{P}_{F}\mathcal{C}=U_{N}^{\dagger}\mathcal{P}_{N}U_{N}.

Hence

\displaystyle U_{1}^{\dagger}\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi_{N}\right)U_{N_{2}}

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:def_op_tilde})}{=}\left(\Upsilon\left(\Phi\right)\right)^{1/2}U_{N_{1}}^{\dagger}\mathcal{P}_{N_{1}}\Phi_{N}^{-\circ}\mathcal{P}_{N_{2}}U_{N_{2}}

\displaystyle=\left(\Upsilon\left(\Phi\right)\right)^{1/2}U_{N_{1}}^{\dagger}\mathcal{P}_{N_{1}}U_{N_{1}}\Phi^{\circ}U_{N_{2}}^{\dagger}\mathcal{P}_{N_{2}}U_{N_{2}}=\left(\Upsilon\left(\Phi\right)\right)^{1/2}\mathcal{C}\mathcal{P}_{F_{1}}\mathcal{C}\Phi^{\circ}\mathcal{C}\mathcal{P}_{F_{2}}\mathcal{C}

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:ups_inv})}{=}\mathcal{C}\left(\Upsilon\left(\Phi^{-1}\right)\right)^{1/2}\mathcal{P}_{F_{1}}\Phi^{\circ}\mathcal{P}_{F_{2}}\mathcal{C}\underset{(\ref{eq:def_op_tilde})}{=}\mathcal{C}\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi^{-1}\right)\mathcal{C}

and similarly
U_{K_{1}}^{\dagger}\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi_{K}\right)U_{K_{2}}=\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi^{-1}\right)
.
So we get

\displaystyle\Phi^{\circ}

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:decomp_phi})}{=}\mathcal{B}_{F_{1}}^{\dagger}\left(\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi_{K}\right)\otimes\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi_{N}\right)\right)\mathcal{B}_{F_{2}}\qquad:L^{2}\left(F_{2}\right)\rightarrow L^{2}\left(F_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{B}_{F_{1}}^{\dagger}\left(\left(U_{K_{1}}\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi^{-1}\right)U_{K_{2}}^{\dagger}\right)\otimes\left(U_{N_{1}}\mathcal{C}\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi^{-1}\right)\mathcal{C}U_{N_{2}}^{\dagger}\right)\right)\mathcal{B}_{F_{2}}

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:def_Uspin})}{=}U_{\mathrm{spin}}^{\dagger}\left(\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi^{-1}\right)\otimes\left(\mathcal{C}\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi^{-1}\right)\mathcal{C}\right)\right)U_{\mathrm{spin}}

giving (
C.49
).
∎

C.9
Some useful decompositions

Here we give a relation that is simple here in the linear setting
and is used in the main text as an approximation, in the proof of
Theorem
3.15
. Let us denote
\tilde{F}=F\oplus F^{*}\underset{(\ref{eq:FF*KN})}{=}K\oplus N

and
\exp:T\tilde{F}\rightarrow\tilde{F}
the exponential map. Now
we restrict the base to
K\subset\tilde{F}
and consider
N\subset T_{K}\tilde{F}

as a sub-bundle
N\rightarrow K
of
T_{K}\tilde{F}\rightarrow K
.
We denote
\exp_{N}:N\rightarrow\tilde{F}
the exponential map that
is an isomorphism.

As before, we have the operators
\mathcal{B}_{F}:\mathcal{S}\left(F\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(\tilde{F}\right)
,

\mathcal{B}_{F}^{\dagger}:\mathcal{S}\left(\tilde{F}\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(F\right)
,

\widetilde{\exp_{N}^{\circ}}:\mathcal{S}\left(\tilde{F}\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(N\right)
,

\widetilde{\left(\exp_{N}^{-1}\right)^{\circ}}:\mathcal{S}\left(N\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(\tilde{F}\right)
.

Let
\Phi:F\rightarrow F
be a linear symplectic map. We have
\Phi^{\circ}:\mathcal{S}\left(F\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(F\right)
.
The map
\tilde{\Phi}:\tilde{F}\rightarrow\tilde{F}
that can be
seen as the bundle map
\tilde{\Phi}_{N}:N\rightarrow N
over
\Phi_{K}
.
Then
\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{N}\right):\mathcal{S}\left(N\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(N\right)

is a bundle map over
\Phi_{K}
. The metaplectic correction
\left(\Upsilon\left(\Phi_{K}\right)\right)^{1/2}>0

has been defined in (
C.17
) and in fact we have
\Upsilon\left(\Phi_{K}\right)=\Upsilon\left(\Phi_{N}\right)=\Upsilon\left(\Phi\right)
.

Lemma C.35
.

We have

\Phi^{\circ}=\mathcal{B}_{F}^{\dagger}\widetilde{\left(\exp_{N}^{-1}\right)^{\circ}}\left(\Upsilon\left(\Phi_{K}\right)\right)^{1/2}\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{N}\right)\widetilde{\exp_{N}^{\circ}}\mathcal{B}_{F}.

(C.50)

Equivalently

\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\tilde{\Phi}\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:decomp_phi})}{=}\mathcal{P}_{F}\widetilde{\left(\exp_{N}^{-1}\right)^{\circ}}\left(\Upsilon\left(\Phi_{K}\right)\right)^{1/2}\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{N}\right)\widetilde{\exp_{N}^{\circ}}\mathcal{P}_{F}.

(C.51)

Remark C.36
.

As the proof belows shows, the metaplectic correction
\left(\Upsilon\left(\Phi_{K}\right)\right)^{1/2}>0

in (
C.50
) is due to the fact that
\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{N}\right)

over
\Phi_{K}
does not preserves the space
\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{P}_{K}
.

Démonstration
.

We have

\displaystyle\Phi^{\circ}

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:resol_ident_bargamnn})}{=}\mathcal{B}_{F}^{\dagger}\mathcal{B}_{F}\Phi^{\circ}\mathcal{B}_{F}^{\dagger}\mathcal{B}_{F}\underset{(\ref{eq:decomp_phi})}{=}\mathcal{B}_{F}^{\dagger}\left(\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi_{K}\right)\otimes\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi_{N}\right)\right)\mathcal{B}_{F}

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:def_op_tilde})}{=}\mathcal{B}_{F}^{\dagger}\left(\left(\left(\Upsilon\left(\Phi_{K}\right)\right)^{1/2}\mathcal{P}_{K}\Phi_{K}^{-\circ}\mathcal{P}_{K}\right)\otimes\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi_{N}\right)\right)\mathcal{B}_{F}

We use that
\mathcal{B}_{F}\underset{(\ref{eq:Bergman_projector},\ref{eq:resol_ident_bargamnn})}{=}\mathcal{P}_{F}\mathcal{B}_{F}\underset{(\ref{eq:PKNB})}{=}\left(\mathcal{P}_{K}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{N}\right)\mathcal{B}_{F}

and similarly
\mathcal{B}_{F}^{\dagger}=\mathcal{B}_{F}^{\dagger}\left(\mathcal{P}_{K}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{N}\right)
.
Hence we can remove the operators
\mathcal{P}_{K}
and get

\displaystyle\Phi^{\circ}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{B}_{F}^{\dagger}\left(\left(\Upsilon\left(\Phi_{K}\right)\right)^{1/2}\Phi_{K}^{-\circ}\otimes\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi_{N}\right)\right)\mathcal{B}_{F}

\displaystyle=\left(\Upsilon\left(\Phi_{K}\right)\right)^{1/2}\mathcal{B}_{F}^{\dagger}\widetilde{\exp_{N}^{\circ}}^{-1}\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{N}\right)\widetilde{\exp_{N}^{\circ}}\mathcal{B}_{F}.

where the last line expresses that
\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{N}\right):\mathcal{S}\left(N\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(N\right)

is a bundle map over
\Phi_{K}
. Using (
C.41
) and
(
C.12
) we deduce (
C.51
).
∎

In the next Lemma, we use the (twisted) operator
\widetilde{\exp^{\circ}}:\mathcal{S}\left(\tilde{F}\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(T\tilde{F}\right)
.
We also use the restriction operators
r_{0}:\mathcal{S}\left(T\tilde{F}\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(\tilde{F}\right)
,

r_{/K}:\mathcal{S}\left(T\tilde{F}\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(T_{K}\tilde{F}\right)
,

r_{N}:\mathcal{S}\left(T_{K}\tilde{F}\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(N\right)
.
We have

\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{S}\left(\tilde{F}\right)}=r_{0}\widetilde{\exp^{\circ}}=\widetilde{\left(\exp_{N}^{-1}\right)^{\circ}}r_{N}r_{/K}\widetilde{\exp^{\circ}},

where the second equality can be seen as a generalization of the first
equality for the respective decompositions
\tilde{F}=\left\{0\right\}\oplus\tilde{F}=K\oplus N
.
More generally we have the following Lemma.

Lemma C.37
.

For the symplectic map
\tilde{\Phi}\underset{(\ref{eq:phi_tilde})}{=}\Phi_{K}\oplus\Phi_{N}:\tilde{F}\rightarrow\tilde{F}
,
using
\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi\right):\mathcal{S}\left(T\tilde{F}\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(T\tilde{F}\right)

and
\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}_{/K}\left(\Phi\right):\mathcal{S}\left(T_{K}\tilde{F}\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(T_{K}\tilde{F}\right)

as bundle map operators, we have the decomposition

r_{0}\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi\right)\widetilde{\exp^{\circ}}=\widetilde{\left(\exp_{N}^{-1}\right)^{\circ}}r_{N}\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}_{/K}\left(\Phi\right)r_{/K}\widetilde{\exp^{\circ}}.

(C.52)

C.10
Taylor operators
T_{k}
on
\mathcal{S}\left(E\right)

Let
E
an Euclidean vector space,
n=\mathrm{dim}E
. If
\left(e_{1},\ldots e_{n}\right)

is a basis of
E
, we write
x=\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}e_{i}\in E
. A
polynomial on
E
is
P\left(x\right):=p\left(x_{1},\ldots x_{n}\right)

with
p\in\mathbb{C}\left[x_{1},\ldots x_{n}\right]
a polynomial
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. For
\alpha=\left(\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{n}\right)\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
,
we write
x^{\alpha}:=x_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}\ldots x_{n}^{\alpha_{n}}

a monomial of degree
\left|\alpha\right|:=\alpha_{1}+\ldots\alpha_{n}
.
For
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we denote
\mathrm{Pol}_{k}\left(E\right)

the space of
homogeneous polynomials

of degree
k
, that is independent on the basis.
P\in\mathrm{Pol}_{k}\left(E\right)

can be written
P\left(x\right)=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k},\left|\alpha\right|=k}P_{\alpha}x^{\alpha}

with components
P_{\alpha}\in\mathbb{C}
. We denote
\alpha!:=\alpha_{1}!\ldots\alpha_{n}!

and
\delta^{\left(\alpha\right)}:=\delta_{0}^{\left(\alpha_{1}\right)}\left(x_{1}\right)\ldots\delta_{0}^{\left(\alpha_{n}\right)}\left(x_{n}\right)

the Dirac distribution on
E
(with
\alpha
-derivatives). We have
that for any
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
,
\langle\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}!}\delta_{0}^{\left(\alpha^{\prime}\right)}|x^{\alpha}\rangle=\delta_{\alpha^{\prime}=\alpha}
.
Hence the set
\left(x^{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha}
forms a basis of

\mathrm{Pol}\left(E\right)
and
\left(\langle\delta^{\left(\alpha\right)}|.\rangle\right)_{\alpha}

is the dual basis. See Section
9
for
comments about the equivalence
\mathrm{Pol}_{k}\left(E\right)\equiv\mathrm{Sym}\left(\left(E^{*}\right)^{\otimes k}\right)
.

Definition C.38
.

For
k\in\mathbb{N}
, let

T_{k}:\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\left(E\right)\rightarrow\mathrm{Pol}_{k}\left(E\right)\subset\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\left(E\right)

be the projector onto
\mathrm{Pol}_{k}\left(E\right)
with kernel

\oplus_{k^{\prime}\neq k}\mathrm{Pol}_{k}\left(E\right)
. We have

\forall k,k^{\prime},\quad T_{k^{\prime}}T_{k}=T_{k}\delta_{k^{\prime}=k},

and explicitly, with respect to a basis of
E
,

T_{k}=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k},\left|\alpha\right|=k}x^{\alpha}\langle\frac{1}{\alpha!}\delta_{0}^{\left(\alpha\right)}|.\rangle.

(C.53)

The next lemma shows how this relation is changed by a small norm
operator if we insert a truncation at large distance
\sigma
from
the origin. We put
\chi_{\sigma}\left(\rho\right)=1
for
\left|\rho\right|_{\boldsymbol{g}}\leq\sigma
,

\chi_{\sigma}\left(\rho\right)=0
for
\left|\rho\right|_{\boldsymbol{g}}>\sigma

and
\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\sigma}\right):=\mathcal{B}^{\dagger}\mathcal{M}_{\chi_{\sigma}}\mathcal{B}
.

Lemma C.39
.

For
k,k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
, we have
\forall N,\exists C_{N}>0,\forall\sigma>0
,

R_{\sigma}:=\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\sigma}\right)\left(T_{k^{\prime}}\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\sigma}\right)T_{k}-T_{k}\delta_{k^{\prime}=k}\right)\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\sigma}\right)

satisfies

\left|\langle\delta_{\rho^{\prime}}|\mathcal{B}^{\dagger}R_{\sigma}\mathcal{B}\delta_{\rho}\rangle\right|\leq\left\langle\mathrm{dist}_{\boldsymbol{g}/\sigma^{2}}\left(\rho^{\prime},\rho\right)\right\rangle^{-N}C_{N}\sigma^{-N}\left\langle\mathrm{dist}_{\boldsymbol{g}/\sigma^{2}}\left(\rho,0\right)\right\rangle^{-N},

Consequently
\left\|R_{\sigma}\right\|\leq C_{N}\sigma^{-N}

for any
N>0
, with
C_{N}>0
.

Démonstration
.

We have

\displaystyle\langle\delta_{\rho^{\prime}}|\mathcal{B}R\mathcal{B}^{\dagger}\delta_{\rho}\rangle

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:def_T_k})}{=}\chi_{\sigma}\left(\rho^{\prime}\right)\chi_{\sigma}\left(\rho\right)

(C.54)

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{k},\left|\alpha^{\prime}\right|=k^{\prime},\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k},\left|\alpha\right|=k,}\langle\varphi_{\rho^{\prime}}|x^{\alpha^{\prime}}\rangle\left(\langle\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}!}\delta_{0}^{\left(\alpha^{\prime}\right)}|\mathrm{Op}\left(\chi_{\sigma}\right)x^{\alpha}\rangle-\delta_{\alpha^{\prime}=\alpha}\right)\langle\frac{1}{\alpha!}\delta_{0}^{\left(\alpha\right)}|\varphi_{\rho}\rangle.

(C.55)

We have
\forall N,\exists C_{N},\forall\sigma>0
,

\left|\langle\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}!}\delta_{0}^{\left(\alpha^{\prime}\right)}|\mathcal{B}^{\dagger}\mathcal{M}_{\chi_{\sigma}}\mathcal{B}x^{\alpha}\rangle-\delta_{\alpha^{\prime}=\alpha}\right|\leq C_{N}\sigma^{-N},

and due to truncations
\chi_{\sigma}\left(\rho^{\prime}\right)\chi_{\sigma}\left(\rho\right)

in (
C.54
) we deduce that

\left|\langle\delta_{\rho^{\prime}}|\mathcal{B}^{\dagger}R\mathcal{B}\delta_{\rho}\rangle\right|\leq\left\langle\mathrm{dist}_{\boldsymbol{g}/\sigma^{2}}\left(\rho^{\prime},\rho\right)\right\rangle^{-N}C_{N}\sigma^{-N}\left\langle\mathrm{dist}_{\boldsymbol{g}/\sigma^{2}}\left(\rho,0\right)\right\rangle^{-N},

and
\left\|R_{\sigma}\right\|\leq C_{N}\sigma^{-N}
from
example (
2.37
).
∎

C.11
Analysis on
T\left(E\oplus E^{*}\right)

In this section we “lift” the analysis from
E\oplus E^{*}
to

T\left(E\oplus E^{*}\right)
. This might been seem a bit artificial
for vector spaces, but it will be useful in this paper as a linearized
model for manifolds.

C.11.1
Bargman transform

Let
\left(E,g\right)
be a linear Euclidean space. Let
F:=E\oplus E^{*}
with
induced metric
\boldsymbol{g}=g\oplus g^{-1}
. One has
TF=T\left(E\oplus E^{*}\right)=\left(E\oplus E^{*}\right)\oplus\left(E\oplus E^{*}\right)
.
The exponential map is

\exp:\begin{cases}TF&\rightarrow F\\
\left(\rho,\rho^{\prime}\right)&\rightarrow\rho+\rho^{\prime}\end{cases}

Let us define the “twisted pull back operator”
\widetilde{\exp^{\circ}}:\mathcal{S}\left(F\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(TF\right)

by its Schwartz kernel as follows. For
\left(\rho_{1},\rho_{1}^{\prime}\right)\in TF
,

\rho_{2}\in F
,
\rho_{1}=\left(x_{1},\xi_{1}\right)
,
\rho_{1}^{\prime}=\left(x_{1}^{\prime},\xi_{1}^{\prime}\right)
,

\langle\delta_{\rho_{1},\rho_{1}^{\prime}}|\widetilde{\exp^{\circ}}\delta_{\rho_{2}}\rangle=\delta_{\rho_{2}-\left(\rho_{1}+\rho_{1}^{\prime}\right)}e^{-i\xi_{1}x_{1}^{\prime}}

We define the “restriction” operator
r_{0}:\mathcal{S}\left(TF\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(F\right)

by its Schwartz kernel

\langle\delta_{\rho_{2}}|r_{0}\delta_{\rho_{1},\rho_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle=\delta_{\rho_{2}-\rho_{1}}\delta_{\rho_{1}^{\prime}}

Let the “horizontal space” be

H:=F\oplus E.

(C.56)

If
\mathcal{B}:\mathcal{S}\left(E\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(F\right)

is the Bargman transform defined in (
C.8
), we
define the partial Bargman transform

B:\mathcal{S}\left(H\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(TF\right)

by
B\left(u\left(\rho\right)\otimes v\left(x^{\prime}\right)\right)=u\left(\rho\right)\otimes\left(\mathcal{B}v\right)\left(x^{\prime}\right)
,
i.e. action on the second part only.

Let
\sigma>0
and let us introduce the cutoff function that truncates
at distance
\sigma
in the fiber
F
:

\chi_{\sigma}:\begin{cases}\mathcal{S}\left(TF\right)&\rightarrow\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\left(TF\right)\\
u\left(\rho,\rho^{\prime}\right)&\rightarrow\begin{cases}u\left(\rho,\rho^{\prime}\right)&\text{ if }\left\|\rho^{\prime}\right\|_{\boldsymbol{g}}\leq\sigma\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\end{cases}

Let

\boldsymbol{B}_{\chi}^{\Delta}:=B^{\dagger}\chi_{\sigma}\widetilde{\exp^{\circ}}\quad:\mathcal{S}\left(F\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(H\right)

\boldsymbol{B}:=r_{0}B\quad:\mathcal{S}\left(H\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(F\right)

C.11.2
Linear map

Let
A:E\rightarrow E
a linear invertible map and
\tilde{A}:=A^{-1}\oplus A^{*}

the induced map on
F:=E\oplus E^{*}
. Let
A^{\circ}:\mathcal{S}\left(E\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(E\right)

the pull back operator and
A_{H}:=\tilde{A}^{-1}\oplus A\quad:H\rightarrow H

the induced map on
H
in (
C.56
).

Lemma C.40
.

Let us consider the difference operator

R:=\boldsymbol{B}A_{H}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{B}_{\chi}^{\Delta}-\mathcal{B}A^{\circ}\mathcal{B}^{\dagger}\quad:\mathcal{S}\left(F\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(F\right).

Then
\forall N>0,\exists C_{N}>0
such that for any
\sigma>0
,

\rho,\rho^{\prime}
,

\left|\langle\delta_{\rho^{\prime}}|R\delta_{\rho}\rangle\right|\leq C_{N}\left\langle\left\|\rho-\tilde{A}^{-1}\rho^{\prime}\right\|\right\rangle^{-N}\sigma^{-N}.

Remark C.41
.

In particular, for
\sigma=\infty
, i.e. no cut-off, then
R=0
.
Another interesting particular case is
A=\mathrm{Id}
.

Démonstration
.

We compute the Schwartz kernel.

\displaystyle\langle\delta_{\rho^{\prime}}|\boldsymbol{B}A_{H}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{B}_{\chi}^{\Delta}\delta_{\rho}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\delta_{\rho^{\prime}}|r_{0}BA_{H}^{\circ}B^{\dagger}\chi_{\sigma}\widetilde{\exp^{\circ}}\delta_{\rho}\rangle

\displaystyle=\int\langle\delta_{\rho}|r_{0}\delta_{\rho_{1},\rho_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle\langle\delta_{\rho_{1},\rho_{1}^{\prime}}|BA_{H}^{\circ}B^{\dagger}\delta_{\rho_{2},\rho_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle\chi_{\sigma}\left(\rho_{2}^{\prime}\right)\langle\delta_{\rho_{2},\rho_{2}^{\prime}}|\widetilde{\exp^{\circ}}\delta_{\rho}\rangle

\displaystyle=\int\left(\delta_{\rho^{\prime}-\rho_{1}}\delta_{\rho_{1}^{\prime}}\right)\left(\delta_{\tilde{A}\rho_{2}-\rho_{1}}\langle\varphi_{\rho_{1}^{\prime}}|A^{\circ}\varphi_{\rho_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle\right)\chi_{\sigma}\left(\rho_{2}^{\prime}\right)\left(\delta_{\rho-\left(\rho_{2}+\rho_{2}^{\prime}\right)}e^{-i\xi_{2}x_{2}^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle=\langle\varphi_{0}|A^{\circ}\varphi_{\rho-\tilde{A}^{-1}\rho^{\prime}}\rangle e^{-i\left(\left(A^{*}\right)^{-1}\xi^{\prime}\right)\left(x-Ax^{\prime}\right)}\chi_{\sigma}\left(\rho-\tilde{A}^{-1}\rho^{\prime}\right)

because Dirac measures gave
\rho_{2}^{\prime}=\rho-\rho_{2}=\rho-\tilde{A}^{-1}\rho^{\prime}
,

\xi_{2}=\left(A^{*}\right)^{-1}\xi^{\prime}
and
x_{2}^{\prime}=x-Ax^{\prime}
. Then

\displaystyle\langle\delta_{\rho^{\prime}}|\boldsymbol{B}A_{H}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{B}_{\chi}^{\Delta}\delta_{\rho}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\varphi_{0}|A^{\circ}\varphi_{\rho-\tilde{A}^{-1}\rho^{\prime}}\rangle e^{i\xi^{\prime}\left(x^{\prime}-A^{-1}x^{\prime}\right)}\chi_{\sigma}\left(\rho-\tilde{A}^{-1}\rho^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:phi_T})}{=}\langle\varphi_{0}|A^{\circ}T_{x-Ax^{\prime}}^{-\circ}\left(\mathcal{F}T_{\xi-A^{*-1}\xi^{\prime}}^{-\circ}\mathcal{F}\right)\varphi_{0}\rangle e^{i\xi^{\prime}\left(x^{\prime}-A^{-1}x^{\prime}\right)}\chi_{\sigma}\left(\rho-\tilde{A}^{-1}\rho^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:weyl_heisenberg})}{=}\langle\varphi_{0}|A^{\circ}\left(\mathcal{F}T_{-A^{*-1}\xi^{\prime}}^{-\circ}\mathcal{F}\right)T_{-Ax^{\prime}}^{-\circ}T_{x}^{-\circ}\left(\mathcal{F}T_{\xi}^{-\circ}\mathcal{F}\right)\varphi_{0}\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad e^{-i\left(-A^{*-1}\xi^{\prime}\right)x}e^{i\xi^{\prime}\left(-A^{-1}x^{\prime}\right)}\chi_{\sigma}\left(\rho-\tilde{A}^{-1}\rho^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=\underset{(\ref{eq:weyl_heisenberg})}{=}\langle\varphi_{0}|\left(\mathcal{F}T_{-\xi^{\prime}}^{-\circ}\mathcal{F}\right)T_{-x^{\prime}}^{-\circ}A^{\circ}T_{x}^{-\circ}\left(\mathcal{F}T_{\xi}^{-\circ}\mathcal{F}\right)\varphi_{0}\rangle\chi_{\sigma}\left(\rho-\tilde{A}^{-1}\rho^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=\langle\varphi_{\rho^{\prime}}|A^{\circ}\varphi_{\rho}\rangle\chi_{\sigma}\left(\rho-\tilde{A}^{-1}\rho^{\prime}\right)=\langle\delta_{\rho^{\prime}}|\mathcal{B}A^{\circ}\mathcal{B}^{\dagger}\delta_{\rho}\rangle\chi_{\sigma}\left(\rho-\tilde{A}^{-1}\rho^{\prime}\right)

If
\left\|\rho-\tilde{A}^{-1}\rho^{\prime}\right\|\geq\sigma
then

\langle\delta_{\rho^{\prime}}|R\delta_{\rho}\rangle=0
otherwise

\forall N>0,\exists C_{N}>0,\quad\left|\langle\varphi_{0}|A^{\circ}\varphi_{\rho-\tilde{A}^{-1}\rho^{\prime}}\rangle\right|\leq C_{A}C_{N}\sigma^{-N}.

∎

C.11.3
Taylor projectors

For
\rho\in F
,
\hat{T}_{\rho}
has been defined in (
C.1
).
Let us define

T_{H}^{\left(k\right)}:=\hat{T}_{-\rho}T_{k}\hat{T}_{\rho}\quad:\mathcal{S}\left(H\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(H\right)

where
\rho\in F
denotes the first variable and the operators acts
on the second variable
x^{\prime}\in E
only.

Lemma C.42
.

Let us consider the difference operator

R:=\boldsymbol{B}T_{H}^{\left(k\right)}\boldsymbol{B}_{\chi}^{\Delta}-\mathcal{B}T_{k}\mathcal{B}^{*}\quad:\mathcal{S}\left(F\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(F\right).

Then for
\left\|\rho-\rho^{\prime}\right\|\leq\sigma
we have
\langle\delta_{\rho^{\prime}}|R\delta_{\rho}\rangle=0
.

Remark C.43
.

In particular for
\sigma=\infty
, i.e. no cut-off, then
R=0
.

Démonstration
.

We repeat the lines of proof of Lemma
C.40
.

\displaystyle\langle\delta_{\rho^{\prime}}|\boldsymbol{B}T_{H}^{\left(k\right)}\boldsymbol{B}_{\chi}^{\Delta}\delta_{\rho}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\delta_{\rho^{\prime}}|r_{0}BT_{H}^{\left(k\right)}B^{*}\chi_{\sigma}\widetilde{\exp^{\circ}}\delta_{\rho}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\varphi_{0}|\hat{T}_{-\rho^{\prime}}T_{k}\hat{T}_{\rho^{\prime}}\varphi_{\rho-\rho^{\prime}}\rangle e^{i\xi^{\prime}\left(x^{\prime}-x\right)}\chi_{\sigma}\left(\rho-\rho^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle\underset{(\ref{eq:inverse_T})}{=}\langle\varphi_{0}|\hat{T}_{\rho^{\prime}}^{\dagger}e^{-i\xi^{\prime}x^{\prime}}T_{k}\hat{T}_{\rho^{\prime}}\varphi_{\rho-\rho^{\prime}}\rangle e^{i\xi^{\prime}\left(x^{\prime}-x\right)}\chi_{\sigma}\left(\rho-\rho^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=\langle\varphi_{\rho^{\prime}}|T_{k}\varphi_{\rho}\rangle\chi_{\sigma}\left(\rho-\rho^{\prime}\right)=\langle\delta_{\rho^{\prime}}|\mathcal{B}T_{k}\mathcal{B}^{\dagger}\delta_{\rho}\rangle\chi_{\sigma}\left(\rho-\rho^{\prime}\right)

∎

Annexe
D
Linear contracting maps

Let
\left(E,g\right)
a finite dimensional vector space with Euclidean
metric
g
. In this section we consider a
linear
invertible
and
contracting map

\phi:\left(E,g\right)\rightarrow\left(E,g\right)

i.e.

\lambda_{\pm}:=\lim_{t\rightarrow\pm\infty}\log\left\|\phi^{t}\right\|^{1/t}

(D.1)

satisfy
16
16

16

Equivalently
\lambda_{-}\leq\lambda_{+}<0
are the log of the minimal/maximal
modulus of eigenvalues of
\phi
given by its Jordan decomposition.

\lambda_{-}\leq\lambda_{+}<0
. Recall that
\mathrm{Op}\left(\Phi\right):\underset{(\ref{eq:def_Op})}{=}\left(\Upsilon\left(\Phi\right)\right)^{1/2}\mathcal{B}^{\dagger}\Phi^{-\circ}\mathcal{B}

with
\Phi:=\phi^{-1}\oplus\phi^{*}:E\oplus E^{*}\rightarrow E\oplus E^{*}

being the induced map on cotangent space and
\mathcal{B}:\mathcal{S}\left(E\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(E\oplus E^{*}\right)

defined in (
C.8
). The pull back operator
\phi^{\circ}:\mathcal{S}\left(E\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(E\right)

has been defined in (
A.4
). The purpose of this
section is to study the spectrum of the following operator

\mathrm{Op}\left(\Phi\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:Op(f)})}{=}\left|\mathrm{det}\phi\right|^{1/2}\phi^{\circ}\qquad:\mathcal{S}\left(E\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{S}\left(E\right)

(D.2)

on an adequate Hilbert space that contains
\mathcal{S}\left(E\right)

(notice that
\mathrm{Op}\left(\Phi\right)
is unitary in
L^{2}\left(E\right)

and has essential spectrum on the unit circle).

For
k\in\mathbb{N}
, consider
T_{k}
the finite rank projector
defined in (
C.53
). The vector space
\mathrm{Im}\left(T_{k}\right)\subset\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\left(E\right)

is finite dimensional. Since
\phi
is a linear map, we have for
any
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

\left[\phi^{\circ},T_{k}\right]=0,

(D.3)

hence
\mathrm{Op}\left(\Phi\right):\mathrm{Im}\left(T_{k}\right)\rightarrow\mathrm{Im}\left(T_{k}\right)

is invariant and has finite rank (we can compute explicitly its spectrum
from the spectrum of
\phi
). Let

\gamma_{k}^{\pm}:=\lim_{t\rightarrow\pm\infty}\log\left\|\mathrm{Op}\left(\Phi^{t}\right)_{/\mathrm{Im}\left(T_{k}\right)}\right\|^{1/t}.

(D.4)

We can
17
17

17

In the simple
1-\mathrm{dim}
case
\phi\left(x\right)=e^{\lambda}x

on
\mathbb{R}
, with
\lambda_{\pm}=\lambda<0
, we get
\gamma_{k}^{+}=\gamma_{k}^{-}=\left(\frac{1}{2}+k\right)\lambda
.
compute
\gamma_{k}^{\pm}
from the eigenvalues of
\phi
, see
Remark
[
18
, rem. 3.4.7]
. As in
(
1.7
), we have

\left(\frac{d}{2}+k\right)\lambda_{-}\leq\gamma_{k}^{-}\leq\gamma_{k}^{+}\leq\left(\frac{d}{2}+k\right)\lambda_{+},

(D.5)

With
d=\mathrm{dim}E
. For every
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we have
\gamma_{k}^{-}\leq\gamma_{k}^{+}
,

\gamma_{k+1}^{\pm}\leq\gamma_{k}^{\pm}
. The spectrum of
\mathrm{Op}\left(\Phi^{t}\right):\mathrm{Im}\left(T_{k}\right)\rightarrow\mathrm{Im}\left(T_{k}\right)

is discrete and contained in the annulus
\left\{z\in\mathbb{C},\,e^{t\gamma_{k}^{-}}\leq\left|z\right|\leq e^{t\gamma_{k}^{+}}\right\}
.
However we want to understand the action of
\mathrm{Op}\left(\Phi\right)

on every function in
\mathcal{S}\left(E\right)
. For
K\in\mathbb{N}
,
let

T_{\geq\left(K+1\right)}:=\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{S}\left(E\right)}-\left(\sum_{k=0}^{K}T_{k}\right).

We will show below in Proposition
D.3
that
\left\|\mathrm{Op}\left(\Phi^{t}\right)T_{\geq\left(K+1\right)}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(E\right)}\leq C_{\epsilon}e^{\left(\gamma_{K+1}^{+}+\epsilon\right)t}

for some adequate norm
\left\|.\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(E\right)}

that we first define.

D.1
Anisotropic Sobolev space
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(E\right)

Let
0<\gamma<1
,
R\in\mathbb{R}
. We define a weight function

\mathcal{W}:E\oplus E^{*}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
similar to (
4.49
)
as follows. For
\rho=\left(x,\xi\right)\in E\oplus E^{*}
, let

h_{\gamma}\left(\rho\right)=\left\langle\left\|\rho\right\|_{g\oplus g^{-1}}\right\rangle^{-\gamma}

and

\mathcal{W}\left(\rho\right):=\frac{\left\langle h_{\gamma}\left(\rho\right)\left\|\xi\right\|_{g^{-1}}\right\rangle^{R}}{\left\langle h_{\gamma}\left(\rho\right)\left\|x\right\|_{g}\right\rangle^{R}}

(D.6)

Definition D.1
.

For
u\in\mathcal{S}\left(E\right)
, we define the norm

\left\|u\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(E\right)}:=\left\|\mathcal{W}\mathcal{B}u\right\|_{L^{2}\left(E\oplus E^{*}\right)},

(D.7)

(where
\mathcal{W}
denotes multiplication operator by
\mathcal{W}
)
and the
Sobolev space

\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(E\right):=\overline{\left\{u\in\mathcal{S}\left(E\right)\right\}}

(D.8)

where the completion is with the norm
\left\|u\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(E\right)}
.

Remark D.2
.

•

For
R=0
we have
\mathcal{W}\equiv 1
hence
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(E\right)=L^{2}\left(E\right)
.

•

We have that
\mathcal{W}\left(0,\xi\right)\leq\left\langle\left|\xi\right|\right\rangle^{r}

and
\mathcal{W}\left(x,0\right)\leq\left\langle\left|x\right|\right\rangle^{-r}

with the order
r=R\left(1-\gamma\right)
as in (
4.20
)
(except for the factor
\frac{1}{2}
that came from the metric).

For a
non linear
map (Axiom A diffeomorphisms), it has been
explained in
[
20
, section 4]

that it is necessary to use a metric on phase space as (
2.25
)
with
\delta^{\perp}\left(\eta\right)=\left\langle\left|\eta\right|\right\rangle^{-\alpha^{\perp}}

and exponent
\alpha^{\perp}\geq 1/2
to perform the micro-local analysis
and get Ruelle spectrum. In this section, we consider the special
case of a
linear
map
\phi:E\rightarrow E
is linear and for
that reason we can use the constant Euclidean metric
g\oplus g^{-1}

on
T^{*}E=E\oplus E^{*}
(i.e. exponent
\alpha^{\perp}=0
) to
get the Ruelle spectrum of the operator
\mathrm{Op}\left(\Phi\right)

in (
D.2
).

D.2
Result

The result that we will show is

Proposition D.3
.

For any
\epsilon>0,

K\in\mathbb{N}
,
R

in (
D.6
) large enough so that

\lambda_{+}R\left(1-\gamma\right)<\gamma_{K+1}^{+},

\exists C_{\epsilon}>0,\forall t\geq 0
,

\left\|\mathrm{Op}\left(\Phi^{t}\right)T_{\geq\left(K+1\right)}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(E\right)}\leq C_{\epsilon}e^{\left(\gamma_{K+1}^{+}+\epsilon\right)t},

(D.9)

with
\gamma_{K+1}^{+}
defined in (
D.4
).

D.2.1
Proof of Proposition
D.3

We have
\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi\right)\underset{(\ref{eq:def_op_tilde})}{=}\left(\Upsilon\left(\Phi\right)\right)^{1/2}\mathcal{P}\Phi^{-\circ}\mathcal{P}

and let

\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(\Phi\right):=\mathcal{W}\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi\right)\mathcal{W}^{-1}.

From (
C.38
) and (
D.7
) we have the
commutative diagram (with the notation of weighted norm
\left\|u\right\|_{L^{2}\left(E\oplus E^{*};\mathcal{W}^{2}\right)}:=\int\mathcal{W}^{2}\left(x,\xi\right)u\left(x,\xi\right)\frac{dxd\xi}{\left(2\pi\right)^{\mathrm{dim}E}}
)

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(E\right)&\overset{\mathcal{B}}{\longrightarrow}&L^{2}\left(E\oplus E^{*};\mathcal{W}^{2}\right)&\overset{\mathcal{W}}{\longrightarrow}&L^{2}\left(E\oplus E^{*}\right)\\
\downarrow\mathrm{Op}\left(\Phi\right)&&\downarrow\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}\left(\Phi\right)&&\downarrow\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(\Phi\right)\\
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(E\right)&\overset{\mathcal{B}}{\longrightarrow}&L^{2}\left(E\oplus E^{*};\mathcal{W}^{2}\right)&\overset{\mathcal{W}}{\longrightarrow}&L^{2}\left(E\oplus E^{*}\right)\end{array}

(D.10)

where horizontal arrows are isometries by definition. Hence, the study
of
\mathrm{Op}\left(\Phi^{t}\right):\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(E\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(E\right)

is equivalent to study
\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(\Phi^{t}\right):L^{2}\left(E\oplus E^{*}\right)\rightarrow L^{2}\left(E\oplus E^{*}\right)
.

Lemma D.4
.

“
Decay property
of
\mathcal{W}

with respect to
\Phi
”. There exists
C>0
, such that for any

t\geq 0
, there exists
C_{t}
such that for any
\rho\in E\oplus E^{*}

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{W}\left(\Phi^{t}\left(\rho\right)\right)}{\mathcal{W}\left(\rho\right)}

\displaystyle\leq C

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-\Lambda t}\text{ if }\left\|\rho\right\|_{g}\geq C_{t}

(D.11)

with

\Lambda=-\lambda_{+}R\left(1-\gamma\right)>0.

(D.12)

Démonstration
.

Similar to the proof in
[
20
, Thm 5.9]
.
Write
\rho=\left(x,\xi\right)\in T^{*}E=E\oplus E^{*}
. We consider
different zones of
T^{*}E
:

1.

If
\left|x\right|,\left|\xi\right|\leq 1
then
W\left(\rho\right)\asymp 1
.

2.

If
\left|x\right|>1
and
\left|\xi\right|\leq\left|x\right|^{\gamma}

then
W\left(\rho\right)\asymp\left|x\right|^{-R\left(1-\gamma\right)}
.

3.

If
\left|\xi\right|>1
and
\left|x\right|\leq\left|\xi\right|^{\gamma}

then
W\left(\rho\right)\asymp\left|\xi\right|^{R\left(1-\gamma\right)}
.

4.

Otherwise
W\left(\rho\right)\asymp\left(\frac{\left|\xi\right|}{\left|x\right|}\right)^{R}
.

We observe that for
t\geq 0
,
\left|\phi^{-t}\left(x\right)\right|\geq e^{-\lambda_{+}t}\left|x\right|

and
\left|\phi^{*t}\left(\xi\right)\right|\leq e^{\lambda_{+}t}\left|\xi\right|

from which we deduce Lemma
D.4
.
∎

Truncation in phase space near the trapped set

Let
\chi\in C_{c}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{+};[0,1]\right)
such
that

\displaystyle\chi\left(x\right)

\displaystyle=1\text{ if }x\leq 1,

\displaystyle\chi\left(x\right)

\displaystyle=0\text{ if }x\geq 2,

Let
\sigma>0
. For
\rho\in E\oplus E^{*}
let

\chi_{\sigma}\left(\rho\right):=\chi\left(\frac{\left\|\rho\right\|_{g}}{\sigma}\right).

Lemma D.5
.

We have
\forall R>0,
\forall\epsilon>0
,
\exists C>0,\forall t\geq 0,

\exists\sigma_{t}>0
,
\forall\sigma>\sigma_{t}
,

\left\|\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(\Phi^{t}\right)\left(1-\chi_{\sigma}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq Ce^{\left(-\Lambda+\epsilon\right)t}.

(D.13)

where
\Lambda
is given in (
D.12
). The operator
\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(\Phi^{t}\right)\chi_{\sigma}

is Trace class in
L^{2}
. Consequently for any
t>0
, the
essential spectral radius

is

r_{\mathrm{ess.}}\left(\tilde{\mathrm{Op}}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(\Phi^{t}\right)_{L^{2}\rightarrow L^{2}}\right)\leq e^{-\Lambda t}.

(D.14)

Démonstration
.

For (
D.13
), see
[
20
, thm 5.13]
.
∎

Lemma D.6
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
. If
k+1<r
in (
4.20
),
then
T_{k}:\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(E\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(E\right)

is a bounded operator.

Démonstration
.

We check that in (
C.53
),
\left\|x^{\alpha}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(E\right)}<\infty

and
\left\|\delta_{0}^{\left(\alpha\right)}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(E\right)^{*}}<\infty
.
∎

Lemma D.7
.

Let
K\in\mathbb{N}
and
R>0
large enough
such that
-\Lambda<\gamma_{K+1}^{+}
in (
D.12
). For

t>0
, the
spectral radius

is

r_{\mathrm{spec}}\left(\left(\mathrm{Op}\left(\Phi^{t}\right)T_{\geq\left(K+1\right)}\right)_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(E\right)}\right)=e^{t\gamma_{K+1}^{+}}

Remark D.8
.

Lemma
D.7
is similar to
[
18
, Claim (2) in Prop. 3.4.6]
.

Démonstration
.

First, from (
D.14
) and because
\mathrm{Op}\left(\Phi^{t}\right)T_{k}

is finite rank, we have that

r_{\mathrm{ess}}\left(\left(\mathrm{Op}\left(\Phi^{t}\right)T_{\geq\left(K+1\right)}\right)_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(E\right)}\right)\leq e^{-\Lambda t},

i.e.
\mathrm{Op}\left(\Phi^{t}\right)T_{\geq\left(K+1\right)}:\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(E\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(E\right)

has discrete spectrum outside the disk of radius
e^{-\Lambda t}
.
From
Taylor-Lagrange remainder formula
,
for any
u,v\in\mathcal{S}\left(E\right)
, we have

\left|\langle v|\mathrm{Op}\left(\Phi^{t}\right)T_{\geq\left(K+1\right)}u\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|\leq Ce^{t\gamma_{K+1}^{+}}\left\|u\right\|_{C^{K+1}}\left\|x^{K+1}v\right\|_{L^{1}}.

(D.15)

i.e. correlation functions decay faster than
e^{t\gamma_{K+1}^{+}}
.
We deduce that
\mathrm{Op}\left(\Phi^{t}\right)T_{\geq\left(K+1\right)}

has no spectrum on
\left|z\right|>e^{t\gamma_{K+1}^{+}}
.
∎

Finally Lemma
D.7
is equivalent to Proposition
D.3
.

D.3
Discrete Ruelle spectrum
in a simple toy model

We give here a very simple example that illustrates Section
D

and some mechanisms that play a role in this paper. (See the lecture
notes
[
14
]
for further details). On
\mathbb{R}_{x}

let us consider the vector field

X=-x\frac{\partial}{\partial x}

(D.16)

that gives a flow
\phi^{t}\left(x\right)=e^{-t}x
that is contracting
for
t>0
, hence we think
E_{s}=\mathbb{R}_{x}
as a stable direction.
We observe that for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, the monomial
x^{k}
is
an eigenfunction of
X
(hence of
e^{tX}
) with eigenvalue
\left(-k\right)
:

Xx^{k}=\left(-k\right)x^{k}

and the spectral projector is

T_{k}=x^{k}\langle\frac{1}{k!}\delta^{\left(k\right)}|.\rangle,

where
\delta^{\left(k\right)}
is the
k
-th derivative of the
Dirac distribution (notice indeed from
\langle\frac{1}{k!}\delta^{\left(k\right)}|x^{k^{\prime}}\rangle=\delta_{k=k^{\prime}}

that
\left(\delta^{\left(k\right)}\right)_{k}
forms a dual basis
to
\left(x^{k^{\prime}}\right)_{k^{\prime}}
).

However
x^{k},\delta^{\left(k\right)}
do not belong to
L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}\right)
.
In the Hilbert space
L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}\right)
one has
X^{\dagger}=-X+1\Leftrightarrow\left(X-\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\dagger}=-\left(X-\frac{1}{2}\right)

that implies that the spectrum of
X
is on the vertical axis
\frac{1}{2}+i\mathbb{R}

with some essential spectrum. Some better Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(\mathbb{R}\right)

is constructed as in (
D.8
) in order to study the pull
back operator
\left(e^{tX}u\right)\left(x\right)=u\left(\phi^{t}\left(x\right)\right)=u\left(e^{-t}x\right)

on functions
u\in\mathcal{S}\left(\mathbb{R}\right)
. We consider
the induced (pull-back) flow on the cotangent space
T^{*}E_{s}=T^{*}\mathbb{R}

given by

\tilde{\phi}^{t}\left(x,\xi\right)=\left(e^{t}x,e^{-t}\xi\right).

For
0<h\ll 1
,
R\geq 0
we define the “escape function” or “Lyapounov
function” on
T^{*}\mathbb{R}
for
\tilde{\phi}^{t}
that is

\mathcal{W}\left(x,\xi\right)=\frac{\left\langle\sqrt{h}\xi\right\rangle^{R}}{\left\langle\sqrt{h}x\right\rangle^{R}}.

Indeed, it satisfies
\frac{\mathcal{W}\circ\tilde{\phi}^{t}}{\mathcal{W}}\leq C

everywhere and
\frac{\mathcal{W}\circ\tilde{\phi}^{t}}{\mathcal{W}}\leq e^{-Rt}

far from the “trapped set” or “non wandering set”
\left(0,0\right)
.
In the Hilbert space

\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(\mathbb{R}\right):=\mathrm{Op}\left(\mathcal{W}^{-1}\right)L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}\right),

for
-R+\frac{1}{2}<-k
, we have that
x^{k}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(\mathbb{R}\right)

and
\left\|T_{k}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}}\leq C

is a bounded operator. See figure
D.1
.For
large time
t\gg 1
, the emerging behavior of
e^{tX}u
is given
by

\displaystyle e^{tX}u

\displaystyle=\Pi_{0}u+O_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}}\left(e^{-t}\right)

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{1}\,u\left(0\right)+O_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}}\left(e^{-t}\right)

i.e. projection onto the constant function.

Figure
D.1:
In blue, discrete spectrum of the operator
(
D.16
),
X=-x\frac{\partial}{\partial x}
in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(\mathbb{R}\right)
.
In brown, the essential spectrum moves far away if
R\rightarrow+\infty
.
